- **The one place a real-time check would be implementable: reconcile capture, comparing a new capture's incoming content against an already-captured prior slot at the same offset, live, aborting on the first divergence.** This would catch anomalies that *differ* between attempts (the packet-transposition and repeating-cycle shapes seen in test_3/test_4) considerably earlier than waiting for the footer. It would **not** help with the dominant, shared [64,512) gap specifically, since every capture examined so far shows that exact same hole — a prior capture would "agree" with a new one skipping it too, giving no divergence to detect. Not implemented — flagged as a real but limited-value option pending a decision on whether it's worth the complexity given that limitation.

### Seventh hardware test (`Rom Stitching Test/test_7`, 2026-07-30) — real completion-rate data gathered; a second, separate fd-unstable propagation bug found and fixed

- **Real measured completion rate this session: 1 success in 154 total dump-start attempts (~0.65%)** — far worse than the historical 5-20% "clean marker" range quoted above (that range measures a weaker bar — marker match, not full completion). At this rate, ~460 attempts are needed for 95% cumulative success within one "Dump ROM" press; at the better historical ~14-20% rate, only ~14-20 are needed. This wide range (14 to 460) is why no single fixed retry-count budget is right across sessions — raising the cap to a number that helps a bad day either does very little (too low) or makes a good day needlessly slow (too high). Flagged for a decision on whether to move to a wall-clock-time-bounded automatic retry instead of a fixed attempt count.
- **A second, real, embarrassing bug found in the previous session's own fix**: `gbop_reopen_wait_fresh()` (`gb_operator.c`) correctly tracks `s_fd_known_unstable` and skips its own retry loop once set — but `dump_rom_with_retry()`'s separate inline 30-try loop in `main.c` only *checked* the flag, it never *set* it. Since nothing else in this session's flow ever called `gbop_reopen_wait_fresh()` itself, the flag never got set at all, and the inline loop kept re-discovering "this connection never cycles the fd" from scratch on every single exhaustion — confirmed directly: 9 separate ~2.5s bursts this session (~22s total), none of them shrinking after the first. **Fix**: added `gbop_mark_fd_unstable()` (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`), called by `dump_rom_with_retry()`'s own loop when it exhausts, so its observations also feed the shared flag.
- **Not yet hardware-verified** (both the bug fix and the earlier retry-budget analysis this entry restates for continuity).

### Continuation-dump experiment (2026-07-30) — NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED

User's hypothesis, refined after the front-check discussion: instead of aborting the instant a bad front is detected (tearing the connection down, closing, reopening, resending a fresh command), let the bad stream run to its own natural completion (footer), then send a fresh command on the **same still-open handle** — no close, no reopen — and see if that next attempt is more likely to start clean. Framed with an analogy: if a stream reads `b c d e f | a b c d e f`, and you start reading at `b`, you can keep going, because `a` comes right after `f` finishes.

**Checked against existing data before building anything**: the literal "stream wraps around within one continuous transfer and delivers the missing front chunk after the end" isn't supported — every front-shifted capture examined so far (test_1/3/4/5) ends with a normal footer at exactly (requested size − missing amount), not a longer transfer that loops back around. But a different, genuinely untested version of the idea survives that check: does the device's *internal state* start more cleanly right after a stream just finished on its own, compared to after the connection was torn down and reopened? No existing data answers this, since every attempt so far has always closed and reopened between tries.

**Built the same way ROM Reconcile was built, per direct request** — a standalone, deliberately lean mechanism, not bolted onto the production `gbop_dump_rom_new()` path, so its own results aren't confounded by unrelated overhead (the same reasoning that made Reconcile's captures feel fast: no `verify_rom_size_before_dump()`, no `rom_cache_exists()`, just the core operation).

- **`gbop_dump_rom_continuation()`** (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`, new): sends the ROM-read command, checks the marker (strict — a marker failure is the *ordinary* handshake-failure case `dump_rom_with_retry()` already handles, not what this experiment targets, so it aborts immediately rather than retrying that case here), then checks the front exactly like `gbop_dump_rom_new()` does — **except** it tolerates up to 2 leading ZLPs after the marker before judging the first packet, fixing the exact false-negative found in test_5 (the ordinary front-check tripping on a ZLP that lands right after the marker drain on nearly every attempt) that would otherwise make almost every cycle here look like a bad front regardless of the real content. On a bad front: drains the rest of that stream to its own footer (discarding into a reusable scratch buffer, reusing the existing `newproto_stream_body()` primitive), then loops — sending a fresh command on the *same* handle, up to `max_cycles` times. On a good front: completes the full capture (same `newproto_stream_body`/`newproto_check_early_footer`/`newproto_confirm_footer_immediate` calls the production path uses) and returns success, reporting how many cycles were needed.
- **New dev-menu item "Continuation Test"** (`DEV_MENU_ITEMS` 13→14, `source/main.c`): a single-action screen (not a persistent multi-slot screen like Reconcile) — checks a cart is detected and GBA, closes/reopens once, allocates a `rom_size_kb*1024`-byte buffer (same scale as Reconcile's own align/merge buffers, already established as safe), runs the continuation experiment with `GBOP_CONTINUATION_MAX_CYCLES` (20), and on success saves directly to the normal cache path (`build_rom_path_sd()`) so a successful run produces an immediately-usable ROM, not just a diagnostic result.
- **What this will tell us**: compare the average number of cycles-per-success here against the ~154-attempts-per-success baseline measured this same session for the normal close-reopen-retry path. If continuation cycles succeed at a meaningfully higher rate per cycle, that's real evidence worth acting on (e.g. adopting this as the default dump strategy). If it's about the same or worse, the ~40s-per-bad-cycle cost (draining a full bad stream) makes it a net loss compared to the ordinary ~0.5-1s-per-failed-attempt reopen approach, and the next step (per the user's own fallback plan) is optimizing the existing checks to fail as fast as possible instead.
- **Not yet hardware-tested.**

### Eighth hardware test (`Rom Stitching Test/test_8`, 2026-07-31) — Continuation Test's real cost measured precisely; automatic outer retry added

**Continuation Test results — precisely measured across 5 real successful dumps (log.txt + log1.txt, 4 different GBA carts: Emerald, FireRed US, Ruby, FireRed JP):**

| Game | Manual presses needed | Cycles on the winning press |
|---|---|---|
| Emerald (log.txt session) | 9 | 2 (drain+retry-on-same-handle actually fired and worked) |
| Emerald (log1) | 12 | 1 (first-try clean) |
| FireRed US (BPRE) | 16 | 1 (first-try clean) |
| Ruby (AXVE) | 63 | 1 (first-try clean) |
| FireRed JP (BPRJ) | 2 | 1 (first-try clean) |

- **4 of 5 successes (80%) were first-time-go's** — the moment the marker matched, the front check also passed immediately, no drain-and-retry needed. The drain-and-retry mechanism inside `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` only had to actually do something once across all 5 successes, and it worked when it did (2 cycles, Emerald).
- **Directly measured the real system-side cost of a marker-only failure (the overwhelming majority of outcomes): 5-12ms**, from the `[dev] Continuation Test` press to the `FAILED` report. Every multi-second gap visible between attempts in the log is 100% human reaction time (re-pressing the A button) — not any real system or USB cost. This directly confirmed the user's own observation ("attempts were instant") with hard numbers.
- **Fix: automatic outer retry loop added**, replacing the manual "press A again" cycle. `GBOP_CONTINUATION_OUTER_ATTEMPTS` (200) wraps `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` with a close+reopen+retry loop, `GBOP_CONTINUATION_OUTER_PACING_US` (100ms, matching the existing codebase-wide retry pacing convention) between attempts, X+Y hold to abort (same pattern `rom_cache_stream_chunks()` already uses). Given the measured ~5-12ms real cost per marker-only failure, 200 automatic attempts at 100ms pacing costs roughly ~20s worst case for the cheap-failure case alone — dramatically cheaper than the up to several minutes of manual button-mashing Ruby's 63-press session actually took, while covering roughly 3x the worst count actually observed this session (63) with headroom to spare.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### Ninth hardware test (`Rom Stitching Test/test_9`, 2026-07-31) — continuation confirmed across 4 different GBA carts; promoted from experiment to the default new-firmware dump protocol; GB/GBC support added; detect-cart budgets raised

Four successful automatic-retry dumps this session, none hitting the 200-attempt cap: Emerald (outer attempt 7, 1 cycle), a mislabeled capture saved under Emerald's filename while actually FireRed content (outer attempt 46, 1 cycle — a real cart's own GBA logo is identical regardless of game, so the front-check and capture were both genuinely correct; only the *filename* was wrong, because the cart had been detected as Emerald moments earlier and the size happened to match), FireRed US (outer attempt 25, 2 cycles), FireRed JP (outer attempt 23, 1 cycle). User's own assessment: the method is consistent enough to become the standard dump protocol, with three follow-on requests — implemented all three:

**1. Continuation is now the default new-firmware dump path, old-firmware path untouched.** `dump_rom_new_protocol()` (`main.c`, new) extracts the automatic-outer-retry logic previously inline in the Continuation Test screen into a reusable function — buffer-based (touches SD exactly once, on success, not per attempt), no `verify_rom_size_before_dump()`-per-attempt or `rom_cache_exists()`-per-attempt overhead, X+Y abort. `dump_rom_best()` dispatches: `g_settings.use_old_firmware` → the original `dump_rom_with_retry()` (completely unmodified, still the exact mechanism that's always worked for old firmware); otherwise → `dump_rom_new_protocol()`. All 5 former `dump_rom_with_retry()` call sites (dev-menu Dump ROM, play_game auto-install, Isolated Test, Dump Only, and one more Dump-ROM-shaped path) now call `dump_rom_best()` instead — a single mechanical swap, same signature. The Continuation Test dev-menu screen itself is now a thin wrapper calling the same `dump_rom_new_protocol()` "Dump ROM" uses, not a separate duplicated implementation — kept as a dev-menu item for isolated manual testing now that it's graduated from experiment to default.

**2. GB/GBC support added to the continuation mechanism.** `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` (`gb_operator.c`) was GBA-only before this — the front-check used a single 64-byte packet, which works for GBA (logo at offset 0x04, inside packet 0) but not GB/GBC (logo at offset 0x104/260, entirely outside packet 0). Fixed by generalizing the probe: GBA uses a 64-byte probe (unchanged), GB/GBC uses a 320-byte probe (5 packets, comfortably covering through offset 0x134). Along the way, replaced the front-check's original hand-rolled "read one raw packet, retry up to 3 times past a leading ZLP" logic with a single call to `newproto_stream_body()` — the same primitive the main capture body already uses, whose existing ZLP/stall tolerance (`NEWPROTO_MAX_STALL`) is more thorough than the fixed 3-try hack, and which naturally supports an arbitrary probe size instead of being hardcoded to one packet. This is a genuine correctness/simplicity improvement independent of the GB/GBC addition, not just enabling infrastructure for it.
- **Not yet hardware-tested for GB/GBC specifically** — no GB/GBC cart has been run through this path yet. GBA behavior should be unchanged (same 64-byte probe, same logo table, just reached via a different code path internally).

**3. Detect Cart budgets raised, with an abort mechanism added.** `GBOP_DETECT_CART_ATTEMPTS` 20→60, `GBOP_HDR_READ_ATTEMPTS` 15→45 (`main.c`) — informed by the same per-attempt cost data gathered from Continuation Test's own retry loop this session (see the previous entry: ~76% of attempts hit an inherent ~800ms IOS/USB-stack latency spike *even with zero per-attempt SD I/O*, confirming the earlier SD-I/O theory was incomplete/wrong as a full explanation — this is connection-level latency variance, not something removable, which argues for a bigger budget rather than trying to eliminate the cost). Not raised all the way to Continuation Test's own 200, since `run_detect_cart_inner()` is also used for passive/automatic detection (boot, background) where a cart being genuinely absent — an ordinary, frequent state — must still resolve in a reasonable time. Added `xy_abort_held()`, a shared X+Y-hold check (same convention `rom_cache_stream_chunks()`/`dump_rom_new_protocol()` already use), wired into both `run_detect_cart_inner()`'s and `read_rom_header_with_retry()`'s retry loops, so a user who doesn't want to wait out the larger budget always has the same way out regardless of which operation is running.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### Tenth hardware test (`Rom Stitching Test/test_10`, 2026-07-31) — new dump protocol confirmed on GBC too (GB/GBC front-check working, 48/48 logo bytes matched); real root cause of Detect Cart's slowness isolated: not cart-info, the ROM-header peek

GBA dump (Emerald) and GBC dump (Pokemon Silver, 2MB) both succeeded via the new protocol. The GBC one is the first hardware confirmation of the GB/GBC front-check added last session: `"front check PASSED (48 logo bytes matched)"` — the full GB_LOGO length, first try. GBA behavior unchanged (60/60 as before).

User pushed back directly on the previous session's "raise the budget" fix for Detect Cart, correctly — raising the attempt count treats the symptom, not the actual bottleneck. Traced this session's own Detect Cart press precisely:
- **cart-info phase: 286ms (2 attempts)** — fast, not the problem.
- **ROM-header-read phase: 31,408ms — 31.4 seconds (35 of 45 attempts needed)** — over 100x slower than cart-info, and this is what the user actually experiences as "Detect Cart is slow."

Root cause: `gbop_read_rom_header_new()` (`gb_operator.c`) — the function `read_rom_header_with_retry()` calls on every detect to get the real game code/title (cart-info's own response doesn't reliably carry this on new firmware) — **never received the 2026-07-28 "streaming throughput fix"** that sped up the full ROM dump path. It was still reading its 512 bytes one 64-byte packet at a time via individual `USB_ReadBlkMsg()` calls (up to 8 per attempt) instead of the batched `newproto_stream_body()` primitive `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` already uses. Every one of this function's calls — which fire constantly during cart detection — was paying for up to 8 individual IOS round trips where the dump path now pays for as few as one.

- **Fix**: `gbop_read_rom_header_new()`'s read loop now goes through `newproto_stream_body()`, matching the dump path exactly (added a forward declaration since that function is defined later in the file). Existing `m==1` recovery + `header_checksum_valid()` validation logic is unchanged — this was a read-loop mechanism swap, not a validation-logic rewrite, since that logic already has a hard-won bug-fix history (test_10-17, Post Firmware Update Test) that shouldn't be casually touched. As a side effect, this is also strictly *safer* than the old loop in one respect: `newproto_stream_body()` recognizes footer/marker-shaped packets and handles them specially, where the old raw-read loop would have blindly copied a footer's `C0 DE` bytes into the header buffer as if they were real data had one ever arrived mid-peek (never observed, but a real gap the old code had no guard against).
- **"Learning more about the info command bytes"** — the user's other suggested direction, floated as an alternative to a mechanical speedup — noted but not pursued this round. This would mean investigating whether cart-info's own 60-byte response (which already succeeds fast and reliably) could be mined for a trustworthy game-code, avoiding the need for the separate, slower header-read entirely. Real, open research question, but distinct from the concrete fix above and not something to improvise without dedicated hardware capture/analysis — worth a dedicated session if the mechanical fix above doesn't close the gap sufficiently.
- **Scope note**: this fix also applies directly to Detect Cart's automated background path — `poll_cart()` only calls `run_detect_cart_inner()` (which calls this same header-read function) when it detects a cart change, not on every poll tick, so the speedup applies there too without further changes.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### First hardware test of the new-firmware implementation confirms real corruption, plus a new-protocol-era menu cleanup (`Detect Cart Test/test_1`, 2026-07-31) — genuinely new investigation folder, tracking Detect Cart specifically now that the dump path is stable

User correctly pushed back that last session's "raised the budget" framing wasn't the fix, and reported a new symptom: after a few cart swaps, Detect Cart started reporting the wrong game — repeatedly, consistently, not a one-off flicker.

- **Root cause found and fixed: a real regression from the previous session's own "modernize the header-read loop" change.** Traced one bad detection precisely: `marker(cmd=00 seq=0) pkt[0] ... drained 7 padding packet(s)` (a genuinely clean marker match) immediately followed by `stream(cmd=00) footer seen at 64 bytes` — the device's real footer arrived after only 64 of the requested 512 bytes. `newproto_stream_body()`'s pad-fill behavior (correct and intentional for the full-dump path, where an early footer against an *unconfirmed/estimated* size is an expected, benign case — see `rom_sizes.h`) zero-filled the remaining 448 bytes and returned success. `gbop_read_rom_header_new()` never checked for this and treated the zero-padded buffer as a complete, valid header — producing `title="PBPR0" code="" rom=32768KB` from mostly-zero data. That wrong result then got cached and reused via the `same_cart` shortcut on every subsequent detect (matching raw_resp bytes), explaining exactly why it looked "consistent" rather than flickering — once wrong, it stayed wrong until something changed the cart's raw_resp.
  - **Fix**: `gbop_read_rom_header_new()` (`gb_operator.c`) now checks `dev->np_footer_seen` immediately after the `newproto_stream_body()` call. A footer arriving before the full 512 bytes are accumulated is now correctly treated as a failed read (triggers a retry) — never legitimate for a header peek, since even the smallest real ROM is far larger than 512 bytes.
- **Confirmed the previous session's "batching" fix did not address the dominant cost.** Checked this session's own attempt spacing: individual header-read attempts still cost ~800-1000ms each, unchanged from before the batching fix. That fix only reduces round-trips for the "marker succeeded, need the rest of 512 bytes" case — but the overwhelming majority of failures happen at the marker stage itself, before any batched reading would even occur, so it never touched the actual bottleneck. Restated plainly for the user: nothing else was found that can be trimmed from the header-read check itself without also cutting correctness; the remaining lever is either (a) further budget/automatic-retry tuning (already fairly generous), or (b) the deferred "learn more about the info command bytes" research thread — i.e. try to make cart-info's own fast, reliable response carry the identifying info directly, avoiding the need for the separate, slower header-read altogether.
- **Dev-menu cleanup, per direct request**: removed "USB Probe", "Isolated Test", and "Dump Only" (`main.c`) — all three built for investigations now superseded by the new-firmware-protocol work (Isolated Test and Dump Only were built around the old `dump_rom_with_retry()`-era retry model; USB Probe's own hardware-characterization tests are long since answered). `gbop_probe_hardware()` itself is left defined in `gb_operator.c` (unused, not deleted — a public function, so no unused-symbol warning either way), consistent with this project's existing pattern for old-but-intentionally-preserved code paths.
  - **Replaced with "Cart Info Raw Dump"** — sends cmd 0x04 via the existing `gbop_raw_command_capture()` primitive (built for the old "Command Test Lab", unused since, does zero interpretation — no marker/header/footer parsing, bounded stall cap so it can never hang) and logs the complete raw response, byte for byte, to the log file. Directly supports the "learn more about the info command bytes" research direction: this project's own resp[] parsing has been wrong before in non-obvious ways (`resp[26..29]` was believed to be a size field for a long time before being shown to be a fixed constant unrelated to size — see the "NEW FIRMWARE PROTOCOL" findings above), so seeing genuinely raw, unfiltered bytes is worth more here than another parsed summary.
  - `DEV_MENU_ITEMS` 14→12. Renumbering: choice 4 keeps its slot (USB Probe's content replaced, not its position); choices 10 (Isolated Test) and 11 (Dump Only) removed outright; former choices 12 (ROM Reconcile) and 13 (Continuation Test) shifted down to 10 and 11. `draw_dev_menu()` and the `dev_menu_names[]` logging array updated to match.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### Cart Info Raw Dump — apparent freeze on first hardware use, no abort capability found and fixed (`Detect Cart Test/test_2`, 2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

User's report: "the cart dump said cmd 04 and then didnt do anything. i think it froze or didnt have a dedicated stop." `logs/Detect Cart Test/test_2/log.txt` (364 lines) ends abruptly, with the very last line being `[dev] Cart Info Raw Dump` — the menu-entry log line — and nothing further at all, not even a failed-send or NOCART line.

- **Root cause not fully provable from this one log alone** — the log simply stops at the point the tool waits for an A press to begin capture, which is consistent with either (a) the user waiting at that prompt without a clear enough on-screen cue that a button press was still needed, or (b) a genuine stall somewhere in `gbop_raw_command_capture()`'s read loop. What is provable, and was the real problem regardless of which of those it was: **the tool had no in-flight abort at all.** `gbop_raw_command_capture()` (`source/gb_operator.c`) only had two ways to stop: reaching its 2048-byte buffer cap, or `GBOP_RAW_CAPTURE_MAX_STALL` (32) consecutive non-positive reads — on a connection this project has extensively measured as capable of ~800ms-1s per attempt and prone to long stretches of failed reads, 32 stalled reads could plausibly run for many seconds to a minute-plus with zero on-screen feedback and zero way to cancel, which is exactly what "froze" would look like from the outside regardless of whether it was truly stuck or just slow.
- **Fix: added a real, checked-every-iteration abort to `gbop_raw_command_capture()` itself** (`source/gb_operator.c`), not just to the surrounding menu code — `#include <ogc/pad.h>` added, and the read loop now calls `PAD_ScanPads()` + checks `PAD_ButtonsHeld(0) & (PAD_BUTTON_X | PAD_BUTTON_Y)` on every iteration, returning a new, distinct `-2` ("user aborted") separate from the existing `-1` ("command failed to send"). Same X+Y-hold convention already used by `rom_cache_stream_chunks()` and `dump_rom_new_protocol()`'s outer retry loop — kept consistent rather than inventing a new gesture.
- **Fix: staged logging and on-screen status**, so the next hardware run pinpoints exactly where things stand instead of going silent. `gbop_raw_command_capture()` now logs `"sending command"` immediately before the OUT write and `"command sent, reading (hold X+Y to abort)"` immediately after, before entering the read loop — if a future log again stops abruptly, which of these two lines is the last one tells us directly whether the stall (if any) is in the send or the read. The `choice==4` handler in `source/main.c` was also rewritten to print progressive on-screen text ("Committing log...", "Reopening device...", "Capturing (hold X+Y to abort)...") instead of one static screen, and to give three distinct, clearly-worded outcomes instead of a single "Captured %d bytes" for everything: command-failed-to-send, user-aborted, and success-with-byte-count.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should confirm the X+Y abort actually escapes a long wait, and — win or lose — the log should now show clearly whether "Cart Info Raw Dump" is genuinely stalling somewhere or was simply slow/waiting on input, which test_2's log could not distinguish.

### Cart Info Raw Dump — real hang root-caused; the X+Y abort fix from test_2 couldn't reach the actual hang point (`Detect Cart Test/test_3`, 2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

User's report: "the abort didnt work. there needs to be a limit set. is there any other way this data can be read?" `logs/Detect Cart Test/test_3/log.txt` (457 lines) pins this down precisely — the log ends exactly at line 456, `[dev] Cart Info Raw Dump: starting capture`, and nothing follows.

- **Root cause, proven directly from the log's own established patterns, not guessed.** `gbop_reopen()` unconditionally logs `"[gbop] reopen: OpenDevice(iface[N])..."` as its very first action — confirmed 90+ times earlier in this exact log (e.g. lines 30, 40, 50...425). The `choice==4` handler's code order is: log `"starting capture"` → `log_commit_sd()` → `gbop_reopen()` → `gbop_raw_command_capture()`. Since no `"[gbop] reopen:"` line ever appears after `"starting capture"`, `gbop_reopen()` itself never started — the hang is specifically inside the single `log_commit_sd()` call between those two points. `gbop_reopen()` and `gbop_close()` are both single, bounded USB calls with no internal loop (confirmed by reading `gb_operator.c`), so neither one is a plausible hang site; `log_commit_sd()` (`fclose(g_log)` then `fopen()`) is the only remaining candidate, and it's a well-precedented one — this exact codebase has hit real fclose/fopen hazards before (tests 76-90 heap corruption, test_23/24's Command Test Lab hang/crash cycle).
- **This directly explains why the test_2 fix (X+Y abort added inside `gbop_raw_command_capture()`'s read loop) didn't help**: that function was never reached. An abort check placed inside a loop can only fire once that loop is running — it cannot rescue a hang in a prior, unrelated blocking call. There is no general way to put a "limit" on `fclose()`/`fopen()` themselves (no interruptible/timeout variant exists in this libc/libfat combination), so the fix has to be to stop calling the risky operation here, not to bound it.
- **Fix: removed both `log_commit_sd()` calls in the `choice==4` handler** (`source/main.c`), replaced with `log_force_flush()` (fflush only, no fclose/fopen). This trades a small amount of write-durability-on-crash for eliminating the actual observed hang: `lprintf()` already calls `fflush(g_log)` on every single line unconditionally (`log.h`), so per-line durability into libfat's own buffering is unaffected — only the "force a physical sector commit via fclose" step is skipped. This diverges from the sibling one-shot actions (Dump ROM/Dump Save/Upload Save all still call a real `log_commit_sd()`), a deliberate exception: this is a low-stakes diagnostic capture, not something whose partial progress needs to survive a crash, so the hang risk isn't worth paying for here.
- **Fix, directly answering "is there any other way this data can be read?": yes — most of it was already being captured for free.** `gbop_read_cart_info_new()` (`gb_operator.c`) already logs `"[gbop] (new-fw) resp[0..29]"` on every single cart-info exchange during ordinary Detect Cart / boot-time detection (visible at line 109 of this very log, from the successful detect earlier in the same session) — no dedicated tool, no extra reopen, no extra `log_commit_sd()` risk, using a path (`gbop_read_cart_info`) already exercised dozens of times per session without ever hanging. It only logged the first 30 of the 60 response bytes. **Widened to all 60** (`resp[0..59]`) — the full payload was already sitting in the local `resp[]` array (and already copied into `out->raw_resp` a few lines above), so this is a zero-cost logging change, not a new capture. Ordinary Detect Cart usage now gives the same raw-byte data the dedicated tool was built for, via a much lower-risk path — worth checking whether "Cart Info Raw Dump" is even still needed as a separate tool once this is confirmed on hardware.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session: confirm "Cart Info Raw Dump" completes without hanging now that `log_commit_sd()` is out of its path, and confirm ordinary Detect Cart's log now shows the full `resp[0..59]` line.

### Follow-up: "the abort didn't work still, the feature needs a limit" — an honest architectural answer, not another patch (2026-07-31)

User's report (still describing the same `Detect Cart Test/test_3` session, with additional detail: a hard power cycle was required, and unlike the two prior sessions' hard cycles, this one's log genuinely never committed anything past `"starting capture"`).

- **This is fully consistent with, not new evidence against, the diagnosis above.** `test_1` ends with a clean `"[dev] Exit to Loader"` / `"Exiting to HBC"` — a deliberate exit, whatever hard-cycle happened there happened after the log had already fully committed via that normal shutdown path. `test_2` ends at the bare `"[dev] Cart Info Raw Dump"` menu-entry line with no `"starting capture"` line at all — because test_2 ran on the pre-test_2-fix build, where that line didn't exist yet; the hang was already `log_commit_sd()` even then, just one line earlier in the sequence. All three sessions' "log didn't fully commit on hard cycle" symptom traces to the exact same root cause (`log_commit_sd()`'s `fclose()`/`fopen()` hanging), not something introduced by the test_2 fix — the test_2 fix (X+Y abort inside `gbop_raw_command_capture()`) simply never touched `log_commit_sd()`, so it couldn't have changed this behavior either way.
- **On "the feature needs a limit": there is no general way to put a timeout on `fclose()`/`fopen()` (or `USB_OpenDevice`/`USB_CloseDevice`) on this platform.** libogc/IOS has no interruptible or timed variant of these blocking calls, and this project has hit that exact wall before — "Abort button limitation: X+Y PAD check ... cannot interrupt a blocked USB_ReadBlkMsg call" is already documented under Hardware test findings from early in this project. A software abort check (X+Y, or anything else) can only ever fire from inside a loop that's actually running; it cannot rescue a call that's blocked before the loop is ever entered. The only real "limit" achievable here is the one already applied in the previous fix: don't call the risky operation at all in a low-stakes diagnostic tool. That fix is already in place (both `log_commit_sd()` calls removed from this screen, replaced with `log_force_flush()`) and hasn't been hardware-tested yet — no session past test_3 exists.
- **A genuine hard timeout that could rescue ANY hang, anywhere, would require a separate watchdog LWP thread that force-restarts on inactivity — deliberately not implemented.** This project has direct, hard-won experience with exactly this class of "recover from a stuck state via a forced restart" idea (`dol_reload.c`, tests 84-90): it repeatedly traded one failure mode for a worse one (BSS-zero wiping the live stack, `.sbss` pointer corruption, `SYSTEM_Init()` re-init DSI crashes) across many hardware-tested iterations, and was ultimately abandoned entirely in favor of just not calling the risky operation (`core->deinit()`) in the first place — the same shape of fix as this one. Given that history, a watchdog-based hard limit is not something to add casually; flagging it here as a known, larger option rather than implementing it unprompted.
- **Practical read**: with `log_commit_sd()` now fully out of this tool's path, the only remaining calls between button-press and result are `gbop_reopen()`/`gbop_close()` (proven non-looping, single bounded USB calls — no hang observed across 90+ calls in this exact session) and `gbop_raw_command_capture()`'s own read loop (which now has the X+Y abort and a hard 32-stall cap). If a hang still reproduces after this fix, that would point at something new — genuinely wedged IOS/USB state, not this screen's own log-commit call — and would be worth its own dedicated log.

### "Cart Info Raw Dump" removed — confirmed hang-prone on hardware, and structurally unfixable without a watchdog thread (2026-07-31)

Hardware-tested after the `log_commit_sd()` fix above: still hung, but at a different point — the log was completely empty (0 bytes) this time, and the on-screen "Capturing (hold X+Y to abort)..." prompt appeared but X+Y did nothing.

- **Root cause: a single blocking `USB_ReadBlkMsg()` call inside `gbop_raw_command_capture()`'s read loop can itself hang indefinitely, not just return slowly.** The X+Y check and the `GBOP_RAW_CAPTURE_MAX_STALL` counter both live *between* loop iterations — they can only run once a `USB_ReadBlkMsg` call actually returns. This project already documented the underlying IOS behavior responsible ("60-byte `USB_ReadBlkMsg` reads hang indefinitely if no USB packet arrives" — very first Hardware test findings entry); this hardware test is the first direct proof it also applies to the 64-byte reads this tool uses. No abort check placed inside a loop body can rescue a call that never returns from inside that same loop.
- **The 0-byte log is consistent, not a new mystery**: `log_force_flush()` (used in place of `log_commit_sd()` after the previous fix) only pushes libc's stdio buffer into libfat — per `log.h`'s own documented caveat, "libfat may not commit dirty sectors to physical SD until fclose." With `log_commit_sd()` removed from this screen and no other real commit having happened earlier in that particular session, nothing was ever physically written before the hang, even though every `lprintf()` line called `fflush()`.
- **Asked directly whether cmd 0x04 is even used elsewhere in the app: yes, extensively** — `gbop_read_cart_info_new()` sends it on every Detect Cart press, every `poll_cart()` tick, and at boot; it's the single most-exercised command in the whole protocol and has never itself been the proven cause of an indefinite hang in that path. The difference is that `gbop_read_cart_info_new()` never does a blind read loop — every read is bounded, tolerates a leading ZLP with exactly one retry, caps its footer-drain at 4 packets, and returns a definite `GBOP_OK`/`GBOP_NOCART`/`GBOP_USB` quickly either way. `gbop_raw_command_capture()`, by design ("zero interpretation, no marker/header/footer parsing" — its whole reason for existing), skips exactly those safety nets.
- **No true fix is possible without a separate watchdog LWP thread that force-restarts on inactivity** — libogc/IOS has no timeout or interruptible variant of `USB_ReadBlkMsg`. This project already has direct, hard-won experience with forced-recovery schemes of that shape (`dol_reload.c`, tests 84-90: BSS-zero wiping the live stack, `.sbss` pointer corruption, `SYSTEM_Init()` DSI crashes, eventually abandoned entirely in favor of just not calling the risky operation) — not something to reintroduce for a diagnostic-only tool.
- **Decision (user-directed, given the resp[0..59] widening above already covers what this tool was for): removed "Cart Info Raw Dump" from the dev menu entirely.** `DEV_MENU_ITEMS` 12→11; `draw_dev_menu()`, `dev_menu_names[]`, and every subsequent `if (choice == N)` block renumbered down by one (old 5-11 → new 4-10: Detect Cart, Commit Log, Back to Main Menu, Exit to Loader, Use Old Firmware toggle, ROM Reconcile, Continuation Test). `gbop_raw_command_capture()` itself is left defined in `gb_operator.c` (unused, not deleted — same precedent as `gbop_probe_hardware()`), in case a future narrowly-scoped, retry-wrapped use ever needs it.
- **Hardware-verified**: the hang itself (test_3 and its follow-up) is confirmed on real hardware. The removal/renumbering has not yet been re-tested on hardware.

### Detect Cart header-read: applied the proven ROM-dump continuation mechanism, the one piece of the new-firmware protocol that never got it (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Directly asked: "how can we make Detect Cart more reliable and/or faster? Prior to the firmware update it was much faster." Two separate causes, one addressed here:

- **Why it was faster before the firmware update, structurally**: old firmware answered "what is this cart" with ONE cheap, non-streaming cart-info (0x04) response that carried type AND size AND a usable title in a single exchange (`logs/test_122`, line 46: `type=GB rom=2048KB ram=32KB` from cart-info alone). New firmware's cart-info response has no field that reliably encodes ROM/RAM size at all (see "resp[26..29] is a fixed constant" earlier in this document) — so Detect Cart now *requires* a second, separate 512-byte ROM-header read (cmd 0x00) just to learn the real game code for `rom_sizes.c`'s lookup. That second read is not a cosmetic add-on; it's structurally the same kind of streamed, marker/footer-framed, per-attempt-unreliable exchange that a full ROM dump always was. This is a one-time capability loss from the firmware itself, not something fixable in this codebase.
- **The actionable half**: that ROM-header read (`gbop_read_rom_header_new()`, used by `read_rom_header_with_retry()`) was still using the *old*, less effective retry pattern — close, 100ms, reopen, for every single attempt (`GBOP_HDR_READ_ATTEMPTS`=45) — even though this project already has hard, hardware-confirmed proof that a different mechanism works dramatically better for the *exact same* marker/footer protocol shape: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()`, promoted to the default ROM dump path after Rom Stitching Test/test_8-9 (8 of 9 successful dumps first-try-clean, ~5-12ms per marker-only failure). The header peek never got that same treatment, despite sharing the identical C0DE-marker/data/footer framing the dump path already handles well.
- **A second, concrete reason to expect a real improvement here, not just "the same trick, hopefully": the old header-read implementation never drains its own footer before the caller closes the handle** — "the device is still mid-stream ... caller closes the handle" was always true by design (the peek only ever requests 512 of a much longer stream), but IOS does not flush EP IN across close/reopen (a recurring theme throughout this project), so that undrained footer sits queued and lands as a *stale/foreign marker* on the very next reopened attempt. Confirmed directly in `Detect Cart Test/test_3`'s own log: `"marker(cmd=00 seq=0): stale/foreign marker (got seq=01 cmd=00 ~cmd=FF)"` is exactly a leftover header-peek footer contaminating the very next attempt — a self-inflicted failure this project's own retry loop was causing, not connection noise.
- **Implemented `gbop_read_rom_header_continuation()`** (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`) — the same mechanism as `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` but requesting only 512 bytes as the declared "total" instead of the full ROM size. This works because the declared total in a ROM-read command is what the device streams before emitting its own real footer (the whole basis for the full-dump continuation mechanism, and independently for `gbop_verify_gba_rom_size()`'s partial-size mirror check) — so a 512-byte request gets a genuine, device-emitted footer right at byte 512. On a bad front or failed `header_checksum_valid()`, it drains to that real footer and resends on the *same* still-open handle instead of closing/reopening — which also means the self-inflicted contamination bug above can't recur between cycles, since every cycle properly drains before resending.
- **Wired in via `read_rom_header_continuation_protocol()` + a `read_rom_header_with_retry()` dispatcher** (`main.c`), mirroring `dump_rom_best()`'s shape exactly: `g_settings.use_old_firmware` picks between the untouched old close+reopen loop (renamed `read_rom_header_with_retry_legacy()`, zero behavior change) and the new continuation-based path. Outer budget `GBOP_HDR_CONTINUATION_OUTER_ATTEMPTS`=30 (much smaller than the dump path's 200 — a header peek only needs to succeed once per physical cart before cartindex/SD-cache takes over on this interactive, poll-driven path, unlike a background dump) wrapping `GBOP_HDR_CONTINUATION_MAX_CYCLES`=20 same-handle inner cycles per outer attempt. The existing `existing_op` persistent-handle-from-cart-info optimization is preserved (used for outer attempt 1, no reopen).
- **Deliberately NOT applied to cart-info's (cmd 0x04) own retry loop.** Considered and rejected: this project already directly tested "same handle, no reopen" vs "reopen every attempt" for that *exact* command — USB Probe Test 5 vs Test 6 (Post Firmware Update Test/test_6-7) — and found **identical** success rates either way (20%/20%, then 10%/5%). The reason the continuation mechanism helps for ROM dumps and header peeks but not cart-info: continuation's real benefit is properly *draining a long-running stream that was aborted mid-flight* before sending anything new, which matters enormously for a multi-megabyte stream but doesn't apply to cart-info, whose exchange (header + data + a capped footer-drain) is already fully consumed within every single attempt — there's no dangling stream for a persistent handle to benefit from. Applying the same fix there would be re-treating something already disproven.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's Detect Cart should be directly comparable against `Detect Cart Test/test_3` (same session shape: 45 old-style attempts needed, ~37s of wall-clock time for the header read alone) — look for a materially higher per-outer-attempt success rate and fewer `stale/foreign marker` lines specifically citing `cmd=00` (the self-inflicted contamination this should eliminate).

### Header-continuation hardware test (`Detect Cart Test/test_4`, 2026-07-31) — three real findings: the "faster" framing was wrong (it's a reliability fix, not a speed fix), console spam confirmed and fixed, and a real pre-existing size bug found in Pokemon Red — NOT YET RE-HARDWARE-TESTED

User's three reports, all traced directly in the log:

- **"Detect cart speed didn't improve" — correct, and my earlier framing overclaimed.** Emerald's header read needed all 20 continuation cycles on its very first outer attempt (`cycle 1/20` at 8366ms through `cycle 20/20: SUCCESS` at 25511ms — ~17.1s just for the header peek); Silver's needed **305 individual cycles across 8 outer attempts** (~130s). Walking the per-cycle timestamps directly: the gap between logging `"requesting 512 bytes"` and the command actually completing its send (`"cmd=0x00 tx=64"`) is itself commonly **900ms-1.5s**, not a small constant — e.g. cycle 1: `8366.638ms → 9935.113ms` (~1.57s), cycle 2: `10009.540ms → 10961.114ms` (~0.95s), with occasional fast ~20ms cycles mixed in. This is the same inherent IOS/USB round-trip latency this project's own Wireshark timing analysis already measured and explicitly could not attribute to protocol or reopen overhead (Epilogue gets ~600-700us on the same hardware). **The continuation mechanism was never going to reduce this** — its real, hardware-confirmed benefit (Rom Stitching Test/test_8-9) is a much larger effective retry budget reachable without paying reopen overhead per attempt, plus eliminating a specific self-inflicted stale-marker contamination bug (see below) — not lower per-attempt latency. Given per-attempt latency is what dominates wall-clock time and is unchanged, a cart that happens to need many attempts (Silver, this session) will still take a while, same as it always would have — the difference is it now *succeeds* instead of exhausting a smaller old-style budget and reporting failure.
- **"It also looks like it's using cmd 00 on the display" — confirmed and fixed.** `lprintf()` writes to both the log file and the TV console by default, so every `"[gbop] cmd=0x00 tx=64"`, marker-check, and checksum-failure line was scrolling across the screen during detection — raw protocol debug text, not a status a player would recognize as "still working." This was always somewhat true (the old close+reopen loop had the same issue), but continuation packs far more attempts into the same wall-clock window, so far more of this text scrolls by before an operation completes. **Fix**: `run_detect_cart_inner()` (`main.c`) is now a thin wrapper — renamed the original to `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()`, and the wrapper sets `g_log_suppress_console` for the duration (same mechanism `mgba_frontend.c` already uses around GX emulation — every `lprintf()` call still reaches the log file, only the console half is muted) and prints a `detect_progress_dot()` (plain `printf(".")`, bypasses the suppression since it isn't going through `lprintf`) once per cart-info retry and once per header-continuation cycle. `gbop_read_rom_header_continuation()` (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`) gained an optional `GbopProgressCB` parameter for exactly this purpose — called once at the top of every cycle, with no effect on retry/return logic. The result: a clean "Detecting cart......." ticking line instead of a wall of raw hex and marker/checksum diagnostics, on both the new-firmware continuation path and the untouched old-firmware legacy path (which also got a progress dot per attempt for consistency).
- **"Pokemon red rom size is wrong... definitely isn't 8mb" — a real, pre-existing bug, not caused by this session's change.** `log.txt` lines 908-909: `type=GB title="POKEMON RED" code="" rom=8192KB ram=128KB`. Traced to `rom_sizes.c`: `match_gbc_prefix()` (used for every non-GBA lookup, GB and GBC alike) unconditionally requires the CGB compatibility flag at header offset 0x143 before it will even attempt a code-based match — but original, pre-Game-Boy-Color carts (Pokemon Red/Blue/Green/Yellow) never set that flag, so the lookup always misses for literally any pure DMG cart, not just this one, and every such cart falls to the generic "unrecognized game" fallback (8192KB GB/GBC, matching exactly what was reported — 8x the real 1024KB). Real Pokemon Red: MBC3, 1024KB ROM, 32KB RAM. This gap has existed since `rom_sizes.c` was created (2026-07-27) — pure GB carts have no reliable machine-readable code the way GBA/GBC carts do, and no fallback path was ever built for them.
  - **Fix**: added a small title-matched table (`kGBTitle[]`, `rom_sizes.c`) for pure-GB games, keyed on the trimmed 16-byte title string at header offset 0x134 (the only reliable identifier these carts have) rather than a code. `extract_title_trimmed()` reads and trims it locally in `rom_sizes.c` (kept self-contained rather than reusing `main.c`'s `extract_rom_title()`, to avoid cross-module coupling for a small, simple extraction). `rom_sizes_lookup_rom_kb()`/`_ram_kb()` now fall through to this title match whenever `match_gbc_prefix()` fails (i.e., no CGB flag — a plain DMG cart). Seeded with Pokemon Red/Blue/Green(Japan)/Yellow (1024KB/32KB each, No-Intro/Pan Docs-sourced, not independently hardware-verified the way the GBC Gold/Silver entries are).
- **Not yet hardware-verified** for any of the three fixes in this entry. Next session should show: (1) a clean "Detecting cart...." status line with no raw protocol text scrolling, for both a fast and a slow detect; (2) Pokemon Red correctly reporting `rom=1024KB ram=32KB`; (3) total detect time for a cart needing many attempts (if reproducible) still taking a while — that's expected now, not a regression, given the finding above.

### Adaptive poll interval — matching Epilogue's own measured cadence for the cheap "did something change" signal (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Clarified the real goal behind "make Detect Cart faster": automatic background cart-swap recognition at a call rate as close as possible to the GB Operator's own real responsiveness — not primarily about a manual "Detect Cart" button press feeling snappier.

- **`poll_cart()` (the cheap, cart-info-only "is something different" check) was running at a fixed ~1s interval (`frame_cnt >= 60` at ~60fps) regardless of state** — slower than Epilogue's own measured *stable* cadence (~497-524ms, confirmed in the Wireshark re-analysis above) and roughly 9-10x slower than Epilogue's *searching* cadence (~104-114ms, the rate it switches to the instant a poll reads "no cart"). This was a real, direct gap against the stated goal, independent of anything about the header-read continuation mechanism.
- **Fix**: `run_dev_menu()`/`run_frontend()` (`main.c`) now take the caller's `cart_was_absent` state and use an adaptive poll interval — `GBOP_POLL_INTERVAL_PRESENT_FRAMES`=30 (~500ms) when a cart's presence is stable/confirmed, `GBOP_POLL_INTERVAL_ABSENT_FRAMES`=6 (~100ms) the instant a poll reads "no cart" — directly mirroring the two cadences measured from Epilogue's own real traffic. This only changes how often the cheap cart-info check runs; it does not change `run_detect_cart_inner()`'s own retry budget or per-attempt cost.
- **Clarifying cmd 0x04 vs cmd 0x00, since this caused real confusion**: Detect Cart genuinely uses *both* commands, at two different stages, not one or the other. cmd 0x04 (cart-info) is the fast, cheap, constantly-repeated first stage — "is a cart here, roughly what type" — and is what the adaptive poll interval above targets. cmd 0x00 (a 512-byte ROM-header peek) is a *second*, separate, much heavier stage needed only to resolve the *real* game code and ROM/RAM size, because (as established at length elsewhere in this document) the new firmware's cart-info response has no field that reliably carries size — this second stage is structurally the same slow, per-attempt-unreliable operation a ROM dump always was, and nothing changes that. Seeing `cmd=0x00` in the log during a Detect Cart is that second stage doing its job, not a bug or a contradiction of "Detect Cart uses cmd 0x04."
- **A real, unresolved architectural tension this raises, flagged rather than acted on unilaterally**: even with the poll interval now fast, `run_detect_cart_inner()` — cart-info retry *plus* the full cmd-0x00 header read — still runs synchronously on the main UI thread whenever `poll_cart()` detects a change and `auto_detect_cart=1`. Per `Detect Cart Test/test_4`'s own data (Pokemon Silver: 305 header-read cycles, ~130 seconds), a genuine swap can still freeze the game/menu for well over a minute in a bad case — faster polling only affects how quickly the *cheap* signal fires, not how long the *heavy* identification step blocks once it starts. This is very likely why `auto_detect_cart` defaults to 0 (disabled) already. Truly non-blocking "recognized while playing" swap detection would need the header-read step to move off the main thread (an LWP background thread, similar in shape to `cart_sync.c`'s existing background save-write thread) — a meaningfully larger, riskier change given this project's own history with threading/shared-state bugs (cart_sync itself, the log-FILE race documented under "Successive-session malloc hang"). Not implemented; needs a decision on whether that's actually wanted before building it.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### New finding while investigating further: cmd 0x00's own WRITE (not just the read side) is disproportionately slow, and it's tied to the command byte, not to reopening vs. resending (2026-07-31, analysis of `Detect Cart Test/test_4`)

User declined background threading for now ("we haven't found an optimal detect cart implementation yet — we can't plan background detects until detect cart is optimized") — correctly deferring the bigger architectural question until the core mechanism is better understood. Kept digging into `test_4`'s own timestamps for anything further to learn, rather than accepting "inherent, unfixable IOS latency" as the final word.

- **`gbop_bulk_send()` (`gb_operator.c`) logs its `tx=` result immediately after `USB_WriteBlkMsg()` returns**, with only a cheap CRC calc and cache flush in between — so the gap between a caller's "requesting N bytes" log line and the matching `"cmd=0x?? tx=64"` line is genuinely the write call itself, not something else hiding in between. Extracted every such gap from `test_4`'s full log (319 cmd=0x00 sends, 69 cmd=0x04 sends) and split by outcome:

  | Command | n | Fraction slow (≥100ms) | Fast-tier median | Slow-tier median |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | cmd=0x00 (ROM-read, header-continuation) | 319 | **77.7%** | 21.6ms | 948ms |
  | cmd=0x04 (cart-info) | 69 | **21.7%** | 1.3ms | 827ms |

- **Directly ruled out "same-handle resend vs. reopen" as the explanation** — the working theory going in, given continuation's whole design is "stay on the same handle." Split cmd=0x00's own gaps by what preceded them: sends right after a fresh `gbop_reopen()` were slow **71.4%** of the time (n=14); sends that were a same-handle resend (no reopen, the continuation mechanism's own resend path) were slow **78.0%** of the time (n=305) — statistically the same, not a meaningful difference. Whatever's happening isn't explained by whether the connection was just torn down and rebuilt.
- **The real differentiator is the command byte itself.** cmd=0x04's sends in this dataset are *also* all reopen-preceded (cart-info's own retry loop always reopens) yet are slow only 21.7% of the time, with a fast-tier median 15-20x faster than cmd=0x00's own fast tier (1.3ms vs 21.6ms). Two structurally identical 64-byte `USB_WriteBlkMsg` calls to the same endpoint, differing only in payload content, show a roughly 3.5x difference in how often the write itself stalls. This points at the *device*, not IOS or the Wii's USB stack generically: a ROM-read command (0x00) very plausibly requires the device to actually engage the physical cartridge bus before it's ready to accept the transfer, where a cart-info command (0x04) may be answerable from state the device already has cached/pollable — consistent with USB flow-control (device NAKing the OUT transfer until ready) being what `USB_WriteBlkMsg` is actually blocking on, not a generic Wii-side scheduling cost.
- **Actionable implication, not yet acted on**: this reframes "optimize Detect Cart" concretely — cmd 0x00 itself is the expensive operation (both to send and, per all the earlier findings, to get a valid response from), not the retry/reopen strategy wrapped around it. The highest-leverage remaining lever is very likely **avoiding cmd 0x00 entirely whenever possible** — which the cartindex/SD-cache fast path already does for a previously-seen cart, but doesn't help a genuinely new or unrecognized one. This directly revives the deferred "learn more about the info command bytes" thread (flagged in the "New protocol" findings and again after Rom Stitching Test/test_9-10): if cart-info's own response (fast, cheap, ~78% first-try-clean) carries enough identifying information on its own — `resp[5..7]` was already shown to vary by cart and is a real candidate, not yet decoded — a known game might be resolvable from cart-info alone, without ever needing the expensive cmd 0x00 peek. Worth a dedicated investigation before any bigger architectural change.

### "Sometimes says no cart when one's inserted" — the absence debounce's own math was the bug; switched to a time-based, deliberately generous threshold (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

User confirmed the intended model going forward: boot-time detect + an explicit, user-triggered "Detect Cart Swap" (no continuous automatic background re-detection — `auto_detect_cart=0` is already the default and already matches this). Given that, the two things that matter are (1) boot detect being fast and correct, and (2) once detected, the cart info **staying** shown correctly rather than spuriously flipping to "No cart detected" — directly reported as happening even with a cart physically inserted.

- **Root cause, found by doing the actual math on `poll_cart()`'s own debounce**: `GBOP_POLL_ABSENT_STREAK` (12) required 12 *consecutive* independent cart-info attempts to all fail before concluding "removed." At this connection's own measured worst-case per-attempt success rate (as low as ~5%, Rom Stitching Test/test_7), the probability that 12 consecutive attempts all fail *while a cart is genuinely present* is `(1-0.05)^12 ≈ 54%` — a false "removed" conclusion was the **more likely** outcome on a bad-connection session, not a rare edge case. Reaching even a modest ~1% false-positive rate at that same worst-case reliability needs roughly 90 consecutive successes-in-a-row-of-failure, not 12.
- **A fixed attempt count was also the wrong unit**, independent of the streak length being too short: the same-day adaptive poll interval change (30 vs 6 frames depending on state) means "12 attempts" no longer corresponds to a fixed amount of real time the way it did against the old constant ~1s interval, and per-attempt read latency itself varies a lot session to session (5-12ms up to ~1.5s, per the write-latency analysis a few turns earlier). A raw attempt count is inherently a moving target; elapsed wall-clock time is the more robust, predictable unit regardless of how fast polling happens to be running or how slow reads happen to be this particular session.
- **Fix**: `poll_cart()` (`main.c`) now tracks `s_absent_since` (a `gettime()` timestamp captured on the first failure of a streak, reset to 0 on any success) instead of a raw attempt counter, and requires `GBOP_POLL_ABSENT_SECONDS` (20 seconds) of **continuous, uninterrupted real-time failure** — not attempt count — before concluding "removed."
- **Why 20 seconds is deliberately generous, not tuned for promptness**: given the now-confirmed model (no automatic background re-detection), nothing actually depends on a real removal being reflected quickly — the only cost of being slow to notice is a stale on-screen title, and the user will trigger a fresh manual "Detect Cart Swap" themselves the instant they actually swap carts anyway. A false "removed" is actively harmful (undermines trust that the app "sticks," per the user's own report); a slow-to-notice real removal costs nothing. That asymmetry is why this leans hard toward avoiding false positives rather than balancing the two.
- **On boot detect being fast**: for a cart the app has seen before, boot detect should already skip the expensive cmd 0x00 header read entirely — `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()`'s cartindex fast path (`try_enrich_from_index()`, matched by the full 60-byte cart-info fingerprint, not by title/code) resolves game_code/title without any device ROM read, and `rom_cache_exists()` + a local SD file read fill in the rest. cmd 0x00 is only reached on a genuine cartindex miss — which should mean "never seen this cart before." **Flagged, not yet confirmed**: if any cart-info response byte legitimately varies session-to-session for the *same physical cart* (a live, open question from the `resp[9]` investigation a few turns earlier — its stability across independent sessions hasn't been confirmed either way), the cartindex's exact 60-byte match could spuriously miss even for a previously-detected cart, forcing an unnecessary slow cmd 0x00 read at boot. Worth checking directly the next time a *known, previously-indexed* cart's boot detect log is available — does it hit cartindex, or does it fall into "cartindex miss — reading ROM header" unexpectedly?
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### Cartindex now records identity on first DETECT, not first DUMP (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Two direct questions: (1) can cartindex populate on the first successful *detect* instead of waiting for the first *dump*, and (2) if the dump currently only gets its title/size from cartindex, wouldn't the dump be missing data until after it's already run once?

- **Answering (2) first, since it changes the shape of the fix**: no — the dump was never actually blocked on cartindex. `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()` already calls `enrich_info_from_buf(info, hdr_buf)` immediately after a successful header read, which fills `info->game_code`/`title`/`rom_size_kb`/`ram_size_kb`/`type` directly from the just-read header bytes — before `rom_cache_exists()` or `cartindex_update()` are ever reached. `dump_rom_best()`/`dump_rom_new_protocol()` read from that same `info` struct, so a dump has always had everything it needs the moment the header read succeeds, regardless of cartindex. Cartindex is a separate, one-way-only optimization: it lets a *future* detect skip the header read entirely — it never gated whether the *current* detect/dump had correct data.
- **What actually needed fixing, confirmed by reading the code directly**: `cartindex_update()` was previously called in only two shapes of place — (a) inside `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()`, but only in the branch where `rom_cache_exists()` *also* already returned true (i.e., a cached ROM file already existed, just needed indexing), and (b) after an actual, successful ROM dump completes (3 other call sites, including `play_game`'s auto-install). **A cart that had been successfully detected — header read, `info` fully enriched — but never actually dumped, never got recorded in cartindex at all.** Every subsequent detect of that same physical cart, even a fresh boot with it still inserted, would hit `rom_cache_exists()`-fails-because-no-file-yet and fall straight back into a full, slow cmd 0x00 header read — indistinguishable from a genuinely new cart, even though it had already been fully identified once.
- **Fix, in two parts**:
  1. **`cartindex.h`/`.c`**: `CartIndexEntry` now carries the cart's full identity (`type`, `type_str`, `game_code`, `title`, `rom_size_kb`, `ram_size_kb`), not just a ROM filename — the on-disk `cartindex.ini` line format grows from `fingerprint|rom_basename` to `fingerprint|rom_basename|type|game_code|title|rom_size_kb|ram_size_kb`. Backward-compatible: `parse_index_line()` (shared by `cartindex_lookup()` and `cartindex_update()`'s own duplicate check) tolerates a short, old-format line (fewer than 7 fields), setting `identity_known=0` for it — an old entry still resolves to a filename lookup exactly as before, it just can't skip a header read on its own the way a new-format entry can.
  2. **`main.c`**: `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()` now calls `cartindex_update()` immediately after `enrich_info_from_buf()` succeeds in the "cartindex miss — reading ROM header" branch — *before* checking `rom_cache_exists()` — using a filename `build_rom_path_sd()` predicts deterministically from the freshly-learned title/game_code (a pure function of those two fields, so it's the exact filename the ROM will occupy once actually dumped, even though the file doesn't exist yet). `try_enrich_from_index()` gained a new branch: when `cartindex_lookup()` returns matches but *none* of their files exist on SD yet (`valid_n == 0`), it now enriches `info` directly from the first `identity_known` entry's stored fields — no file read at all, since there's no file to read. The existing `cartindex_update()` call site (inside the `rom_cache_exists()`-succeeded branch) and the 3 post-dump call sites are unchanged and now simply become harmless duplicate-suppressed no-ops for a cart already indexed this way.
- **Net effect**: cartindex now captures identity the moment a cart is first successfully identified — via boot detect, manual "Detect Cart," or "Detect Cart Swap" — regardless of whether the user ever presses "Dump ROM" for it. Every subsequent detect of that same physical cart (swap back to it mid-session, or a fresh boot with it inserted) resolves from cart-info (cmd 0x04) plus the cartindex entry alone, with cmd 0x00 reserved for genuinely first-ever-seen carts.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should show: detect a never-before-seen cart *without* dumping it, exit, reboot (or swap to a different cart and back), and confirm the second detect resolves fast (`"[index] Enriched from index (not yet dumped): ..."` in the log) instead of repeating the full header-read retry loop.

### Region tracking must stay real (not dropped) — decided; header-read request size shrunk as the concrete next lever (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Follow-up to the cartindex-fingerprint-collision finding above: user laid out the precise failure case (JP FireRed dumped/indexed → US FireRed detected → cartindex's fingerprint match silently applies JP's identity to the US cart, with no way to tell afterward) and initially proposed dropping region tracking to sidestep it. After I asked which of three shapes that should take, the decision came back the other way: **region must be tracked correctly, not discarded** — cart-info (cmd 0x04) alone should never be trusted as sufficient for exact identity, but the fix is to make the confirming cmd 0x00 read cheaper/faster, not to give up on region precision.

- **Confirmed directly, not assumed: cmd 0x00 (a ROM-header read) is the *only* way to learn a cart's region.** The real 4-char game code — the only field that actually encodes region (the 4th character: E/J/F/D/I/S) — lives at ROM header offset 0xAC-0xAF, which only a device ROM read can reach. Cart-info's own crude title field (`resp[13+]`, e.g. `"PBPE0"`/`"PBPR0"`) has a fixed shape with no trailing region letter in every sample captured so far — reasoned-but-not-directly-confirmed across multiple regions of the same game (no captured Japanese FireRed cart-info sample exists yet to prove this conclusively), but consistent with everything observed.
- **User's direct question: "Dump ROM was able to use cmd 0x00 much more effectively [via continuation] — is there anything else to mimic that, and is reducing the amount of data read the right answer?"** Honest answer: the write-stall differentiator found two turns earlier (cmd 0x00 stalls ~75-78% of the time vs cmd 0x04's ~22%) was correlated with the *command byte*, not proven to depend on the *declared size* within a cmd 0x00 request — this hasn't been isolated by an actual test, since every existing cmd 0x00 use case so far has requested either a full ROM (16MB+), roughly half a ROM (`gbop_verify_gba_rom_size()`), or a flat 512 bytes (the header peek) — never anything smaller. Worth testing directly rather than guessing either way.
- **Fix (an experiment, framed honestly as one): `gbop_read_rom_header_continuation()`'s declared `total` shrunk from a flat 512 bytes to the smallest size that still supports everything downstream actually needs.** `header_checksum_valid()` (the function's own validation) needs bytes through 0xBD for GBA (≥190) or 0x14D for GB/GBC (≥334) — nothing else downstream (`enrich_info_from_buf()`'s title/game-code extraction) reads further than that either. Rounded up to a packet-aligned (64-byte) boundary: **192 bytes for GBA** (well under half the old 512 — 3 packets instead of 8) and **384 bytes for GB/GBC** (a smaller reduction, since its checksum sits much later in the header layout — 6 packets instead of 8). `gb_operator.h`'s doc comment updated to match.
- **This directly targets both the region-precision goal and the speed goal at once, if the hypothesis holds**: same mechanism (continuation, checksum-validated), same correctness guarantees, just less data requested per cycle — so a confirming read (or the first-ever detect of a truly new cart) costs less if the write-stall rate turns out to correlate with declared size at all.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's header-read logs should show whether cmd=0x00's write-stall fraction changes at all with a 192/384-byte request compared to the previously-measured 512-byte baseline (test_4: ~78% slow) — if it's unchanged, that would mean the stall is tied to the command itself regardless of size, and this change still gets to keep its (smaller, real, but secondary) benefit of moving fewer bytes once a cycle does succeed, but the bigger win would need a different lever entirely.

### Detect Cart Test/test_5 — two real bugs found in the absence-debounce work from a few turns earlier, plus a ROM dump progress bar (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Three direct reports: (1) after removing a cart, controls felt laggy — "every 3rd press" registered — only while no cart was detected; (2) after successfully detecting FireRed Japan, it immediately flipped back to "not detected," repeatedly; this also happened to Emerald after switching back to it mid-session, but not on its initial boot-time detect; (3) a request for a live progress bar during ROM dumps.

- **Bug 1 (input lag): the adaptive "absent" poll interval (6 frames, ~100ms) never actually achieved a faster real poll rate — it just left almost no idle time for input scanning.** Traced directly from the log's own timestamps during a "no cart" stretch: consecutive `poll_cart()` cycles were ~500-1000ms apart regardless of the 100ms target, because `poll_cart()`'s own blocking cost (`gbop_reopen()` + the cmd 0x04 write, which stalls ~22% of the time with a slow-tier median ~827ms — see the write-latency analysis a few turns earlier) already exceeds the configured interval most of the time. Input scanning (`PAD_ScanPads()`) only happens inside the menu's own per-frame loop, which only runs *between* `poll_cart()` invocations — so pushing the target interval down to 100ms didn't speed up detection at all (still bottlenecked by `poll_cart()`'s own latency), it just left far less idle time between calls for input to actually be read. A straightforwardly bad trade once measured against real data. **Fix**: `GBOP_POLL_INTERVAL_ABSENT_FRAMES` reverted from 6 back to 30 (matching `PRESENT`'s ~500ms) — the adaptive-interval `#define`s are kept as separate constants (not merged into one), in case a future, genuinely cheaper presence check (not this same blocking cart-info read) makes a faster absent-state cadence safe again.
- **Bug 2 (immediate false "not detected" right after a real detect): `poll_cart()`'s time-based absence debounce (added a few turns ago) has no visibility into detects that happen outside of it.** Traced precisely from the log: FireRed Japan detected cleanly at t=426198ms; the very next `poll_cart()` tick, a single ordinary transient failure only ~10.5 real seconds later, immediately logged `"177299 ms of continuous failure"` and concluded "Cart removed." `s_absent_since` (the debounce's timestamp) is set and read only inside `poll_cart()` itself — `run_detect_cart_inner()` (boot detect, manual "Detect Cart," "Detect Cart Swap," or the poll-triggered auto-detect) has no way to reset it. So a failure streak that had already been quietly accumulating *before* a cart was even present (e.g. while a different cart, or no cart, occupied the slot) stays primed, and the instant a real detect succeeds and then hits even one ordinary transient miss, the stale, already-expired streak trips immediately — explaining exactly why this happened to a *newly swapped-to* cart but not the very first boot-time detect (nothing had polled yet to build up a stale streak).
  - **Fix**: `s_pending_change`/`s_pending_change_valid`/`s_absent_since` moved from `poll_cart()`-local statics to file-scope statics, with a new `poll_cart_reset()` function that clears both. Called at all 4 `run_detect_cart_inner()` success sites (boot, poll-triggered auto-detect, dev-menu "Detect Cart," frontend "Detect Cart Swap") — any successful detect, regardless of which path triggered it, now clears `poll_cart()`'s internal state instead of leaving it to silently carry over from before.
- **ROM dump progress bar, implemented as requested**: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` (`gb_operator.c`) previously had *no* periodic progress reporting at all during its main transfer — only per-cycle start/complete/drain log lines, meaning the current "Dump ROM" screens showed nothing during the actual multi-second-to-multi-minute transfer. Added `GbopByteProgressCB` (a `(given, total, ctx)` callback, `gb_operator.h`), threaded through `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` → `dump_rom_new_protocol()` → `dump_rom_best()`. The single big post-front-check `newproto_stream_body()` call is now broken into `GBOP_DUMP_PROGRESS_CHUNK` (512KB) pieces in a loop, calling the callback after each — ~32 updates for a 16MB ROM, matching the granularity the (unused, legacy) per-chunk dump path already logged progress at. `main.c`'s `draw_dump_progress()` renders the two-line display via absolute ANSI cursor positioning (`\x1b[<row>;1H`, redrawn in place every call, never appended) — a ticking `"Rom dump in progress . . . ."` line (dots accumulate up to 20, then wrap, so the line can't grow forever on a very long dump) and a `[@@@@______] 8192/16384` bar/KB-counter line below it, matching the requested layout exactly. Wired into both explicit "Dump ROM" screens (dev-menu and frontend); `play_game`'s auto-install and the "Continuation Test" screen pass `NULL`/`NULL` (no progress bar) deliberately, kept out of scope for this change.
- **Not yet hardware-verified**, all three.

### No polling at all while a cart is confirmed absent; console suppression added to ROM dumps (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Two direct follow-ups, both agreed as real gaps rather than needing discussion.

- **"Why poll at all when there's no cart, if detection is manual-only?"** — correct, and a cleaner design than what was there. With `auto_detect_cart=0` as the settled model, `poll_cart()`'s "did something change" signal was always inert while no cart was known present — it can only ever log a message, never trigger a redetect on its own. So continuous background polling in that state bought zero functionality, only USB traffic and the structural chance of blocking input (the exact bug fixed a few turns earlier). **Fix**: `run_dev_menu()`/`run_frontend()` (`main.c`) now skip the poll-interval check entirely once `cart_absent` is true — `frame_cnt` isn't even incremented, so `-2` (poll interval elapsed) is never returned in that state, and the menu loop runs indefinitely on pure input-scanning until a manual "Detect Cart"/"Detect Cart Swap" action. Polling still runs normally while a cart *is* considered present, to notice a real removal and update the display — only the confirmed-absent state goes fully idle. `GBOP_POLL_INTERVAL_ABSENT_FRAMES` removed (no longer has a use now that the absent state doesn't poll at any interval); `GBOP_POLL_INTERVAL_PRESENT_FRAMES` (30 frames, ~500ms) is now the only poll-interval constant.
- **"ROM dumps had scrolling text on screen — should be suppressed and kept in the log. Is this accurate now?"** — it was a real, unaddressed gap in the just-added progress bar: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` logs plenty during a dump (cycle/front-check/marker lines via `lprintf()`), which reaches the console by default and would scroll straight through the fixed-position progress bar's screen rows. The progress bar itself (`draw_dump_progress()`) uses bare `printf()`, not `lprintf()`, so it was never touched by console suppression — but nothing had actually set `g_log_suppress_console` around the dump call to stop the *other* noise. **Fix**: both explicit "Dump ROM" screens (dev-menu and frontend) now set `g_log_suppress_console = 1` immediately before calling `dump_rom_best()` and restore it right after — every line still reaches the log file, only the TV console is muted, same mechanism `run_detect_cart_inner()` already uses. The final `[OK] Saved`/`[FAIL] ROM dump failed` result line prints normally afterward, once suppression is lifted.
- **Not yet hardware-verified**, either.

### Detect Cart Test/test_6 — background polling can never be trusted to declare absence; dump progress dots must tick before the bar exists (2026-07-31) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

- **"Emerald on boot had a false negative and went not detected."** Confirmed directly in the log: boot-time detect succeeded cleanly (`t≈24868ms`, `title="POKEMON EMER" code="BPEJ"`), then `poll_cart()`'s background reads genuinely, repeatedly returned NOCART for a continuous ~25 real seconds (`t≈25518ms` through `54302ms`, every single attempt in that window failing) while Emerald sat physically untouched — not the stale-timestamp bug fixed a few turns ago (that would have tripped instantly; this took the full, correctly-measured 25s), a *bona fide* sustained failure streak on this connection. Same pattern recurred 3 more times later in the same session (`116667ms`, `43375ms`, `86936ms` streaks — all logged as `"Cart removed"`). No debounce threshold fixes this: this connection's own worst sustained failure streaks are simply longer than any reasonable wait, so tuning the number up further just delays the same false conclusion, it doesn't prevent it.
  - **Decision (direct, user-driven)**: "suppress the 'no cart detected' unless a detect cart itself comes back negative." Only `run_detect_cart_inner()` — boot, manual "Detect Cart," or "Detect Cart Swap," each with its own far larger and more thorough retry budget (60 cart-info attempts, up to 30×20 continuation cycles for the header) — may ever declare a cart absent from now on. The background poll must never drive that conclusion by itself.
  - **Fix**: `run_dev_menu()`/`run_frontend()`'s poll-interval check (`main.c`) is now additionally gated on `g_settings.auto_detect_cart`. When it's 0 (the default), **no background polling happens at all** — not `poll_cart()`, not its "changed" signal, not its "absent" debounce — the menu loop is pure input-scanning until a manual detect action runs. This is a direct, logical extension of the same-day "why poll when absent" fix: since `auto_detect_cart=0` already made `poll_cart()`'s "changed" signal a no-op, and now its "absent" signal is untrusted too, there was nothing left for the background poll to usefully do in that mode at all. `auto_detect_cart=1` (opt-in, non-default) is unaffected — its whole premise is trusting the background poll to drive behavior automatically, so it keeps the original interval-gated polling and debounce logic unchanged.
- **"Dump ROM doesn't have the progressing dots like Detect Cart does... the dots should be before the progress bar appears so the player knows attempts are being made."** Confirmed as a real gap in the just-added progress bar: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()`'s byte-progress callback only ever fires once a cycle has *already* passed its marker+front checks and started real streaming — but per every measurement this session, most cycles fail before ever reaching that point. So the screen showed nothing at all — not even a dot — for however long it took to land a working attempt, indistinguishable from a hang.
