  - **Fix**: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`) gained a second, independent callback — `cycle_cb`/`cycle_ctx` (reusing the existing `GbopProgressCB` type already used by the header-read continuation), called once at the very top of *every* cycle attempt, success or failure, mirroring exactly how the header-read continuation already ticks. `main.c` splits the old single `draw_dump_progress()` into two: `dump_cycle_tick()` (ticks the `"Rom dump in progress . . . ."` line on every cycle attempt, wired to `cycle_cb`) and `draw_dump_progress()` (draws the `[@@@@______] 8192/16384` bar, still wired to the byte-progress callback, only reached once real streaming exists). Both share one `DumpProgressState` (now with a `bar_shown` field, currently just a latch/placeholder for the bar's first draw). Threaded through `dump_rom_new_protocol()` → `dump_rom_best()`; both explicit "Dump ROM" screens now pass `dump_cycle_tick` alongside `draw_dump_progress`. `play_game`'s auto-install and "Continuation Test" pass `NULL` for both, unchanged.
- **Not yet hardware-verified**, either fix.

### Save read/write vs. Wireshark: command bytes confirmed accurate; RTC read/write (cmd 0x09/0x10) discovered and partially implemented (2026-07-31/08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Directly compared the current save read/write implementation against fresh Wireshark analysis, prompted by "let's look at save reading and writing next."

- **Core save read/write (cmd 0x02/0x03) confirmed unchanged and correct** — `gbop_read_save_new()`/`gbop_write_save_new()` (`gb_operator.c`) match `save_dump_GBA.pcapng`/`save_upload_GBA.pcapng` exactly: command bytes, `cmd[1]/[4]/[5]` type-select, size at `cmd[6..8]` LE, C0DE marker/footer framing. No drift since this was first reverse-engineered.
- **Real gap found: `save_read_new()`/`write_save_new()` are single-attempt with no retry at all** — unlike every other USB operation in this codebase (which all went through the reopen-retry → continuation evolution), every save read/write call site (`play_game`'s save load, `dump_save_to_sd`, `write_save_to_cart`, the upload verify-read) is a bare, unwrapped single call. `cart_sync.c`'s background auto-sync thread is the one exception — it already has a close+reopen retry loop (3s delay between attempts), just not the newer continuation mechanism. Given everything learned this session about per-attempt reliability, single-attempt save read/write is very likely to fail far more often than it needs to.
  - **FIXED (2026-08-01)**: added `save_read_with_retry()`/`save_write_with_retry()` (`source/main.c`, right after `dump_rom_best()`) — a plain close+reopen retry loop, `GBOP_SAVE_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` (60) tries at the shared `GBOP_RETRY_PACING_US` (100ms) pacing, X+Y-hold abort via the existing `xy_abort_held()`. No continuation mechanism (unlike ROM dump/header read) — a save is at most 128KB (GBA Flash) vs a 16MB ROM, so the same reopen-loop shape already proven for cart-info/header reads should be more than adequate; nothing rules out a continuation variant later if a hardware test shows otherwise. Both wrappers follow `dump_rom_new_protocol()`'s exact contract: `*op` is always closed and set to `NULL` before returning (success or failure), so every existing call site's own `if (op) gbop_close(op)` cleanup becomes a safe no-op afterward, same as how ROM dump already works.
  - **Retrying a write from scratch on failure is safe**: `gbop_write_save_new()` only ever returns non-zero for a failure that happened before or during data transfer (command send, header marker, or a chunk write itself erroring) — a footer-marker mismatch after all data was already sent is treated as a soft warning and still returns success, so a retry never fires after a real write has already gone through. Each retry resends the complete, correct buffer from byte 0 via a fresh cmd 0x03, simply overwriting whatever a prior failed attempt left behind.
  - **Wired into every call site**: `dump_save_to_sd()`/`write_save_to_cart()` signatures changed from `GBOperatorHandle op` to `GBOperatorHandle *op` (so they can reopen internally) and now call the retry wrappers instead of the bare `gbop_read_save`/`gbop_write_save`; `play_game`'s save load and the dev-menu upload-verify read also switched to `save_read_with_retry()`. `cart_sync.c`'s background thread is untouched — its own indefinite retry loop (3s delay, retries until success or shutdown) already has no attempt cap, and being fully backgrounded, a slower-but-eventually-successful retry cadence there costs nothing the player would notice.
  - **Not yet hardware-verified.**
- **New discovery: cmd 0x09 (read) / cmd 0x10 (write) — previously completely undocumented, never implemented anywhere in this codebase.** First spotted as a single sample in `save_dump_GBA.pcapng`/`save_upload_GBA.pcapng` (one genuine occurrence each), then confirmed with 5 genuine reads and 4 genuine writes in a dedicated follow-up capture (`save_dump2upload_gba_and_gbc.pcapng`, repeated dump/upload cycles of both Pokemon Gold (GBC) and Ruby (GBA) in one session) — several other candidate matches in that capture turned out to be false positives (coincidental data-stream bytes, not real isolated commands; verified by checking for a genuine C0DE marker immediately following each candidate before trusting it).
  - **GBC (MBC3+RTC) — fully solved, high confidence.** Payload is four little-endian 32-bit integers: `[seconds, minutes, hours, day_counter_low]` — the cart's own raw MBC3 RTC registers (not BCD, not tied to any external clock). Confirmed by the seconds field incrementing in *exact* lockstep with real elapsed capture time across all 4 independent read/write samples (deltas of 6, 6, and 11 real seconds all matched precisely), while the other 3 fields stayed constant throughout a ~24-second window. The write payload mirrors a read taken moments earlier with the seconds value correctly interpolated forward — consistent with "preserve the cart's own running RTC state across a save operation," not syncing to any external time source.
  - **GBA (Seiko RTC) — date/weekday and timestamp fields solved; middle bytes still unresolved.** Date bytes (0-3) confirmed BCD year/month/day/weekday, matching the capture's real date. The write payload's bytes 8-11 are a **confirmed, verified** little-endian Unix timestamp of the PC's wall-clock time — a 16-second delta between two write samples' timestamps matched a 16.279-second real capture-time gap almost exactly, and the decoded local time (2026-07-31 21:57:20/36, UTC-5) landed within about a minute of the capture file's own save time. Bytes 4-7 (three genuinely unresolved bytes plus a separately-noted constant at offset 7, originally guessed as hour/minute/second) remain **unresolved** — they did not track elapsed real time cleanly across samples the way the other two confirmed fields did; best guess is these reflect the cart's own (possibly drifting/inaccurate) RTC hardware, which is plausibly the whole reason this sync mechanism exists, but this isn't independently verified.
  - **CORRECTION (2026-07-31, later same day): the "no wall-clock API exists" claim below was wrong — user pushed back correctly, real Wii RTC access exists.** The original check only searched `ogc/system.h`/`ogc/stm.h` headers and concluded no API existed — too shallow, since it never checked the *compiled* `libogc.a`'s actual symbol table, which is where the real answer was. Using `powerpc-eabi-nm.exe`/`objdump` directly on the linked library found `__SYS_GetRTC` as a real, exported, linkable symbol — undocumented in any public header this project includes, but genuinely present and callable via a manual `extern` declaration. Confirmed via `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` against libogc's own source (`libogc/timesupp.c`, fetched both via a doxygen mirror and directly from `raw.githubusercontent.com/devkitPro/libogc/master/libogc/timesupp.c`) and WiiBrew/GBAtemp community documentation: signature `int __SYS_GetRTC(u32 *gctime)`, returns seconds since the Wii/GameCube epoch (2000-01-01 00:00:00 UTC) into `*gctime`, non-zero return = success. Adding the fixed offset `946684800` (seconds between 1970-01-01 and 2000-01-01, itself confirmed against `timesupp.c`'s own use of the same constant) converts this to a real Unix timestamp. **Lesson**: "grep the public headers, find nothing" is not the same as "no such API exists" — the next check should always be the compiled library's real symbol table before concluding a capability is absent.
  - ~~GBA RTC write is NOT implemented, and deliberately so — this project's installed devkitPPC/libogc has no wall-clock time API at all — checked ogc/system.h, ogc/stm.h, and the whole libogc source tree for SYS_GetRTC, gettimeofday, settimeofday; none exist.~~ **Retracted per the correction above — see "Implemented (GBC and GBA)" below.**
- **Implemented (GBC and GBA)**: `gbop_read_rtc_gbc()`/`gbop_write_rtc_gbc()` (unchanged from the original implementation) plus new `gbop_read_rtc_gba()`/`gbop_write_rtc_gba()` and `gbop_wii_unix_time()` (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`).
  - `GbaRtcSnapshot` carries `date_bcd[4]` (device bytes 0-3), `misc_bytes[4]` (device bytes 4-7 — the unresolved bytes plus the offset-7 constant, passed through byte-for-byte unmodified, same "read-then-write-back for anything not understood" philosophy as the GBC implementation), and `unix_time` (device bytes 8-11 on write only — the read response has no confirmed timestamp field, so `gbop_read_rtc_gba()` leaves this at 0).
  - `gbop_wii_unix_time()` wraps `__SYS_GetRTC()` + the epoch offset, returning 0 (treated as failure, never written) if the underlying call fails.
  - The write strategy matches exactly what was agreed ("im fine with the proposed implementation") plus the newly-available real clock: read the cart's current RTC block, keep `date_bcd`/`misc_bytes` byte-for-byte identical to what was just read (never modified, since their meaning is unresolved), overwrite only `unix_time` with a fresh `gbop_wii_unix_time()` value, then write back. This never fabricates or guesses at the unresolved bytes, and now uses a genuinely real timestamp instead of either omitting the field or reusing a stale one.
  - Both read and write use the same full-512-byte marker-block-read / C0DE-header-and-footer framing as the GBC implementation, for the same short-read-desync-avoidance reason.
- New dev-menu item **"RTC Sync Test (GBC/GBA, read-then-write-back)"** (unchanged item slot, `DEV_MENU_ITEMS` still 12) now branches on cart type: GBC path unchanged (exact read-then-write-back, no modification at all); GBA path reads, prints the passed-through date/misc bytes and the read-time anchor, writes back (aborting without writing if the anchor read itself failed), then reads again to verify. Not yet wired into the main save read/write flow (`gbop_read_save`/`gbop_write_save`, `cart_sync.c`) for either cart type — kept as an isolated, hardware-testable action first, matching this project's established pattern (Continuation Test, ROM Reconcile) of proving a new mechanism in isolation before promoting it into a hot path, especially given `cart_sync.c`'s background auto-sync thread is not a safe place to introduce an unverified write operation.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should run "RTC Sync Test" against both a real Pokemon Gold/Silver/Crystal cart (GBC path, unchanged expectation: verify's readback should match what was written, modulo a few elapsed seconds) and a real Ruby/Sapphire/Emerald cart (GBA path — see the elapsed-time redesign below for what to expect).

### GBA RTC write redesigned to use elapsed monotonic time, not the Wii's absolute clock (2026-07-31, same day) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Immediately after the above was implemented, the user pointed out a real, common hardware condition this design hadn't accounted for: many Wiis — including the one this project is developed on — have a **dead RTC/CMOS backup battery**, meaning `__SYS_GetRTC()`'s own reported absolute date/time is simply wrong (the chip resets to some default/stuck value once its backup power is gone). Writing that wrong absolute date into the cart's RTC would defeat the entire point of syncing real time onto the cart. The user's requested fix: base the write on the **elapsed time between the read and the write**, not on a fresh absolute clock query at write time.

- **Design**: `GbaRtcSnapshot` (`gb_operator.h`) gained two new fields — `base_unix_time` (a `gbop_wii_unix_time()` anchor captured once, at read time) and `read_tick` (`gettime()` ticks, also captured at read time). `gbop_write_rtc_gba()` (`gb_operator.c`) no longer takes `unix_time` as caller-supplied input at all — it computes it internally as `base_unix_time + ticks_to_secs(gettime() - read_tick)` and writes the *computed* value back into `snap->unix_time` (signature changed from `const GbaRtcSnapshot *` to `GbaRtcSnapshot *` so the caller can log what was actually sent). `gettime()`/`ticks_to_secs()` (`<ogc/lwp_watchdog.h>`, already used elsewhere in this project for `lprintf()`'s timestamp prefix) read the Wii's monotonic CPU tick counter — driven by the CPU bus clock, not the battery-backed RTC chip — so it keeps correct real-time pace regardless of whether the RTC battery is alive.
- **What this fixes and what it doesn't**: the *absolute* date/time written may still be wrong, since it's still ultimately anchored to whatever wrong value `__SYS_GetRTC()` returned at read time on a dead-battery Wii — this redesign does not and cannot fix that (no dead-battery Wii can independently know the true calendar date without an internet time source, which this project has no access to). What it *does* fix: the *progression* from read to write is now always correct — if 3 real hours pass between reading the cart's RTC and writing it back, the written value is guaranteed to be exactly `base_unix_time + 3 hours`, never affected by whether `__SYS_GetRTC()` itself is frozen, stuck, or drifting. This is the same "read, then interpolate forward by elapsed time" strategy already confirmed correct for GBC (see the GBC section above — Epilogue's own write payload does exactly this), just using the Wii's monotonic tick counter instead of a second absolute-clock read as the elapsed-time source (safer, since a truly dead/stuck RTC chip could plausibly return the *same* stuck value on both reads, making a naive "read twice and subtract" delta compute as zero regardless of real elapsed time).
- **`gbop_wii_unix_time()`'s doc comment updated** to state this caveat directly: callers must treat its return value as a monotonic-forward-progressing anchor only, never as a trustworthy absolute date.
- **Dev-menu "RTC Sync Test" (GBA path) updated**: no longer calls `gbop_wii_unix_time()` a second time before writing; prints the read-time anchor, aborts before writing only if that anchor is `0` (a genuine `__SYS_GetRTC()` IOS-level failure, not merely a wrong-but-present date), and after a successful write prints the actual computed `anchor + elapsed` value that was sent.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's GBA RTC test should confirm: (1) the write succeeds and the verify readback matches what was written; (2) waiting a known amount of real time between read and write (e.g. closing the dev-menu screen, waiting, then triggering the write again) produces a written value offset from the anchor by approximately that real elapsed time, regardless of whether the Wii's own system-menu clock/date is known to be wrong.

### RTC sync deprioritized — the cart's RTC chip is autonomous of save read/write, not something this project needs to actively manage (2026-08-01)

`Save Read Write Test/test_1`'s log showed the RTC Sync Test's dozens of GBA attempts almost all failing at the marker step (the same low-reliability shape cmd 0x00 has shown throughout this project), with the one "successful" read returning implausible all-zero date/misc bytes — user correctly flagged this as untrustworthy rather than a real signal about the cart. That prompted the broader question this entry resolves: **is RTC read/write needed at all for this project's actual use case (dumping and restoring saves via USB)?**

**No.** The GBA Seiko RTC chip and the GBC MBC3's built-in RTC registers are physically separate hardware from the cart's SRAM/Flash save chip, with their own backup power (a small coin-cell battery on GBA RTC carts; a backed circuit on MBC3) and their own independent register set — confirmed structurally in this codebase too: cmd 0x02/0x03 (save read/write) and cmd 0x09/0x10 (RTC read/write) are two entirely separate USB commands with zero code overlap (`gbop_read_save`/`gbop_write_save`/`cart_sync.c`'s background thread never call any `gbop_*_rtc_*` function — grepped directly, only the isolated "RTC Sync Test" dev-menu screen does). The RTC keeps advancing in real time on its own hardware clock regardless of what happens to the save data — dumping a save, uploading a save, even reverting to an older save backup, none of it touches or resets the RTC.

The only case where the RTC genuinely needs attention is when the cart's *own* backup battery is dead — the RTC then resets/stops, and the affected game (Ruby/Sapphire/Emerald, FireRed/LeafGreen) detects this itself and prompts the player in-game to set the clock (standard, well-known behavior tied to the Berry/day-care mechanics). That is a cart-hardware condition the player resolves through the game's own UI, not something a USB save-sync tool can or should compensate for.

**Decision: RTC sync is deprioritized, not pursued further.** The "RTC Sync Test (GBC/GBA)" dev-menu screen stays exactly as it is — isolated, never wired into any real save flow, safe to leave alone — but no further effort will go into hardening `gbop_read_rtc_gba()`/`gbop_write_rtc_gba()`'s reliability (e.g. adding a `header_checksum_valid()`-style plausibility gate for RTC read content, as flagged as a possible next step in the previous session) unless a concrete need for it reappears. The cmd 0x09/0x10 discovery and decode work already done stays documented above for reference, but is no longer an active thread of work.

### In-emulation cart-sync review: a real data race found and fixed in cart_sync.c (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Direct request: review the save-write protocol and the GB/C manual vs. GBA automatic in-emulation sync mechanisms (`mgba_frontend.c`/`cart_sync.c`), not just the menu-side dev-menu/frontend save read/write already reviewed and fixed this session.

- **Wiring confirmed correct.** GBA: `on_save_data_updated()` (the `savedataUpdated` core callback) diffs the current save against a rolling snapshot; any changed byte (`threshold=1`) is treated as a real Flash write and calls `cart_sync_queue()` directly — fully automatic, no player action needed. GB/C: the same callback sets `s_player_saved_ingame` on a real save (`threshold=512`, filtering out RTC-tick-sized SRAM writes) but deliberately never auto-queues a sync — the OSD's "Sync to Cart" item (SELECT menu) is the only way a GB/C save reaches the cart, matching the documented manual-only design. Both paths funnel into the same `cart_sync_queue()` → background LWP thread → `gbop_write_save()` (the same dispatcher already confirmed to respect `use_old_firmware`), which already retries indefinitely (3s delay) until success or shutdown.
- **Real bug found: `cart_sync.c` had a genuine data race that could corrupt a save write mid-transmission.** The sync thread read `s_buf` for the *entire* duration of `gbop_write_save()` — which can legitimately take many seconds for a 128KB GBA Flash write (2048 × 64-byte USB chunks) or far longer across retries on a bad connection — but that read was never protected by `s_mutex`. Meanwhile `cart_sync_queue()` (called from the main thread inside `on_save_data_updated`, which can fire again mid-transfer on any further SRAM/Flash write within that multi-second window) `memcpy`'d new data directly into the *same* buffer under the mutex — but since the reader side never checked the mutex, a concurrent queue call during an in-flight write could tear the buffer, sending a corrupted mix of two different save states to the physical cartridge. This is a real, plausible scenario in practice, not just theoretical: GBA's `threshold=1` means even a small incidental SRAM/Flash write during an already-slow multi-second transfer could re-trigger `cart_sync_queue()` before the current write finishes.
  - **Fix**: split the single buffer into two — `s_xmit_buf` (read by `gbop_write_save()`, touched by the sync thread only) and `s_pending_buf` (written by `cart_sync_queue()`, always under the mutex). The sync thread copies `s_pending_buf → s_xmit_buf` in one quick, mutex-protected memcpy at the moment it picks up a queued job, *before* starting the write-retry loop — from that point on, `s_xmit_buf` is stable for the entire (possibly long, possibly retried) transfer, since nothing else ever touches it. A concurrent `cart_sync_queue()` call during that window only ever writes to the separate `s_pending_buf`, which is safely picked up on the *next* cycle instead of corrupting the one in flight. `cart_sync_init()`/`cart_sync_shutdown()` updated to allocate/free both buffers; the abandoned-thread shutdown path (thread still blocked in a USB call after the timeout) still deliberately leaks `s_xmit_buf` only (same reasoning as before — the thread may still access it when IOS eventually unblocks) but now safely frees `s_pending_buf` immediately, since the thread only ever reads it once, synchronously, before the blocking write call begins.
  - **Secondary finding, fixed alongside**: `cart_sync_queue()` silently no-op'd (zero logging) whenever `size != s_buf_size`. Every call site already clamps its own upload size to `<= s_buf_size` before calling this, so the only way to hit this branch is `mCore::savedataClone()` returning *smaller* than `info->ram_size_kb*1024` (e.g. a save-size mismatch between this project's own hardware-derived detection and mGBA's own auto-detected save type) — a real, if rare, possible detection disagreement that was previously invisible anywhere in the log. Added a clear `lprintf()` before the drop so a future occurrence is diagnosable instead of just "saves silently stopped working."
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** This race would only ever manifest under a specific timing window (a second real save event landing while a prior write is still actively streaming) — likely rare in normal play but not exotic on a slow/bad connection where a single write can take much longer than usual. No existing test log was found to show clear evidence of a torn write actually happening (would look like PKHeX rejecting a save or showing a nonsensical mixed state) — this was caught by code review, not by reproducing the symptom on hardware. Next session should confirm no regression in normal single-save-event sync behavior (both GB/C manual and GBA automatic).

### Play Game confirmation prompt + re-verify-before-proceeding, closing the stale-detect data-loss risk (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Direct request/design discussion: since `auto_detect_cart=0` (manual-only detect) is the default, "the detected game" shown on screen can silently go stale if the player physically swaps carts without pressing "Detect Cart Swap" again — pressing "Play Game" would then install/play/dump under whatever title happens to still be cached in `info`, not the cart actually in the slot. User proposed a confirmation prompt ("Play `<detected title>`? A=confirm, B=back to menu") and separately raised a sharper question: even if the player presses A anyway with the wrong title showing, the resulting ROM/save files still need to end up under the *correct* name — what did I think of that?

- **Confirmation prompt implemented as described**: frontend "Play Game" (`choice == 0`) now shows `"Play <display_title>?"` via the existing `prompt_yesno()` helper before doing anything else; B returns to the menu (`continue`), matching every other Y/N prompt in this codebase. Skipped entirely when nothing is detected yet (`play_game()` already shows its own "No cart detected" message in that case, unchanged).
- **The prompt alone doesn't solve the deeper problem, and the user was right to ask about it separately.** Traced through what actually happens if the player confirms anyway with a stale `info`: the ROM-cache check (`rom_cache_exists(info, ...)`) builds its expected filename/size from the *stale* `info` — if a cached ROM already exists under that stale name (likely, since the player presumably played that game before), the flow concludes "already installed," skips any fresh dump entirely, and goes straight to reading the *save* from whatever cart is now physically inserted — using the stale game's `ram_size_kb`/filename the whole way. Worst case: the physically-different cart's real save data gets read and written **over the previous, correct save file for the stale game**, silently, with no re-verification anywhere in that path. This is a real, silent data-loss risk, not just a display/wording issue — the confirmation text alone can't prevent it, since the player has no way to know `info` is stale until it's too late (that's the whole premise of the ask).
- **Fix: after the player confirms, re-run the same detect used everywhere else (`run_detect_cart_inner()`) before doing any ROM/save I/O**, refreshing `info`/`display_title`/`rom_hdr` in place before `play_game()` ever runs. This is deliberately *not* a hand-rolled lighter-weight check — it reuses `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()`'s own existing `same_cart` fingerprint fast path (a single cart-info round-trip, comparing the 60-byte raw response against what's cached), the same mechanism "Detect Cart Swap" already relies on to stay cheap when nothing has changed. So the added cost is: near-zero when the same cart is still inserted (the common case — one quick, already-fast cart-info check confirms the fingerprint matches, `info` is left as-is), and a full, necessary re-detect only in the exact case that actually matters (a different cart is now in the slot) — at that point `info` gets corrected to the real, physically-inserted cart's identity *before* any file is named or any USB read happens, closing the overwrite risk described above at the source rather than trying to patch it after the fact.
- **This also directly answers the third part of the ask** ("after playing, the most recent game information should occupy the current game data") **for free**: `run_detect_cart_inner()` updates `info`/`display_title`/`rom_hdr` in place — the same variables the rest of the frontend loop already reads — so once `play_game()` returns, whatever was actually just played is already what's shown as "the current game," with no separate synchronization needed.
- **No second confirmation dialog added** if the re-detect reveals a *different* game than what the player just confirmed — per the user's own framing ("the dumped rom and save still need to be saved under the correct name," not "ask again"), the fix silently corrects `info` and proceeds, rather than adding a second prompt for something the player already said yes to once. If the re-detect fails outright (cart genuinely not found — e.g. actually removed), the flow mirrors "Detect Cart Swap"'s own failure handling exactly (`cart_was_absent=true`, `info`/`rom_hdr` cleared, `display_title="No cart"`, message shown) and returns to the menu without ever calling `play_game()`.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next test: (1) confirm the prompt shows the right title and B correctly returns to the menu; (2) confirm proceeding with the *same* cart still inserted feels just as fast as before (no perceptible re-detect delay); (3) if practical to test, swap carts without pressing Detect Cart Swap, press Play Game, confirm the stale-title prompt, and verify the subsequent dump/play correctly reflects the *new* cart rather than the one shown in the prompt.

### Save Read Write Test/test_7 — CRITICAL: Check #2 caused a genuine infinite loop on every GBC cart, root-caused and fixed (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

User report: playing FireRed then swapping to Gold without redetecting correctly triggered a real mismatch and a ROM install, exactly as designed — but then "save detect starts again" repeatedly after the install, and console suppression was missing during the save-dump section. The log (4444 lines, one session) confirmed something far more serious than a UI gap: **Check #2 (added in the previous entry) was in a genuine, reproducible infinite loop**, firing the identical false "cart type mismatch (expected GBC, cart reports GB)" over 10 times across ~230 real seconds before the session happened to break out of it.

- **Root cause: `gbop_read_cart_info()` can never report `CART_TYPE_GBC` — only `CART_TYPE_GB` or `CART_TYPE_GBA`.** Confirmed directly in `gb_operator.c` (both the legacy and new-firmware paths): the bare cart-info response (`resp[2]==0x20` vs `0x30`) only distinguishes "GB/GBC family" from GBA — upgrading to `CART_TYPE_GBC` specifically requires an actual ROM header read to check the CGB flag at offset 0x143, which the cheap Check #2 comparison never does. So `live.type` (from the quick check) is *structurally incapable* of ever equaling `CART_TYPE_GBC`, even when comparing a cart against itself. Every properly-detected CGB game (Gold/Silver/Crystal) therefore mismatched against its own quick-check reading, 100% of the time, unconditionally: quick check → "GB" vs cached `info->type` → "GBC" (correctly set by the earlier full detect) → treated as a mismatch → `run_detect_cart_inner()` (correctly re-confirms GBC) → recursive `play_game()` call → hits the exact same quick check again → mismatches again → repeat, with no way to ever exit the cycle on its own. This is exactly what the log shows: `"hdr] Enriched: type=GBC..."` / `"detect] Same cart"` (a completely correct, successful re-detect) immediately followed by the same false mismatch message again, over and over.
- **Fix**: added `cart_type_family_mismatch()` — treats `CART_TYPE_GB` and `CART_TYPE_GBC` as the same broad family for this specific comparison, so only a genuine category difference (GBA vs. GB-family, which is exactly what correctly fired for the real FireRed→Gold swap earlier in the same session) counts as a mismatch. GBC vs GBC (or GB vs GB) can no longer falsely trigger.
- **Console suppression also fixed**, scoped tightly per-loop rather than function-wide (to avoid accidentally leaving `g_log_suppress_console` stuck at 1 across one of the many early-return paths in this section): all three retry loops in `play_game()`'s save-read section — the pre-save-read cart-info recheck, the fresh-fd-wait, and the second recheck after a fd-spent condition — now suppress console output for the loop itself only, restoring immediately after the loop ends (`break` or falls through) and before any subsequent decision logic (a shown message, a detected mismatch) runs.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should repeat the exact same sequence (play FireRed → swap to Gold without redetecting → Play Game → confirm stale prompt → mismatch correctly detected → install Gold → save dump) and confirm it now proceeds straight through to actual gameplay after the install, with no repeated "cart type mismatch" and no unsuppressed reopen/retry spam during the save-dump section.

### Save Read Write Test/test_6 — the blanket re-verify above was too slow in practice; redesigned around two narrow, opportunistic checks instead (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Four issues reported from one session, all traced directly to log timestamps:

1. **"Detect cart still ran when the game wasn't changed."** Confirmed: the blanket re-verify added above still needs a *real* cart-info exchange to succeed before its `same_cart` fingerprint comparison can even run — and this connection's own per-attempt reliability is often poor enough that a single cart-info check alone needed 10+ retries in this log (e.g. 14 retries, ~9.6 real seconds, immediately after "Play Game" was pressed on a cart that had NOT changed). The "near-zero cost in the common case" claim in the entry above was wrong — it assumed the *first* cart-info attempt usually succeeds, which this connection doesn't reliably do even when nothing changed. So the blanket re-verify visibly ran a full Detect-Cart-length operation on every single Play Game press, contrary to the intent.
2. **"Rom dump progress has a lot of other text on the console, not just the progress bar."** Root cause: `verify_rom_size_before_dump()` (the GBA ROM-size mirror check, called immediately before every ROM dump) ran its own marker/protocol logging *before* any of its three callers (`play_game()`, dev-menu Dump ROM, frontend Dump ROM) ever set `g_log_suppress_console` — confirmed directly in the log (lines 331-339: the mirror-check's own "Verifying reported ROM size," marker-mismatch, and "aborted (read error)" lines all precede where suppression would start). **Fix**: moved the suppression *into* `verify_rom_size_before_dump()` itself (save/restore `g_log_suppress_console` around its own body, including the fd-reopen fallback path) — fixes all three call sites at once instead of needing the same fix repeated three times.
3. **"After Rom dump got a cart not detected error"** and 4. **"Subsequent attempts had a fast detect... but could not get to the save dump step due to the 'cart not detected' error."** Both trace to the same bug: `play_game()`'s pre-save-read cart-info check (confirms the cart is still present / resets the device to idle before the save read) was a **single attempt plus one NOCART retry (2 total)** — never updated to use the `GBOP_DETECT_CART_ATTEMPTS`-scale budget every other cart-info check in this codebase uses. Confirmed directly: exactly 2 failed cart-info attempts appear immediately after `"ROM dump complete... SUCCESS"` in the log, followed by `show_message("Cart not detected...")` aborting the *entire* play_game() flow right after a real, successful ROM install — forcing the player to press Play Game again from scratch, every time, on a connection where 2 attempts is nowhere near enough. **Fix**: both cart-info checks in this section (the initial one, and the second one on the fresh fd after `need_cart_recheck`) now retry up to `GBOP_DETECT_CART_ATTEMPTS` (60) times, matching `run_detect_cart_inner_impl()`'s own budget, with `xy_abort_held()` support.

**Given finding #1, the blanket re-verify from the entry above is removed entirely** — replaced with a design worked out directly with the user, prioritizing speed over automatic certainty:

- **The confirmation prompt is now the only guard for the general case.** No automatic hardware re-check runs after confirming "Play `<title>`?" — Play Game is exactly as fast as before again when the cart hasn't changed, since nothing extra runs.
- **Two narrow, opportunistic checks catch what's cheaply detectable from operations that already have to happen anyway, and escalate to a real detect only when they actually fire:**
  - **Check #1 (ROM dump title mismatch, `play_game()`'s ROM-install section):** a pre-dump snapshot of `info->title`/`game_code` (what the player just confirmed) is compared against the *freshly-dumped ROM's own real title/code* (from `enrich_info_from_rom()`, which reads the just-written file's actual header bytes) immediately after a dump completes. `enrich_info_from_rom()` already correctly renames the file to match its real content regardless (unchanged, pre-existing behavior) — what it does *not* do is touch `rom_size_kb`/`ram_size_kb`/`type`/`raw_resp`, which stay stale and wrong for whatever's actually inserted, and which the following save-dump step needs correct. On a mismatch, `run_detect_cart_inner()` runs for real to refresh everything from the actual cart before the existing rename/cartindex logic proceeds — accepting one redundant device header-read in this rare, mismatch-only path in exchange for reusing the same already-proven detection logic rather than a second hand-rolled one.
  - **Check #2 (save type mismatch, the pre-save-read cart-info check):** the cart-info read this check already has to do anyway is also compared for `type` (GB/GBC vs GBA) against the cached `info->type` — free, no extra USB cost. On a mismatch, `run_detect_cart_inner()` runs for real, and then (per direct instruction — this case is different from check #1, since every ROM-cache/install decision already made this call was based on stale info and can't just be patched in place) `play_game()` calls itself recursively with the corrected info, restarting the whole sequence (install check → save dump → play) from the top rather than trying to continue inline.
- **Deliberately does not catch a same-type, same-size mismatch** (e.g. Ruby ↔ Sapphire — both GBA, both 128KB Flash) — raised directly with the user, who accepted this as residual risk. Their reasoning, confirmed correct for GB/C but with one real caveat surfaced and also accepted: GB/C sync is manual (player-gated, SELECT → Sync to Cart), so a player noticing a mismatch can exit without ever syncing back, leaving the physical cart's real save untouched. **GBA sync is fully automatic and confirmation-free** (`cart_sync.c`'s background thread writes to the cart the instant any save byte changes, threshold=1) — so in the Ruby/Sapphire scenario, the real physical Sapphire cart could be overwritten by an incidental in-game write *before* the player necessarily notices anything is wrong, with no "are you sure" step in between. Raised this explicitly; user's decision: accept the risk, reasoning that the wrong title on the GBA boot/title screen plus visibly different save contents should be noticeable enough, **provided quitting never forces an additional, unwanted sync**. Verified directly (not just assumed): `mgba_run()`'s teardown only ever does an SD-only fallback write (gated on `s_player_saved_ingame`, never touches the cart) and `cart_sync_shutdown()` never starts a *new* sync — it only waits for one already in flight or abandons it. So quitting a GBA session never adds a cart write beyond whatever auto-sync had already independently decided to do — this requirement was already satisfied without any code change.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should confirm: (1) Play Game feels fast again with an unchanged cart (no visible detect running); (2) the two log/bug fixes (console suppression, cart-info recheck budget) resolve issues 2-4 from this test; (3) if a mismatch can be deliberately reproduced (swap ROM-uncached cart without redetecting, or swap GB↔GBA), confirm the corresponding check fires and corrects identity/restarts appropriately.

### Save dump/upload progress display + Play Game text consistency (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

Direct request after a clean test (`Save Read Write Test/test_5`, no issues): (1) replace the save dump/upload progress display (previously raw scrolling `printf(".")` per retry attempt) with a fixed-position "Dumping save . . . ." ticker matching the ROM dump's existing style; (2) make `play_game()`'s internal ROM-install and save-read sections use the exact same header text and progress display as the dev-menu/frontend "Dump ROM"/"Dump Save" screens, not their own different wording.

- **New `SaveProgressState`/`save_cycle_tick()`** (`source/main.c`, right after `draw_dump_progress()`) — same ticking-dots mechanism as `dump_cycle_tick()` (redrawn in place via absolute cursor positioning, wraps at 20 dots so a long retry stretch can't grow the line forever), parameterized with a `label` field so one helper covers both "Dumping save" (read) and "Uploading save" (write) instead of needing two near-identical functions.
- **`save_read_with_retry()`/`save_write_with_retry()`** now take `GbopProgressCB cycle_cb, void *cycle_ctx` and fire it once per attempt — same "fires on every attempt, success or failure" convention as `dump_rom_new_protocol()`'s `cycle_cb`, so the ticker shows progress from the very first try. Removed the old raw `printf(".")` call site now that the caller-supplied callback handles display.
- **Every call site updated**: `dump_save_to_sd()`, `write_save_to_cart()`, and `play_game()`'s inline save-read now all pass `save_cycle_tick` with an appropriate label; the dev-menu's post-upload verify-read passes `NULL, NULL` (a background check, not a primary user-facing wait, left undecorated same as before). All four also wrap their retry call in `g_log_suppress_console = 1/0` — the same reasoning as the ROM dump bar: `gbop_read_save`/`gbop_write_save`'s own marker/protocol logging would otherwise scroll straight through the fixed-position ticker line. Log file unaffected either way.
- **`play_game()` text/display now matches the dev-menu exactly**: the ROM-install section's old "Installing ROM — please wait..." replaced with the same `"Dump ROM\n--------\n\n"` header, and its `dump_rom_best()` call now passes `dump_cycle_tick`/`draw_dump_progress` (previously `NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL` — no progress feedback at all during a Play-Game-triggered ROM install). The save-read section's old "Reading save from cart..." replaced with the same `"Dumping save: %s (%u KB)\n-------------------------------\n\n"` header `dump_save_to_sd()` already used. This only changes `play_game()` (the "Play Game" flow) — the frontend's own separate standalone "Dump ROM"/"Dump Save" menu items (a different screen, not part of the Play Game sequence) were intentionally left as they were; not in scope for this request.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

### GBC custom border missing for Pokémon Silver (Korean) — wrong gate condition, fixed (2026-08-01) — NOT YET HARDWARE-VERIFIED

User report (no log available for this one — reported directly): `border_AAXK.bmp` correctly named and placed in the GBC borders folder, but never loads for Pokémon Silver (Korean); the equivalent US Gold/Silver borders both work fine.

- **Root cause**: the custom-border block in `mgba_frontend.c` was gated on `if (has_sgb && is_cgb && ...)` — both the CGB flag (ROM header 0x143) AND the Super Game Boy compatibility flag (0x146 == 0x03) had to be set before the code even looked for a border file. The Korean localization of Pokémon Gold/Silver does not set the SGB-compatible flag in its ROM header the way the US/JP/EU releases do (a real, documented difference in that release's ROM header, independent of this project) — so `has_sgb` came back false and the entire block, including the `border_AAXK.bmp` lookup, was skipped before ever checking whether the file existed.
- **Why `has_sgb` was in the gate at all, and why it was never actually needed there**: this same detection block has two branches — pure-SGB, non-CGB games (e.g. Pokémon Blue) get mGBA's own native SGB border handling (`if (has_sgb && !is_cgb) { mCoreConfigSetValue(..., "sgb.model", "SGB"); }`), while CGB games get this project's own custom composited border instead. `has_sgb` was needed to identify the *first* branch; it was carried over into the second branch's gate too, but `is_cgb` alone already fully identifies "this is a color game a border image could apply to" — nothing about showing a custom composited border actually depends on whether the ROM also happens to declare SGB peripheral compatibility.
- **Fix**: the gate is now `if (is_cgb && rom_data && rom_len >= 0x144)` — `has_sgb` dropped. A CGB game with no matching `border_CODE.bmp` on SD still degrades gracefully exactly as before (`load_border_bmp` returns NULL, `s_border_active` stays false); this change only stops excluding CGB games whose ROM doesn't declare SGB compatibility, which is exactly the Korean Gold/Silver/Crystal case (and would apply to any other CGB game with the same header quirk).
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session should confirm Pokémon Silver (Korean) now loads `border_AAXK.bmp` correctly, and that US Gold/Silver (already working) show no regression.

### Save Read Write Test/test_4 — two real bugs found from the same root cause: GBA sync icon frozen during retries, and "Sync & Exit" still exiting early despite the test_3 fix

User report: "GBA Save sync indicator doesn't cycle frames when sync in progress." Traced directly from the log's own timestamps, which also caught a second bug in the exact same area — the test_3 fix (making `cart_sync_queue()` set state synchronously) turned out to be necessary but not sufficient.

- **Icon-freeze root cause, confirmed precisely from one real GBA auto-sync in this log**: `state: IDLE → IN_PROGRESS` at 358672.025ms, then `state: IN_PROGRESS → FAILED` at 358691.603ms — **only ~19.5ms later**, because the very first write attempt failed almost immediately (`header marker mismatch`). The retry loop's *second* attempt then actually streamed the full 128KB successfully, but that took until 375154.033ms (`state: FAILED → SUCCESS`) — **about 16 seconds**, all spent in the `FAILED` state. `draw_save_indicator()` (`mgba_frontend.c`) only animated the icon while `st == CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS`; `CART_SYNC_FAILED` explicitly drew a static frame ("no animation"). Since a real sync spent ~19.5ms animating and ~16 seconds frozen, the animation was visible for well under 1% of the sync's actual duration — indistinguishable from "doesn't cycle" to anyone watching it. `CART_SYNC_FAILED` means "actively retrying in the background" per `cart_sync.h`'s own doc comment, not "stopped" — freezing the icon there was never really representing what was actually happening.
  - **Fix**: `draw_save_indicator()` now animates through `CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS` and `CART_SYNC_FAILED` identically — the icon keeps cycling for the entire time a sync is genuinely still being attempted, not just the brief window of whichever single attempt happens to be in flight at that instant. The fallback 8×8-dot path (used when icon BMPs aren't on SD) is unaffected — it still colors red during `FAILED` vs. yellow during `IN_PROGRESS`, since that distinction was never the problem (a static dot was never expected to animate in the first place).
- **Second bug, same root cause, caught while tracing the same log**: the test_3 fix (`cart_sync_queue()` setting state synchronously) is confirmed working — `state: SUCCESS → IN_PROGRESS` now appears immediately when "Sync & Exit" queues a job (158374.618ms), right where test_3 showed nothing at all. But the "Sync & Exit" wait loop in `osd_show()` (`mgba_frontend.c`) still exited within ~35ms (`"[mgba] Exiting emulation"` at 158410.105ms) — because its condition was `while (st == CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS && ...)`, and this particular queued write *also* failed its first attempt almost instantly, flipping to `FAILED` just as fast as the GBA case above. The wait loop treated `FAILED` as "done waiting" and exited, and teardown shut the sync thread down long before its retry loop ever got a real chance to succeed — the exact same silent-drop outcome test_3 found, just reached through a different trigger (a fast first-attempt failure) than the original state-visibility race (which that fix already closed).
  - **Fix**: the wait loop's condition changed to `while ((st == CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS || st == CART_SYNC_FAILED) && --timeout > 0)` — it now correctly keeps waiting through retries, only stopping on `SUCCESS`, the 10s timeout, or the player holding B to skip.
- **Both bugs share the same lesson**: `CART_SYNC_FAILED` is a real, often long-lived, "still working" state — not a terminal one — and every place that reasons about "is a sync still happening" needs to treat it that way. Two independent call sites had the same blind spot; worth checking for a third if this class of bug resurfaces.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session: watch the GBA icon during a sync that needs a retry (should now keep animating throughout, not freeze), and repeat the test_3 "Sync & Exit" sequence again to confirm it now waits out a slow/retried write instead of exiting early.

### Save Read Write Test/test_3 — CONFIRMED bug found and fixed: "Sync & Exit" can silently drop the player's final save with zero log evidence it was ever attempted

User report: dumped a save, played Pokemon Gold, saved in-game, used "Sync to Cart," then on the next session's Dump Save the cart came back with a fresh/blank save with no prior progress — user's own hypothesis was "the sync synced a blank save." Traced precisely from `log.txt`'s timestamps and confirmed a different, more specific mechanism: **the player's actual final sync never happened at all.**

- **Exact sequence from the log**: a first manual "Sync to Cart" (main OSD, mid-session) genuinely succeeded — `state: IDLE → IN_PROGRESS` at 148384ms, one failed attempt, a retry, `state: FAILED → SUCCESS` at 152017ms. The player then apparently resumed playing and later chose "Sync & Exit" from the exit sub-menu. That queues a **second** write: `[sync] Save queued` / `[osd] Sync & Exit queued: 32768 bytes` at 159025.889/897ms. But **no `state: IDLE → IN_PROGRESS`, no `Save write start`, nothing at all appears between that queue call and `[mgba] teardown: shutting down cart_sync` a mere 44ms later, followed by `[sync] Cart sync thread joined cleanly` only 10ms after that** — the background sync thread exited without ever picking the job up.
- **Root cause: a state-visibility race, not a protocol bug.** `cart_sync_queue()` sets `s_pending=1` and posts a semaphore to wake the background thread, but `s_state` (what `cart_sync_state()` reports) stays `IDLE` until the *thread itself* gets scheduled and calls `set_state(CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS)` — which can take a real, measurable amount of time on a single-core Wii. The OSD's "Sync & Exit" wait loop (`osd_show()`, `mgba_frontend.c`) is a `do { st = cart_sync_state(); ... } while (st == CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS && ...)` — a `do-while`, so its *first* check runs immediately after queuing. If the background thread hasn't been scheduled yet at that exact moment, this first check sees stale `IDLE`, the loop's condition is false immediately, and it exits after a single ~16ms frame — even though a job was *just* queued and is still sitting there unprocessed. `do_quit=true` follows immediately, teardown calls `cart_sync_shutdown()`, which sets its own shutdown flag and wakes the thread — and the thread, waking up for the first time only now, sees the shutdown flag already set and exits without ever touching the still-pending job. Two independent race windows (the wait loop's first check, and `cart_sync_shutdown()`'s unconditional immediate shutdown) compounding on the same underlying gap: nothing distinguished "genuinely idle" from "a job was just queued but the thread hasn't started it yet."
- **This directly matches the user's symptom**: whatever additional progress/save happened *after* the first successful mid-session sync was captured correctly into the "Sync & Exit" queue call, but was then silently discarded — the cart was left holding only the earlier mid-session state, not the player's actual final save before quitting. (Separately, worth noting honestly: every `savedataUpdated` diff logged this whole session stayed well under the 512-byte real-save threshold — never once reaching the ≥3584 bytes a genuine Pokémon save section rewrite produces — so it's also possible the in-game save itself didn't fully register before either sync attempt; this wasn't something the log could confirm either way, and doesn't change that the dropped-queue bug above is real and independently confirmed regardless.)
- **Fix (`source/cart_sync.c`)**: `cart_sync_queue()` now calls `set_state(CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS)` itself, synchronously, on the calling (main) thread — immediately after setting `s_pending=1`, before posting the semaphore — instead of leaving that transition for the background thread to perform whenever it next gets scheduled. `s_state` already had no dedicated lock (`cart_sync_state()` always read it directly), so writing it from the main thread too doesn't introduce a new synchronization requirement — it just closes the visibility gap. Any caller checking `cart_sync_state()` right after `cart_sync_queue()` returns — the "Sync & Exit" wait loop, the OSD's status line, anything else — now correctly sees `IN_PROGRESS` immediately, with no window where a genuinely-queued job looks indistinguishable from idle.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session: repeat the same sequence (mid-session manual sync, keep playing, save again, "Sync & Exit") and confirm the wait loop now correctly shows "Syncing..." and waits for the second write to actually complete (or times out/gets skipped *after* genuinely trying), and that the next session's Dump Save reflects the truly final in-game state.

### Save Read Write Test/test_2 — save read/write retry confirmed working (PKHeX-validated); Detect Cart budget confirmed honored end-to-end, but a real 0-byte-log risk found and fixed (2026-08-01)

User reported "Detect cart seems to have failed twice... it gets to 4 lines of dots before stopping. It needs to commit to the budget of reads," and separately confirmed save dumps all checked out in PKHeX (direct hardware validation that `save_read_with_retry()`/`save_write_with_retry()`, added this same session, work correctly). `log.txt` for this test is a genuine 0-byte file; `log1.txt`/`log2.txt` (630 and 1855 lines) are two later, complete sessions.

- **The retry budget is NOT being cut short — verified directly from the log data, not assumed.** Every single Detect Cart in `log1.txt`/`log2.txt` genuinely exhausted multiple full 20-cycle header-continuation outer attempts (up to 4-5 of the 30-attempt budget, i.e. 80-100+ individual cmd 0x00 cycles) before eventually succeeding — no instance of `"exhausted 30 outer attempts"` (the real, final give-up message) appears anywhere in either log; every detect that started also finished, successfully, just slowly (some took 100-290+ real seconds). `log2.txt` itself ends cleanly at a deliberate `"[dev] Exit to Loader"` / `"Exiting to HBC"`, not an abrupt cutoff — confirming that session ran to completion and was exited on purpose. So "gets to 4 lines of dots before stopping" describes what the user *saw* on screen, not what the code was actually doing underneath — the process kept retrying well past that point in every logged case.
- **Root cause of the visual impression, and the real, separate finding: none of the four `run_detect_cart_inner()` call sites (boot-time detect, the poll-triggered auto-detect, dev-menu "Detect Cart", frontend "Detect Cart Swap") ever called `log_commit_sd()` before starting.** Every `lprintf()` line during a detect is already `fflush()`'d (per `log.h`), but per that same file's own documented caveat, "libfat may not commit dirty sectors to physical SD until fclose" — so a detect that runs for minutes on nothing but `fflush()`s is one indefinite USB stall away from losing everything back to the last *real* commit (which, at boot, could be the entire session so far). This is the exact same shape of bug already found and fixed once before in this project (test_23's Command Test Lab 0-byte-log finding) — it just hadn't been applied to `run_detect_cart_inner()`'s own call sites, which didn't exist in their current continuation-based form until this same session.
  - **This directly explains `log.txt`'s 0 bytes**: a detect that genuinely blocked indefinitely inside a raw `USB_ReadBlkMsg` call (a real, structural, previously-documented limitation of this hardware/IOS combination — there is no interruptible or timed variant of that call in libogc, so neither the retry loop nor the X+Y abort check can rescue a hang *inside* a single blocked read, only between attempts) would produce exactly this symptom: total silence on disk, requiring a hard power cycle, with the log offering zero forensic evidence of where it got stuck.
  - **Fix**: added `log_commit_sd()` immediately before each of the 4 `run_detect_cart_inner()` calls (`source/main.c`). If a future session hangs the same way, the log will now show exactly how far the detect got (which retry/cycle number) instead of nothing at all.
- **Not yet hardware-verified** (the `log_commit_sd()` fix specifically — the retry-budget-honored finding and the save read/write PKHeX validation are already confirmed from this test's own logs). If a genuinely stuck detect recurs, no software fix can rescue it mid-hang (the underlying USB call itself has no timeout), but the log should now at least show precisely where it happened.

Directly requested: re-look at the cart-swap capture (already in `logs/Firmware-V10_0_10-WireShark/`, first analyzed 2026-07-28) specifically to pin down *where* cart info transfers during a swap, to inform whether Detect Cart is missing something structural. Re-extracted via `tshark` (`usbcom.data.out_payload`/`in_payload`, same method as every prior Wireshark analysis in this project) and walked the full timeline frame-by-frame across several swap events in the capture, not just the single transition spot-checked previously.

- **The transfer mechanism itself never changes.** Every single cart-info read — steady-state or mid-swap — is the identical 3-packet exchange already documented: one 64-byte OUT (`cmd=0x04`, same bytes and same CRC every time, confirmed byte-identical across dozens of polls), then three 512-byte INs (`C0 DE 00 04 FB...` header, the real 60-byte response zero-padded to 512, `C0 DE 01 04 FB...` footer). There is no separate "cart changed" command, no special identification handshake, and no different framing during a swap — Epilogue is doing exactly the same poll it always does, just landing on a moment of good physical contact.
- **Epilogue's poll cadence is adaptive and confirmed directly, both rates in the same capture**: ~497-524ms between polls whenever the last read succeeded (cart present, known-good), collapsing to a **tight ~104-114ms** cadence the instant a poll reads `resp[3:4] == 00 00` (clean "no cart"). This matches the adaptive-cadence finding from the original 2026-07-28 analysis, now confirmed with exact numbers from a full walk rather than a spot check. **This is worth noting directly against this project's own `GBOP_RETRY_PACING_US` = 100000 (100ms, `main.c`) — already right in the same ballpark as Epilogue's own real-world search cadence**, which is a good sign the Wii-side pacing choice isn't the gap.
- **On physical removal, the device reports "no cart" cleanly and near-instantly** — `resp[3:4]` goes straight to `00 00` with a normal, well-formed 3-packet response (no garbage, no partial read) the very first poll after removal. There's no ambiguous or degraded state during removal itself.
- **The first successful read of the newly-inserted cart is already fully correct** — no degraded/partial data, no separate settling period visible at the protocol level. The very first poll that catches good contact returns the complete, correct response for the new cart (confirmed for both Emerald→Ruby and Ruby→Emerald transitions in this capture — different title bytes, `'PBPE0'` vs `'PAXV0'`, everything else byte-identical in shape).
- **A real, repeatable contact-bounce artifact recurs around every transition, confirmed at 6 separate swap events in this one capture**: a response with marker `30 00 00 00 00` — i.e. the real GBA type marker (normally at `resp[2]`) landing 2 bytes early, at `resp[0]`. This is the same shape already documented in the original 2026-07-28 analysis, now confirmed as a *recurring, general* transport-level artifact rather than a one-off: it appears **6 times** across this capture, always right around a physical contact change, and — notably — not always strictly *before* the first good read of the new state; in several cases it appears sandwiched *between* two already-good reads (e.g. frame 539 good → frame 547 bounce → frame 559 good again), meaning it reflects ongoing mechanical noise during the swap, not a one-time "settling" step that only happens once per transition.
- **One swap event in this capture (t≈18.9-19.7s) was messier than the rest** — three consecutive anomalous frames in a row (`0a 00 30 00 00`, `30 00 00 00 00`, `0a 00 30 00 00`) before landing on a clean read, versus the single-bounce shape everywhere else — consistent with a slightly less clean mechanical reseat on that particular attempt, not a different underlying mechanism.
- **Net conclusion, directly answering the "is Detect Cart missing something structural" question this was meant to inform: no.** Epilogue achieves its near-100% success rate during a swap using literally the same request-response cart-info exchange this project's own `gbop_read_cart_info()` already implements, at a search cadence this project's own `GBOP_RETRY_PACING_US` already approximately matches. There is no hidden second command, no discovery/handshake step, and no special first-contact framing being missed. This reinforces the standing conclusion from the earlier Wireshark timing analysis (under "Wireshark timing analysis" above): the gap is in per-attempt Wii/IOS-side round-trip reliability, not in protocol understanding — nothing in this re-analysis points at a concrete code change to make.

### Status of the stitching/reconcile approach overall (2026-07-30)

Per direct user assessment: not clearly sustainable as the primary approach, since real [64,512) coverage has never been observed in any capture across two dedicated hardware sessions (8 attempts) plus the historical test_25-34 tally. Keeping the feature in place — deliberately not removed — since other games/ROMs may show different, genuinely complementary gap shapes (the whole premise depends on gap position varying per-cart/per-content, which is plausible but unconfirmed for carts other than this project's primary Emerald test cart). The fast front-check above is a genuine, independent improvement regardless of whether stitching itself pans out — it should raise the rate of landing *any* clean full dump, which is valuable on its own.

### What makes the new protocol slower/less reliable than the old one — a structural answer, from comparing old-firmware logs and code directly (2026-07-29) — NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED

User asked directly: what's required to make detection faster or more reliable, and pointed at something concrete — pre-firmware-update handshakes needed multiple passes too, but they were fast and barely noticeable, while post-update ROM dumps stream faster once started but the surrounding handshake is far less reliable. Investigated by reading `logs/test_122` (a pre-firmware-update log, predates the `lprintf` timestamp feature so no absolute timing, but the message sequence itself is fully legible) directly against the current protocol.

- **Old firmware answered "what is this cart" with ONE cheap, non-streaming command.** `logs/test_122` line 46: a single cart-info (0x04) response directly yields `type=GB rom=2048KB ram=32KB` — size AND type in one simple call-response, no marker/footer scanning of any kind. The only reason a ROM-header read (cmd 0x00) ever ran on old firmware was to get the *real* title/game-code for filenames (cart-info's own title field is crude, e.g. `"P"` vs the header's `"POKEMON_GLD"`/`"AAUE"`) — a cosmetic nice-to-have, not a requirement for basic identification. On the new firmware, cart-info was already established (exhaustively, in the original protocol-rebuild investigation) to never carry a size field at all — so the ROM-header read is now **mandatory** just to learn size and identity, on every single detect that isn't a cartindex/cache hit. What used to be answerable by the cheapest command in the whole protocol now requires the most fragile one, every time. This is the core reason detection got slower — not a retry-loop inefficiency, a genuine capability regression in what cart-info alone can tell you (confirmed unfixable: no hidden size field has ever been found despite extensive searching, see "resp[26..29] is a fixed constant").
- **Old firmware's cmd 0x00 exchange had an explicit host-ACK handshake step that the new protocol lacks entirely.** Same log, lines ~70-77: `[hdr] cmd ACK: r60=60 r4=4` (device acknowledges) → `[hdr] host ACK: w=64` (**host writes its own 64-byte ACK back** — an explicit synchronization step) → `[hdr] ready resp: r1=60 r2=4` (device confirms) → **then** a bounded, fixed 512-byte/10-chunk read begins. That extra round trip gave the device natural processing/settle time between "command received" and "start sending data." The reverse-engineered new-firmware protocol (confirmed via the Wireshark captures already in this project) has none of that: `send command → read C0DE marker → immediately stream until footer`, no host-ACK step at all. Leaner and faster once streaming is genuinely underway (matches the user's own observation that dumps stream noticeably faster now, and the measured ~7.2x batched-read speedup from 2026-07-28), but with nothing built into the protocol to give the device breathing room before that stream starts — and the marker/header step is where essentially all of this project's documented failures happen.
- **Fix (experiment, not a proven win): `NEWPROTO_PRESTREAM_SETTLE_US` (20ms), `source/gb_operator.c`.** A deliberate `usleep()` inserted between sending the 0x00 command and the first marker read, in both `gbop_read_rom_header_new()` and `gbop_dump_rom_new()` — a direct, cheap, reversible test of whether reintroducing some of the old protocol's lost pacing improves the marker-read success rate. Deliberately scoped to just these two 0x00-command call sites for now (cart detect and ROM dump start, the two things asked about); save read (cmd 0x02) uses the same shared marker-reading machinery and could get the same treatment later if this proves out.
- **Pinned for later, not implemented yet (explicitly deferred by the user): maximize cartindex durability.** Since the header read is now unavoidable but only needs to succeed once per cart for the cartindex fast-path (previous entry) to take over, there's a case for persisting a successful header read's bytes more durably/immediately so a later crash or dump failure can't undo that one hard-won success — reducing how often the expensive operation needs to run at all. Revisit this after the pre-stream-delay experiment has a hardware result.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's header-read and dump-start success rates should be compared against test_31/32/33's baseline (0/60 header-read successes in test_32) to see whether the added pacing measurably helps, hurts, or makes no difference.

### test_31/test_32 (`logs/Post Firmware Update Test/`, 2026-07-29) — cartindex.ini never found at all this session (zero fast-path hits possible); user correctly challenged the "connection issue" framing; persistent-handle experiment implemented

- **test_31/test_32 both show zero `"[index] Lookup: N match(es)"` lines anywhere** — `cartindex_lookup()` unconditionally logs this line whenever it runs, so its total absence means `fopen(cartindex.ini, "r")` failed every single time (the file wasn't found at `g_app_root`), not that lookups came up empty. This means the cartindex fast-path fix from the previous session had zero opportunity to help in either test — every single detect (4 in test_31, 4 in test_32) was forced through the slow live ROM-header-read path with no possible shortcut. Confirmed test_32 also predates this specific finding being acted on: 0 of 60 total header-read attempts across the session succeeded (41 all-zero/ambiguous, 11 recovered-but-failed-checksum, rest other mismatches) — the existing checksum-gated safety logic behaved correctly throughout (never accepted bad data), the connection simply never handed it anything good across all 60 tries.
- **User directly and correctly challenged the "connection reliability" framing this whole investigation has used**: if Epilogue Playback gets clean, reliable reads from the *same physical device* on the *same firmware*, "the connection is flaky" doesn't hold up as an explanation — a bad cable/port/electrical-noise problem would affect any software talking to the device, not just this one. The user also pointed out that the *old* firmware needed multiple handshake passes too, but those were fast and unnoticeable — implying the *new* firmware's protocol is specifically less tolerant of something this codebase does, not that the hardware got worse. This reframing is correct and overdue: the real question is what's different between how Epilogue talks to the device and how this codebase does, not "how bad is the cable."
- **Concrete, testable difference identified: Epilogue holds ONE persistent USB handle for its entire session** (confirmed directly from the Wireshark captures already in this project — 94 separate cart-info polls in `insert_withdraw_GBA.pcapng`, zero `SET CONFIGURATION`/`GET DESCRIPTOR` re-enumeration events after the first). This codebase, by contrast, closes and reopens the USB handle before nearly every command — including, previously, between a *successful* cart-info read and the very next ROM-header-read attempt. That pattern is a holdover from an old-firmware-era workaround (the "IOS USB fd spend" bug, where the fd went stale after ~5 EP OUT writes) that may no longer apply on the new firmware, and could plausibly be interfering with the device's own internal state in a way Epilogue's approach never triggers.
  - **Honesty check on what's already been tested**: USB Probe Test 5 vs Test 6 (tests 6/7) already compared reopening-every-attempt vs. a persistent handle, and found no difference — but that test only ever repeated the *same* command (cart-info) back to back. It never tested the specific transition this codebase actually does: closing and reopening *between* a just-succeeded cart-info read and a *different* subsequent command (the ROM header read). That's a narrower, previously-untested variant of the persistent-handle idea.
- **Fix (experiment, not a guaranteed win): `read_rom_header_with_retry()` (`source/main.c`) now accepts an optional already-open handle to try first**, with no close+reopen cycle before it. `run_detect_cart_inner()` no longer closes `detect_op` immediately after a successful cart-info read — it stays open through the `same_cart` check, `try_enrich_info`/`try_enrich_from_index`, and the SD-cache-exists check (all of which are pure CPU/file-I/O, no reason to tear down the USB connection for them), and is passed straight into `read_rom_header_with_retry()` if a live header read turns out to be needed. The persistent attempt replaces (doesn't add to) the first of the existing `GBOP_HDR_READ_ATTEMPTS` (15) slots, so the total retry budget is unchanged — only whether attempt #1 gets a fresh handle or the one already sitting there from cart-info. Every branch that doesn't end up needing the handle (`same_cart`, SD cache hit) closes it explicitly; the one that does (cartindex miss) hands ownership to `read_rom_header_with_retry()`, which always closes whatever it's given.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's log should show `"[detect] ROM hdr read succeeded on persistent handle (no reopen)"` whenever this path wins — worth directly comparing how often that line appears (and how much faster overall detection is) against how often the fallback close+reopen loop is still needed. If persistent-handle attempts succeed at a meaningfully higher rate than reopened ones, that's strong evidence for the theory and a case to apply the same pattern elsewhere (e.g. cart-info → dump-start); if there's no difference, that's a second, more specific confirmation that reopening isn't the reliability gap and the search needs to look elsewhere.

### OSD (Z-menu) controls blocked after opening — real "Sync & Exit" bug found and fixed, but user confirmed that's NOT the reported issue; diagnostic timing added instead (2026-07-29) — NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED

User reported: "when i press z to open the menu it prevents me from moving the cursor to exit" — controls blocked for some time right after opening the in-game OSD (SELECT/Z-triggered pause menu in `mgba_frontend.c`), before ever selecting anything.

- **A real, separate bug was found and fixed along the way, but user confirmed it is NOT the one being hit:** the OSD's "Sync & Exit" flow (GB/C 3-item exit sub-menu, `exit_item == 0`) polls `cart_sync_state()` in a `do { ... } while (st == CART_SYNC_IN_PROGRESS && --timeout > 0)` loop with a ~10s timeout and **never called `PAD_ScanPads()` at all** — controls were genuinely, completely unresponsive for up to the full timeout with no way to interrupt it. Fixed: the loop now scans pads every iteration and lets the player hold B to bail out early (safe to do — `s_player_saved_ingame` is already set before the wait begins, so `mgba_run()`'s teardown failsafe still writes the save to SD regardless of whether this wait completes, and the cart_sync background thread keeps retrying independently of this screen). **This fix is real and worth keeping, but the user confirmed they never pressed Sync & Exit** — the block happens on Z press alone, before reaching this code path at all.
- **Re-inspected `osd_show()`'s setup sequence** (AUDIO_StopDMA, GX_DrawDone, console_init/VIDEO_Configure/VIDEO_SetNextFramebuffer/VIDEO_Flush, the Z-release wait loop) for anything that could stall for seconds — nothing stood out as an obvious multi-second blocker by inspection; all of these are normally sub-frame operations and none had ever actually been measured on hardware.
- **Fix (diagnostic only, no behavior change):** added `lprintf()` timestamps at OSD entry, after `GX_DrawDone()`, after the video reconfigure, after the Z-release wait (with frame count if it took more than 0 frames), after the first draw/main-loop entry, and on every single non-zero `PAD_ButtonsDown()` result inside the main navigation loop (with the current frame index, item, and in_exit_menu state). This will show, frame-by-frame, exactly where the delay is on the next hardware test — whether it's in the setup calls before the loop even starts, a long Z-release wait, or presses genuinely not registering once inside the loop.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next test: open the OSD, try to navigate immediately, and check the log for how many milliseconds/frames elapse between `"[osd] show: enter"` and the first `"[osd] show: frame N pressed=..."` line, and whether UP/DOWN presses appear promptly once the loop is running.

### test_30 (`logs/Post Firmware Update Test/test_30/`, 2026-07-29) — three user-reported problems traced to two real bugs; both fixed; the third ("auto-detect during emulation") ruled out by direct log evidence — NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED

User reported three things in one session: (1) cart detect takes a lot of tries — can it be faster; (2) a detected cart "goes away quickly," making it hard to run programs, which felt related to the earlier background-auto-detect freeze issue; (3) suspicion that auto-detects fire *during* emulation despite `auto_detect_cart=0`, which should pause them.

- **(3) ruled out directly: no poll/detect/USB activity occurs during the actual `mgba_run()` window.** Checked every log line between `"Emulation started"` (318217ms) and `"Exiting emulation"` (342260ms) — zero `[poll]`/`[detect]`/`[gbop]`/`[newproto]` lines anywhere in that ~24s window. Architecturally this can't happen anyway: `poll_cart()`/`run_detect_cart_inner()` only run from the synchronous frontend/dev-menu loop between screens, and `mgba_run()` takes over the main thread until the session ends — there's no interleaving path. What's actually happening (see (2) below) is `poll_cart()` firing constantly in the menu screens immediately before/after a session, which — combined with the bug below — created a strong impression of constant background interference even though nothing runs mid-session.
- **(2) root cause found: `poll_cart()`'s `auto_detect_cart=0` fast path (added in response to test_25) had NO debounce at all — a single failed read was enough to flip `*was_absent` to true, and `main.c`'s "Confirmed removal" branch (the `else if (!was_before && cart_was_absent)` block) wipes `info.rom_size_kb`/`ram_size_kb`/`title`/`display_title` the instant that flag transitions false→true, unconditionally, with no debounce of its own.** At this connection's measured ~5-20% per-attempt success rate, a single miss is the *ordinary* outcome even with the cart sitting untouched — confirmed directly in this log: `"Cart removed"` fired 3 separate times, each only 1.3–4.9 seconds after a successful detect had just populated real title/size info. This is a direct, unintended side effect of the test_25 fix — it correctly stopped the multi-second blocking freeze, but over-corrected by removing debounce entirely instead of just moving it somewhere non-blocking.
  - **Fix:** added `GBOP_POLL_ABSENT_STREAK` (12) — a *non-blocking* debounce spread across independent ~1s poll ticks (a static counter in `poll_cart()`, incremented on each failed tick, reset to 0 on any success), rather than either of the two previous extremes (5 blocking retries compressed into one call, or zero debounce at all). A genuine removal now takes up to ~12 extra seconds to reflect in the UI; a transient miss no longer wipes anything. 12 was picked as a reasonable default (at a pessimistic 15% per-attempt success rate, 12 consecutive independent misses with the cart present is a ~14% event per 12-tick window, versus effectively guaranteed with the old single-check version) — tune if real sessions show it's still too trigger-happy or too slow to reflect a real removal.
- **(1) root cause found: the cartindex fast-path (`try_enrich_from_index()`) could never fire on a genuinely fresh detect, only on back-to-back polls of the identical cart — forcing a live, unreliable USB ROM-header read every other time, which is the actual slow part.** Traced the dependency chain: `try_enrich_from_index()`'s per-entry validation compared a candidate file's real size against `info->rom_size_kb * 1024` — but on any fresh detect (not the `same_cart` reuse path in `run_detect_cart_inner()`, which already ran enrichment beforehand) `info->rom_size_kb` is legitimately still 0 at this point, since the new-firmware cart-info response never carries a usable size field (rom_sizes.h). Comparing against 0 meant *every* cartindex entry always looked like a "size mismatch" and got discarded, regardless of whether the fingerprint genuinely matched a cart already dumped and sitting on SD. This directly explains the slow detects in this log: e.g. lines 584→1236 took **~86 seconds** and lines 938→1236 (~65s within the same window) purely from the forced live header-read retry loop, for a cart that had already been identified and dumped in this exact session moments earlier.
  - **Fix:** `try_enrich_from_index()`'s validation now only requires the candidate file to exist and be non-empty (`sz > 0`), not a match against a value that isn't known yet — a real ROM file is never 0 bytes, so this is still a meaningful sanity check. After selecting the match, `rom_size_kb`/`ram_size_kb` (which `enrich_info_from_rom()` never sets — title/game_code only) are now filled in by reading the matched file's own header bytes through `enrich_info_from_buf()`, with a further fallback to the file's own real byte size (ground truth for something already dumped) if `rom_sizes.c` has no table entry for the game — preferred over the generic, deliberately-oversized unrecognized-game guess in that case.
