This lets `run_detect_cart_inner()`'s downstream `rom_cache_exists()` check (which requires `rom_size_kb > 0`) actually succeed for a cartindex hit, skipping the slow live header read entirely whenever the cart has been seen before in this app's history — not just "since the last poll."
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session: a cart detected once, then re-detected later (different poll cycle, possibly after other carts in between) should resolve via `"[index] Enriched from index: ..."` almost instantly instead of falling into `"cartindex miss — reading ROM header"` and burning 15+ retries; and a cart's info should survive several seconds of ordinary connection noise without getting wiped to "No cart" the way it was in this log.

### Same validation logic applied to cart detection: only report a detect as successful if it actually enriched to a real title/code (2026-07-29) — NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED

Requested directly, drawing the same principle as the ROM-dump early-footer fix: `run_detect_cart_inner()` (`source/main.c`) had a "false success" path structurally identical to the dump-corruption bug — it could return `1` (valid detect) even when it never actually learned anything real about the cart.

- **The bug:** when cartindex has no fingerprint match and the ROM isn't already cached on SD, the function falls into a "cartindex miss — reading ROM header for game code" branch that calls `read_rom_header_with_retry()`. If that call exhausts its full retry budget and fails (`hdr_rc != 0`), the old code just logged `"ROM hdr read failed (exhausted retries)"` and fell straight through to `return 1` anyway — with `game_code` still empty, `title` still empty, and `rom_size_kb` still at whatever cart-info itself provided (0, since the new-firmware protocol never populates size from cart-info alone — see rom_sizes.h). Every caller would then display an empty-title, zero-size "detected" cart as if it were real.
- **Fix:** that branch now `return 0;` instead of falling through — treated exactly like the existing `!cart_ok` ("no cart found") case, which every one of this function's 5 call sites already handles correctly (reset `info`/`rom_hdr`/`display_title` to a "No cart" state). A failed enrichment now means the caller retries on the next poll cycle or the next manual "Detect Cart" press, instead of confidently showing wrong/empty info as a successful detect.
- **Scope check — every other path that reaches `return 1` already has real, validated data behind it, so none needed the same gate:** the `same_cart` fast-path reuses a `rom_hdr` that's only non-zero because a *previous* successful header read populated it; both SD-cache-hit branches (`try_enrich_info`/`try_enrich_from_index` success, and the cartindex-miss branch's own post-header-read cache check) call `enrich_info_from_buf()` on bytes read from a file that already passed `rom_cache_exists()`'s exact size check; and the `hdr_rc == 0` success case itself is gated by `gbop_read_rom_header_new()`'s own `header_checksum_valid()` validation (test_12) before ever being accepted. The one broken path was specifically the retry-exhaustion dead end.
- **Deliberately did not add a separate "game_code must be non-empty" check on top of `hdr_rc`.** Plain (non-Color) GB carts legitimately never populate `game_code` (`enrich_info_from_buf()`'s GBC-code extraction is gated on the CGB flag) — title is their real identifying field instead. Gating success on `hdr_rc == 0` (backed by the header checksum) covers all three cart types correctly without misclassifying a legitimately-identified plain GB cart as a failure.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session: a cart that previously showed up as "detected" with an empty title/zero size after repeated "ROM hdr read failed" retries should now correctly read as "no cart" (or keep retrying) instead — worth checking that no ROM/save operation is ever attempted against a `CartInfo` with an unresolved title.

### test_28 (`logs/Post Firmware Update Test/test_28/`, 2026-07-29) — the "footer 64 bytes LATE" rejection was a false positive: a benign ZLP, not real misalignment; the preserved debug file proved the stream was byte-perfect

First hardware run with the rejected-dump preservation (previous entry) actually producing a file: a `"footer arrived 64 bytes LATE (1 extra packet(s))"` rejection left `_debug_last_rejected.bin` (16,773,120 bytes — exactly 4095 of the 4096 expected 4096-byte chunks, i.e. only the very last chunk's read attempt was ever rejected).

- **Diffed the preserved file directly against the reference ROM: byte-for-byte identical, zero shift anywhere.** This is a materially different result from every previous corruption case in this investigation (test_16/18/20/25's front-of-stream or mid-file shifts) — here, everything actually streamed was completely correct.
- **The new per-packet content logging (added the same session) showed why: the "extra packet" was `rd=0` — a zero-length read (ZLP), not real data — both times it occurred in this log.** `newproto_confirm_footer_immediate()` treated any non-footer read, including a completely empty one, as proof of misalignment and rejected. But a ZLP right around a marker/footer boundary is already a known-benign, well-precedented artifact elsewhere in this exact protocol (e.g. `"cart-info footer-drain[2] rd=0"`, which every session shows routinely and which is already tolerated without complaint in the cart-info marker drain). The confirm-footer check just never carried that same tolerance over.
- **Conclusion: this specific rejection shape was very likely a false positive** — a completely clean, correct dump thrown away because of one harmless empty read between the real data and the real footer, recurring identically across test_26 (3×), test_27 (1×), and test_28 (2×, both confirmed `rd=0`).
- **Fix:** `newproto_confirm_footer_immediate()` (`source/gb_operator.c`) now treats `rd == 0` as benign and continues the scan without counting it against the misalignment tally; only a genuine non-footer, non-zero-length read still counts as `real_extra` and triggers rejection. The bounded iteration cap (`NEWPROTO_FOOTER_CONFIRM_MAX` = 64) still applies regardless, so a connection that keeps sending nothing but ZLPs forever still can't hang this check.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.** Next session's "footer arrived N bytes LATE" (if any) should now only fire for a genuine non-ZLP extra packet — worth checking whether previously-rejected dumps of this exact shape (rd=0-then-footer) now complete successfully instead.

### test_29 (`logs/Post Firmware Update Test/test_29/`, 2026-07-29) — FIRST CONFIRMED BYTE-PERFECT DUMP: SHA1-verified against reference, and it's the direct product of the ZLP-tolerance fix

`POKEMON_EMER_BPEJ.gba` (16,777,216 bytes) hashes to `d7cf8f156ba9c455d164e1ea780a6bf1945465c2` — identical to the independently-verified reference ROM's SHA1. First fully clean, byte-perfect ROM dump produced anywhere in this entire investigation, and it booted successfully on hardware per the user's own confirmation.

- **The log shows this is the ZLP-tolerance fix (previous entry) working exactly as designed, not a coincidentally-clean stream:** the winning attempt hit the identical `"ZLP (benign) before footer, continuing"` → `"footer confirmed — clean"` → `"ROM dump complete"` sequence that test_28's analysis predicted would recover previously-discarded good dumps. This is direct, hardware-confirmed proof that the false-positive diagnosis was correct — this exact event shape (a stray ZLP immediately before the real footer) is benign, and a dump ending this way really is complete and correct, not misaligned.
- **Session shape otherwise unchanged from every prior one**: most dump-start attempts still failed at the initial handshake (header marker mismatch), one hit the already-understood genuine 512-byte early-footer corruption (correctly rejected, unrelated to this fix), then after exhausting the 15-attempt budget and falling back to the fresh-fd wait, the very next attempt streamed the full 16MB cleanly and landed on the now-tolerated ZLP-before-footer case at the very end.
- **Conclusion:** the underlying connection-quality bottleneck (most attempts still fail before streaming even begins) is unchanged and remains the dominant cost — but the correctness-checking layer built up over tests 20/25/26/27/28 is now confirmed, end-to-end, to accept a genuinely good dump and reject genuinely bad ones, without a false accept or a false reject in this session.

### test_26 (`logs/Post Firmware Update Test/test_26/`, 2026-07-28) — both fixes confirmed working on hardware; the early-footer bug reproduced identically and was caught both times; a new, smaller-scale misalignment shape surfaced

- **`poll_cart` freeze fix confirmed.** Only 3 "no cart" events across the whole ~380s session, each a single-check `"[poll] no cart (single check, auto_detect_cart=0)"` — none of test_25's ~4.5s/5-retry freeze cycles appear anywhere in this log.
- **The early-footer corruption fix is confirmed working, and reproduced the exact bug it targets.** 32 total dump-start attempts; 29 failed at the initial handshake (usual low first-packet success rate on this connection); the 3 that reached full streaming were each rejected. Two hit `"footer arrived EARLY: only 16776704 of 16777216 bytes received (512 bytes missing) on a CONFIRMED exact size — stream was misaligned, rejecting"` — the **identical** 512-byte shortfall, at the identical byte count, that test_25 silently saved as `"[OK] Saved"` — direct confirmation both that the bug is real/repeatable (not a one-off) and that the new check now catches it instead of writing a corrupt file. The final `POKEMON_EMER_BPEJ.gba` is correctly 0 bytes this session (nothing ever streamed clean start-to-finish) — strictly better than test_25's silently-wrong "success."
- **New wrinkle: a smaller, non-marker-block-sized misalignment.** Three rejections this session read `"footer arrived 64 bytes LATE (1 extra packet(s)) — stream was misaligned, rejecting dump"` (via the existing test_20 late-footer check, not new code). Every previously-characterized gap in this investigation (Emerald 512B, FireRed 512B×2, Ruby 56KB) was an exact multiple of one full 512-byte marker block; a 64-byte (single-packet) discrepancy is smaller than that unit — consistent with one spurious extra packet slipping into the stream somewhere that isn't C0DE-marker-shaped (so the existing marker-drop logic can't catch it as such), rather than a whole marker block going missing. Already caught correctly by the existing check (no corruption escaped), but worth noting misalignment isn't always marker-block-granularity — smaller single-packet drift happens too, in both directions (early/missing vs. late/extra).
- **Not yet resolved:** underlying initial-handshake connection reliability (29/32 attempts failing before streaming even begins) remains the dominant bottleneck — the fixes this session confirms are about correctness (never silently accepting a shifted file), not about raising the success rate itself.
- **One real inefficiency found and trimmed:** tracing exact timestamps around the first `"exhausted 15 attempts — waiting for fresh fd"` (line 624) showed `dump_rom_with_retry()`'s fallback loop (`source/main.c`) spent a full 24.5 seconds (75 tries × ~330ms, `68387ms`→`92911ms`) reopening on the exact same fd (`-434241503`, never changed) before giving up and using that same fd anyway (`"same_fd fallback"`). This loop exists to recover from a genuinely spent/stale fd (`tx=-7005`) — but every failure that triggered it this session was a header-marker mismatch (a connection-timing issue), not fd exhaustion, and this specific hardware/cIOS pairing is already documented elsewhere in this file as never cycling its fd at all. The full 75-try budget was pure dead time here. **Fix:** trimmed `dump_rom_with_retry()`'s own inline fresh-fd loop from 75 to 30 tries (~10s instead of ~24.5s worst case) — still comfortably above the ~42-try/2.5s fd-cycle timing documented for hardware where the fd genuinely does cycle, while cutting the wasted worst case by more than half. Scoped narrowly to this one function; the shared `gbop_reopen_wait_fresh()` helper (used by ROM header retry, play_game's save-read recovery, and other call sites) was deliberately left untouched — it's relied on far more broadly and wasn't the subject of this specific finding.
- **Not yet hardware-verified** (the fresh-fd-wait trim).

### Independent hash verification + FireRed gap size (2026-07-28) — the Playback file is proven byte-perfect; every gap observed anywhere is exactly one marker-block (512 bytes); the "missing bytes" most likely aren't transferred at all

User added a second, independently-sourced Emerald ROM (`Pokemon Emerald (J).gba`, different naming convention, different provenance than the No-Intro-style file already in the folder) and confirmed the existing file came from one of the already-analyzed capture sessions, backed by three years of using this exact hardware/software combination.

- **SHA1 hash comparison: byte-for-byte identical.** `Pocket Monsters - Emerald (Japan).gba` (the Playback-produced file) and `Pokemon Emerald (J).gba` (independent source) hash to the exact same SHA1 (`d7cf8f15...`), including the specific gap region checked directly (`0x2A5000:0x2A5200` matches byte-for-byte between the two). This retracts the hedge from the previous entry ("plausible the file has the same defect, just unnoticed") — it doesn't. The Playback-produced ROM is proven, independently, to be completely correct at exactly the address the wire capture shows as missing. Three years of reported reliable use is now also corroborated by direct evidence, not just taken on faith.
- **FireRed's two gaps are each exactly 512 bytes** (shift progression 0 → -512 → -1024, each individual jump exactly 512), identical in size to Emerald's single gap. **Every gap event found anywhere in this entire investigation — Emerald's one, FireRed's two, both instances in the 4x-repeat file — has been exactly 512 bytes.** That is not a coincidental size: 512 bytes is exactly one full C0DE marker/header block (8×64-byte packets), the same unit this whole protocol is already built around. This was not previously stated this precisely and is a strong structural clue: whatever causes this is tied to the marker-block framing itself, not an arbitrary amount of packet loss.
- **Given the file is now proven correct, and every mechanism by which the missing bytes could reach Playback via USB has been checked and ruled out** (capture-tool frame loss — ruled out, uniform timing and no isolated frame-number gap; a second endpoint — ruled out, 0x82 is unrelated ~1/sec status traffic; a hidden retry/NAK — ruled out, full USB field dump around the gap shows nothing unusual; reordering within the same session — ruled out, the "missing" chunk was searched for across the entire captured stream and found only once, exactly where the existing shift already predicts, never duplicated elsewhere) — **the most likely remaining explanation is that the missing bytes are never transferred over USB a second time at all.** Playback most plausibly patches this specific, exactly-marker-block-sized, address-fixed gap using its own bundled/internal per-game reference data, not a live device re-read. This is consistent with the `OperatorKit` architecture already confirmed via its public Swift interface (`CartridgeSignature.lookupKey: String`, size resolved by database lookup rather than raw device bytes) — an app already built around "identify the game, then consult a database" is a natural place to also carry "known problem regions for this specific game" as bundled correction data, silently applied, which would explain both why the defect is perfectly reproducible (a real, address-fixed device/firmware quirk) and why the shipped output is always correct (a known, silently-applied fix) without requiring any additional USB traffic this capture would have shown.
- **A concrete, decisive test for this theory, not yet run:** dump a ROM Epilogue's database would have no entry for (an unrecognized/homebrew ROM, or any game outside their supported list) via Playback and check whether *that* saved file has an uncorrected gap. If Playback has no bundled reference data for an unknown game, a database-patch mechanism would have nothing to substitute and the defect should show up in the final file; if Playback still produces a clean file even for an unrecognized ROM, the "bundled per-game patch" theory is wrong and something else (still unidentified) is responsible.

### Ruby 3x dump (2026-07-28) — the 0xFF visual pattern is legitimate ROM padding, not the defect; but a genuinely new data point turned up: a 56KB gap, breaking the "always exactly 512 bytes" pattern

User ran a fresh 3x-repeat Ruby dump (`rom_dump_GBA_ruby_3x.pcapng`) and, watching Epilogue Playback's own live byte-flow display, noticed two stretches that looked like solid `0xFF` — one spanning roughly 46-85% of the transfer (~1s), another at 96-100% (<1s) — and asked whether the known defect lives there.

**It doesn't — that's real, legitimate ROM content.** Checked the actual Ruby ROM file directly: bytes 46%-85% (`0x75C28F`-`0xD99999`) are 90.6% literal `0xFF`, and bytes 96%-100% (`0xF5C28F`-end) are **100.0%** `0xFF` — a solid unused-space padding tail. This is completely normal for a GBA cart: Ruby's actual game data doesn't fill the full 16MB chip capacity, so the remainder defaults to the flash chip's erased-state value (`0xFF`). Confirmed directly against all three dumps in this capture: none of them show any gap anywhere in either of these ranges — dumps 2 and 3 are perfectly clean end-to-end, and dump 1's own defect (below) is nowhere near either region.

**What actually turned up instead, in dump 1 of 3, is a new and important data point.** A gap of **56KB (57,344 bytes = exactly 112 × 512-byte marker blocks)** at real-ROM address ≈`0x561600` (~3.3% through the file) — no marker signature there either (checked directly, same as every prior instance), and the surrounding content is dense, high-entropy-looking data (not repetitive graphics/tile patterns like the Emerald/FireRed gaps). Dumps 2 and 3 in the same session are fully clean, zero shift anywhere.

**This revises the "every gap is exactly 512 bytes" characterization from the previous entries — it isn't the size that's fixed, it's the unit.** Confirmed gap sizes so far: Emerald 1×512B, FireRed 2×512B (two separate gaps), this Ruby attempt 1×57,344B (112×512B). Always an exact multiple of one marker block (512 bytes); the *multiple* varies enormously, from 1 to 112. The address, timing (no pause — not independently re-checked for this capture, but consistent with every prior instance), and lack of any marker signature all still hold.

**On the retry-vs-capture-artifact question:** this data point is honestly more supportive of *some* form of attempt-level variability (2 of 3 back-to-back attempts on the identical cart came out perfectly clean, one didn't) than it is decisive either way on retry timing or capture-tool loss — both points the user has pushed back on directly and neither of which this entry tries to settle. Recorded here as a data point, not a conclusion.

**On the Command Test Lab:** per direct user feedback, this tool's design (single isolated commands, later retry-wrapped) has not been a productive line of testing and is being set aside — further hardware sessions should default to direct, hypothesis-driven analysis like the above (comparing real captures/dumps against independently-sourced ground truth) rather than more Test Lab iteration, unless a specific need for it comes up again.

**New diagnostic tooling: `align_rom_dump.py`** (ad hoc scratch script, not committed to the repo — same status as the Python/tshark analysis behind the test_16-18 findings). Automates the manual Wireshark-reference byte-alignment process: extracts the ground-truth reference ROM from `rom_dump_GBA.pcapng` via `tshark`, then coarse-samples a dumped file, locates each sample inside the reference using a fast substring search gated on a **uniqueness check** (reject any match that isn't the sole occurrence in a nearby search band — a plain small fixed-window scan was tried first and produced dozens of spurious matches from short repeated sequences in real ROM code/data, which is why uniqueness matters here), and binary-searches to bound any transition down to a small window, checking directly for a marker signature at the boundary. Validated against this session's own dump: reproduced the same two shifts found by hand (leading +64, second transition to +576 in the same ~0x2A2000+ region, correctly flagged as ambiguous, correctly found no marker there) in well under a second. Only meaningful for whichever cart the reference capture contains (currently Pokemon Emerald/BPEJ) — running it against a different game reports no match, which is expected, not a bug.

## GB Operator USB Protocol

### Device Identifiers
- **VID:** `0x16D0`
- **PID:** `0x123D`
- Confirmed from Wii USB enumeration (web research had wrong values: 0x1D50:0x6018)

### Transport
- **USB bulk transfers** on EP OUT=`0x01`, EP IN=`0x81` (confirmed from hardware)
- IOS `USB_GetDescriptors` does not work — endpoints found by trial and error
- IOS `USB_GetDeviceList` ignores class filter — always returns all devices

### Packet Format
- **OUT (command):** 64 bytes — bytes 0-59 = payload, bytes 60-63 = CRC32-MPEG2 of bytes 0-59 (little-endian)
- **IN (command response — hardware-verified structure):**
  1. **ACK:** two separate USB short packets: `USB_ReadBlkMsg(60)` → 60 bytes (zeros, discard), then `USB_ReadBlkMsg(4)` → 4 bytes (zeros, discard)
  2. **Data chunk 0:** one 64-byte USB packet: `USB_ReadBlkMsg(64)` → 64 bytes (first 60 = actual response payload, last 4 = CRC)
  3. **Data chunk 1:** one 64-byte USB packet: `USB_ReadBlkMsg(64)` → 64 bytes (zeros/padding)
  - Cart info lives in the first 60 bytes of data chunk 0 (NOT in the ACK)
  - Total: device sends 4 USB packets (ACK as 2 short packets + 2 full 64-byte data packets)
  - **Critical Wii vs PC difference:** the ACK is sent as two separate USB packets (60 and 4 bytes), while data chunks are sent as single 64-byte USB packets. Reading a data chunk with `USB_ReadBlkMsg(60)` truncates the 64-byte packet and the subsequent `USB_ReadBlkMsg(4)` times out (-7008 = IOS USB timeout error) because no 4-byte packet follows. Data chunks must be read with a single `USB_ReadBlkMsg(64)` call.
  - **gbopyrator on PC accumulates 256 bytes (4×64) using PyUSB's `read(endpoint, 64)` which catches `USBTimeoutError` gracefully. On Wii, reading past the 4 real packets with a 60-byte read hangs indefinitely (no IOS timeout for 60-byte reads). Only read 2 data chunks.**
- **ROM dump command (0x00) response (hardware-verified 2026-06-13):** device sends 84 bytes across 4 USB packets as a "ready to stream" handshake, all zeros: 16-byte + 4-byte + 60-byte + 4-byte. Read sequence: `USB_ReadBlkMsg(60)` → rd=16 (short packet); `USB_ReadBlkMsg(4)` → -7008 (IOS 4-byte timeout fires before 4-byte packet arrives). Then drain the remaining 3 packets with 3 × `USB_ReadBlkMsg(64)` calls (returns rd=4, rd=60, rd=4). Only after all 84 bytes are drained does ROM data begin. **Do NOT attempt to read any response after subsequent in-stream host ACKs** — device sends none and continues streaming immediately.
- **Save read data stream format (confirmed test_43/test_45/test_46):**
  - **Command format differs by cart type** (confirmed Epilogue Playback USB captures; size at bytes 6-8 LE for both):
    - GBA: `cmd[0]=0x02, cmd[1]=0x03, cmd[4]=0x00, cmd[5]=0x01` (Flash — test_45 bit-perfect)
    - GBC: `cmd[0]=0x02, cmd[1]=0x00, cmd[4]=0x20, cmd[5]=0x00` (SRAM — test_46 GBC_SAVE_CART2WIN)
  - **GBA: drain after ACK.** r4 of command ACK returns -7008 (no real 4-byte trailer). Then drain 3 "ready to stream" packets — `rd=16 + rd=60 + rd=4` = 80 bytes (all zeros, discard). Data starts after drain.
  - **GBC: peek-2 drain detection.** r4 of command ACK returns 4 (real packet). After the 60+4 ACK, read two more packets: if (pkt_a=rd60)+(pkt_b=rd<60), discard both (64-byte protocol overhead sent after write); otherwise inject both as real data. Cold reads (no prior write) start data immediately with no overhead; after-write reads have 60+4 overhead (confirmed test_47).
  - After ACK (+ drain): pure `USB_ReadBlkMsg(64)` loop accumulating bytes until save_size received. Device streams (4×rd=60 + 1×rd=16) = 256 bytes/batch. No OUTs at any point during streaming.
  - **Critical errors in earlier implementations:** (1) same command bytes for GBA/GBC — wrong mode for GBC; (2) wrong GBA bytes put Flash in wrong mode → all zeros; (3) spurious 64-zero host ACK triggered Flash erase → all zeros; (4) 80-byte drain not discarded → 80-byte offset corruption (PKHeX "corrupt").
- **Save write data packet format (confirmed test_46 GBA_SAVE_WIN2CART + GBC_SAVE_WIN2CART):**
  - **Command format differs by cart type** (size at bytes 6-8 LE for both):
    - GBA: `cmd[0]=0x03, cmd[1]=0x03, cmd[4]=0x00, cmd[5]=0x01`
    - GBC: `cmd[0]=0x03, cmd[1]=0x00, cmd[4]=0x20, cmd[5]=0x00`
  - After command ACK (60+4 bytes): data upload begins IMMEDIATELY — NO initial host ACK.
  - Each data packet: 64 bytes of raw save data, no CRC.
  - After each data packet: device sends per-chunk ACK — `USB_ReadBlkMsg(60)` then `USB_ReadBlkMsg(4)`. Same format for GBA and GBC.
  - No final command sent after all chunks.
  - **Critical errors in earlier implementations:** (1) wrong command bytes (cmd[1]=0x00 for GBA instead of 0x03) — device may not program Flash correctly; (2) spurious initial host ACK wrote zeros to SRAM[0..63] and shifted all data one chunk; (3) per-chunk ACK read with 4 bytes instead of 60+4, leaving unread packets in IOS buffers.
- CRC32-MPEG2: Poly=0x04C11DB7, Init=0xFFFFFFFF, RefIn=false, RefOut=false, XorOut=0x00
- **Without correct CRC the device returns the ACK but no data follows**

### Command Codes (byte 0 of payload)
| Byte | Command | Notes |
|------|---------|-------|
| 0x00 | Read ROM | cmd[1]=0x00; bytes 2-5 = ROM size LE; bytes 6-8 = save size LE (Epilogue includes this; device ignores it for ROM streaming) |
| 0x01 | Write ROM | |
| 0x02 | Read Save | GBA: cmd[1]=0x03,cmd[5]=0x01; GBC: cmd[1]=0x00,cmd[4]=0x20,cmd[5]=0x00; size at bytes 6-8 LE |
| 0x03 | Write Save | GBA: cmd[1]=0x03,cmd[5]=0x01; GBC: cmd[1]=0x00,cmd[4]=0x20,cmd[5]=0x00; size at bytes 6-8 LE |
| 0x04 | Read Cart Info | no parameters needed |
| 0x05 | Enter DFU Mode | **never send as a probe** |

### Command 0x04 Response Format (60-byte payload)
| Offset | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| resp[2] | 0x20 = GB/GBC cart; other = GBA |
| resp[3:5] | 0x00 = no cart inserted |
| resp[5:8] | ROM size in bytes, 3-byte little-endian |
| resp[9:12] | RAM/save size in bytes, 3-byte little-endian |
| resp[13+] | Cart title (ASCII) |
| resp[14] | MBC type byte |
| resp[15] | ROM type byte |
| resp[16] | RAM type byte |

### libogc USB APIs
```c
USB_GetDeviceList()   // enumerate — class param ignored by IOS
USB_OpenDevice()      // open interface by device_id
USB_WriteBlkMsg()     // bulk OUT — 64-byte command packet
USB_ReadBlkMsg()      // bulk IN — call twice: 60 bytes then 4 bytes
USB_CloseDevice()     // release handle
```

## Sources

All external sources consulted during development are listed here. **This section is mandatory: any external source used to inform protocol decisions, API usage, or implementation details must be added before that information is used in code.** Citing the project is sufficient for collaborative/open-source works; individual files or sections only need to be called out when a specific file is the primary reference.

| Source | URL | Used for |
|--------|-----|----------|
| gbopyrator | https://github.com/N0ciple/gbopyrator | Primary GB Operator USB protocol reference. Command codes, packet format (64-byte OUT with CRC32-MPEG2, 5×(60+4) IN exchange), ROM read trigger format (`_craft_rom_read_trigger` in `coms_utils.py`), save read/write trigger format, in-stream ACK cycle (every 320 chunks). **Targets old firmware only** — last commit 2026-07-27 confirmed as 2023-08-25 ("add url to setup.py"), preceded by a 2023-08-24 commit titled "update com_utils.py for firmware 9.0+". Never updated for firmware v10 (see issue below). |
| gbopyrator issue #5 ("A quick update on a dead end") | https://github.com/N0ciple/gbopyrator/issues/5 | Independent, third-party confirmation (opened 2026-03-18 by kantosCoder, unrelated to this project) that gbopyrator's protocol assumptions break outright on GB Operator firmware v10 ("the new inner commands are new and the old ones doesn't seem to work") — corroborates this project's own from-scratch protocol rebuild after the developer's firmware updated past what gbopyrator/the original Epilogue capture ever documented. Comment thread links to a firmware-v10-specific successor project (see next entry). |
| kantosCoder/FreeGbOperatorFirm10Py | https://github.com/kantosCoder/FreeGbOperatorFirm10Py | Independent (not this project's) Python reimplementation targeting GB Operator firmware v10 specifically ("won't work with anything below 10"), GB/GBC only (Spanish Gen 1/2 Pokémon focus), accessed via a Windows COM port (pyserial) rather than raw USB bulk transfers. Cross-checked 2026-07-27: their cart-info command (`0x04`) is byte-for-byte identical to ours, independently confirmed via CRC32-MPEG2 (computed locally, matched their stored trailer exactly). Revealed a command this project didn't know about — `0x15` ("Get Info") — whose response also follows a 512-byte-header-then-data shape (their code reads the real identifying checksum from bytes 512-1024 of a 1024-byte read), independently reinforcing this project's C0DE-marker-then-data model via a completely different command. Their save-read command mostly matches this project's (`cmd[0]=0x02`, `cmd[1]=0x00` for GB/GBC, size at bytes 6-8) but shows `cmd[4]=0x10` where this project uses `0x20` — inconsistent even with their own write script (which uses `0x20`, matching this project) — and their write protocol (per-512-byte-chunk headers with explicit offsets) conflicts with the continuous single-header push this project captured directly from real Epilogue Playback traffic. Their own README/code explicitly flags the write path as experimental and currently broken for Gen 2 saves, so where the two disagree this project's Wireshark-grounded understanding (from the actual manufacturer software) is treated as authoritative — the `cmd[4]` discrepancy is logged as an open question, not yet acted on without a hardware test. No GBA support in their project at all, so nothing here bears directly on the active ROM-dump corruption investigation. |
| Epilogue Playback | (proprietary — Epilogue website) | Manufacturer's Windows companion app for GB Operator. USB traffic captured with Wireshark to confirm exact command bytes for GBA/GBC save read/write (cmd[1], cmd[4], cmd[5] fields), absence of initial host ACK in write protocol, per-chunk ACK format (r60+r4), and streaming packet structure on the **original** GB Operator firmware. A second capture set (`logs/Firmware-V10_0_10-WireShark/`, 2026-07-27, Playback v1.10.0/52f3150b, GB Operator firmware v10.0.10, System v2.0.0) is the ground truth for the redesigned protocol on that firmware — see "New firmware protocol" under Hardware test findings. Where the two disagree, the firmware version in use determines which applies. |
| Wireshark | https://www.wireshark.org | USB traffic capture tool. Used to sniff Epilogue Playback ↔ GB Operator traffic on Windows. Primary method for reverse-engineering undocumented protocol fields (save command bytes, ACK packet sizes, streaming batch format). Captures referenced as test_43/45/46 in hardware findings. |
| PKHeX | https://github.com/kwsch/PKHeX | Pokémon save editor. Used as save file validator throughout GBA Flash save work — "cannot read", "all zeros", and "corrupt" errors pinpointed protocol bugs; successful parse with correct game state confirmed bit-perfect saves. |
| gba-link-cable-dumper | https://github.com/FIX94/gba-link-cable-dumper | Wii/GC homebrew GBA ROM/save dumper via link cable. **Different transport (SI serial, not USB) — protocol does not apply.** Consulted for: GBA ROM header layout (title at 0xA0, 12 bytes; game code at 0xAC, 4 bytes; maker at 0xB0, 2 bytes); future reference for GBA save type detection. |
| No-Intro | https://www.no-intro.org | Canonical ROM preservation database. Used as the primary source for GBA game codes (4-char at ROM header 0xAC) and GB/GBC title strings (at ROM header 0x134) in `source/gametitles.c`. Game codes and titles are factual data; No-Intro is credited as the authoritative reference for completeness and verification. Also the source for `source/rom_sizes.c`'s ROM/RAM size table (added 2026-07-27) — see that file's header comment for which entries are additionally hardware-confirmed via this project's own testing versus No-Intro-sourced only. |
| libretro-database | https://github.com/libretro/libretro-database | Community ROM database derived from No-Intro data. Cross-referenced for GBC CGB codes (4-char field at ROM header 0x13F) and GB DMG header title strings used in `source/gametitles.c`. |
| gb-operator-reverse-engineering | https://github.com/jaames/gb-operator-reverse-engineering | Early reverse engineering notes. **Targets old firmware (VID `0x1D50` PID `0x6018`) — not the hardware we are using.** Consulted for context only; do not use its VID/PID or endpoint assumptions. |
| GBATEK "Nintendo Logo & Header Checksum" | https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-gba-cartridge-header.htm | Documents the GBA cartridge header layout: fixed 156-byte Nintendo logo at 0x04-0x9F (checked by the real GBA BIOS before it will boot the cartridge), header checksum formula at 0xBD. Already the basis for `header_checksum_valid()`'s GBA half (`gb_operator.c`). The exact 156 logo bytes now hardcoded in `source/rom_reconcile.c` (`GBA_LOGO[]`, added 2026-07-29 for partial-front-capture anchor validation — see ROM Dump Reconciliation) were not transcribed from this page directly; they were extracted programmatically from four independently-sourced GBA ROM files already in this repo (see Epilogue Playback capture files below) and cross-verified byte-identical across all four, with a CRC32 of the extracted sequence (`0xD0BEB55E`) matching vendor/mgba's own `LOGO_CRC32` constant (`src/gba/core.c`) exactly — this page is cited for the documented offset/algorithm the extraction targeted, not as the literal source of the hardcoded bytes. |
| Pan Docs "Header Checksum" | https://gbdev.io/pandocs/The_Cartridge_Header.html | Documents the GB/GBC cartridge header layout: fixed 48-byte Nintendo logo at 0x104-0x133 (checked by the real DMG/CGB boot ROM), header checksum formula at 0x14D. Already the basis for `header_checksum_valid()`'s GB/GBC half. The 48 logo bytes hardcoded in `source/rom_reconcile.c` (`GB_LOGO[]`, added 2026-07-29) were extracted from the Pokemon Silver reference file already in this repo (`logs/Firmware-V10_0_10-WireShark/`) and cross-checked by independently recomputing `header_checksum_valid()`'s own GB/GBC formula against that file's real, on-disk checksum byte (both match) — same reasoning as the GBA logo above: this page documents the offsets/algorithm the extraction was checked against, not the literal source of the hardcoded bytes. |
| DevKitPro | https://devkitpro.org | Wii/GameCube homebrew toolchain (devkitPPC compiler, CMake integration). |
| libogc | https://github.com/devkitPro/libogc | Wii runtime library. Provides USB bulk transfer APIs (`USB_GetDeviceList`, `USB_OpenDevice`, `USB_WriteBlkMsg`, `USB_ReadBlkMsg`, `USB_WriteCtrlMsg`, `USB_CloseDevice`), DMA cache coherency macros (`DCFlushRange`, `DCInvalidateRange`), video, and controller input. |
| libogc `libogc/timesupp.c` (source) | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/devkitPro/libogc/master/libogc/timesupp.c | Real source for `__SYS_GetRTC(u32 *gctime)` — an undocumented-in-public-headers but real, linkable libogc symbol (confirmed present in the compiled `libogc.a` via `powerpc-eabi-nm`/`objdump` before this source was consulted). Confirms the function reads the Wii/GameCube hardware RTC (seconds since 2000-01-01 00:00:00 UTC) and confirms the exact `946684800`-second offset this project uses to convert to a Unix timestamp in `gbop_wii_unix_time()` (`source/gb_operator.c`), since `timesupp.c` itself applies the same constant when converting between the two epochs. Consulted 2026-07-31 specifically to correct an earlier, wrong "no wall-clock API exists on this platform" conclusion — see "Save read/write vs. Wireshark" findings. |
| WiiBrew / GBAtemp community documentation (`__SYS_GetRTC`) | (community wiki/forum knowledge, no single canonical URL) | Cross-referenced alongside the `timesupp.c` source above to confirm `__SYS_GetRTC`'s signature and epoch behavior are established, previously-documented community knowledge (not a one-off reverse-engineering guess) before writing `gbop_wii_unix_time()`. |
| libfat | https://github.com/devkitPro/libfat | FAT filesystem driver for SD and USB mass storage. Used for all SD file I/O (`fatMountSimple`, stdio `fopen`/`fwrite`/`fclose`). |
| mGBA | https://github.com/mgba-emu/mgba | Game Boy / GBC / GBA emulator. Vendored at `vendor/mgba/` as the Phase 3 emulation core. API used: `mCoreFind`, `mCoreCreate`, `mCoreLoadFile`, `mCoreCallbacks.savedataUpdated`, `blip_buf` audio ring buffer, GBA/GB core headers. Link cable design: `include/mgba/gba/interface.h` (`struct GBASIODriver`, `GBASIOSetDriver`), `include/mgba/internal/gba/sio.h` (`struct GBASIODriverSet`, `SIO_JOYBUS` enum), `include/mgba/gb/interface.h` (`struct GBSIODriver`, `GBSIOSetDriver`), `src/gba/sio/dolphin.c` (JoyBus TCP driver — template for SI_Transfer adaptation), `src/gba/sio/lockstep.c` (multiplayer lockstep — template for Wi-Fi link), `src/gb/sio/lockstep.c` (GB lockstep). |
| mGBA SIO dolphin driver | https://github.com/mgba-emu/mgba/blob/master/src/gba/sio/dolphin.c | mGBA's JoyBus SIO driver for Dolphin emulator connectivity. Uses two TCP sockets (clock port 49420, data port 54970) and processes JoyBus commands 0xFF/0x00/0x14/0x15 via `GBASIOJOYSendCommand`. Direct template for the DOL-011 hardware JoyBus path: replace TCP with libogc `SI_Transfer`. |
| gba-link-cable-rom-sender | https://github.com/FIX94/gba-link-cable-rom-sender | Wii homebrew that uses SI_Transfer to send GBA ROM data via JoyBus over DOL-011. Reference for libogc SI_Transfer call patterns (command buffer layout, `SI_GetTypeAsync` for GBA detection, 50µs inter-command delay). |
| afska/gba-link-connection | https://github.com/afska/gba-link-connection | GBA-side link cable library. `LinkCube.hpp` documents the GBA JoyBus slave register interface: RCNT bits 14–15=1 to enter joybus mode, REG_JOYCNT (0x4000140), REG_JOYSTAT (0x4000158), JOY_RECV_L/H (0x4000150/52), JOY_TRANS_L/H (0x4000154/56). Confirms GBA is always slave in JoyBus. |
| GBATEK SIO JoyBus | https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-sio-joy-bus-mode.htm | Hardware reference for the GBA JoyBus register interface and protocol. JoyBus command byte encoding (0xFF reset, 0x00 poll, 0x14 read 4B, 0x15 write 4B), 32-bit transfer format, and RCNT/JOYCNT register semantics. |
| Pan Docs Serial Transfer | https://gbdev.io/pandocs/Serial_Data_Transfer_(Link_Cable).html | Reference for GB/GBC SIO protocol: 8-bit transfers, $FF01 (SB data) and $FF02 (SC control) registers, master/slave clock selection, transfer timing (512 cycles normal / 16 cycles GBC high-speed). Informs `GBSIODriver.writeSB`/`writeSC` hook design. |
| Celio-Client | https://github.com/Celio-Link/Celio-Client | Online Pokémon Gen 3 trading client (browser-side). Uses WebUSB/WebSerial to talk to Celio-Firmware dongle. Network protocol to Celio-Server not published. Consulted for architecture reference only; not directly adaptable to Wii. |
| epilogue-co/DeltaOperator | https://github.com/epilogue-co/DeltaOperator | Epilogue's own official integration layer between their `OperatorKit` framework and the Delta iOS emulator — the manufacturer's first-party client, not a third-party reimplementation. `OperatorKit` itself ships only as a closed `.xcframework` binary (no protocol source), but its Swift `.swiftinterface` file (`Frameworks/OperatorKit.xcframework/ios-arm64/OperatorKit.framework/Modules/OperatorKit.swiftmodule/arm64-apple-ios.swiftinterface`, auto-generated by the Swift compiler for library-evolution support) is plain text and exposes the full public API surface: `readGame(size:progress:) async throws -> Data`, `readSave(size:saveChip:romSize:progress:)`, `writeSave(_:saveChip:romSize:expectedLookupKey:progress:)`, `readSignature() async -> CartridgeSignature?`, and a `CartridgeSignature` struct (`platform`, `romSize`, `saveSize`, `lookupKey: String`, `deviceFingerprint`, `firmwareSaveChip`, `hasCartridge`, `isFullyDetected`). Consulted 2026-07-28 for architecture validation, not byte-level protocol details (those remain inside the closed binary). Key confirmations: (1) `OperatorDeviceState`/`CartridgeSignature.Platform` include an explicit `.unstable` case and `OperatorError` includes `.unstableConnection`/`.readyTimeout`/`.commandFailed` as first-class, expected error states — confirms the connection instability this project has extensively documented is a real, known property of this hardware that Epilogue's own official client has to design around, not something specific to this project's implementation; (2) `OperatorKitController` exposes `cartridgeReadRetryNotification` and `reattemptSaveRead()`, and the integration's `OperatorLaunchCoordinator.swift` shows the shipped consumer app presenting a "Couldn't Read Save Data — Try Again / Play Without Save" alert to the end user on a failed save read — Epilogue's own production software sometimes fails to read a cart and has to ask the user, not just a theoretical edge case; (3) `readGame`/`readSave` take an explicit `size`/`romSize` parameter rather than deriving it from the device response mid-read, and `CartridgeSignature.romSize`/`saveSize` are populated alongside a `lookupKey: String` — consistent with this project's own conclusion (see "resp[26..29] is a fixed constant" finding) that ROM/save size is resolved via an internal game database keyed by an identifying lookup value, not read directly from raw device response bytes, validating `rom_sizes.c`'s approach as the same general strategy Epilogue itself uses. No wire-level command bytes, marker formats, or firmware version details were recoverable from this source. |
| Celio-Firmware | https://github.com/Celio-Link/Celio-Firmware | RP2040 firmware for the Celio-Link custom dongle. Connects to a physical GBA via AGB-005 link cable (SO/SI/SC pins — GBA multiplayer SIO, NOT JoyBus). Targets Gen 3 GBA Pokémon only. Reference for understanding GBA multiplayer SIO signal requirements. Not compatible with DOL-011 (which uses only SD/JoyBus). |
| pret/pokefirered | https://github.com/pret/pokefirered | FRLG decompilation. Primary reference for trade legality rules (`src/trade.c` `CanTradeSelectedMon()`), flag constants (`include/constants/flags.h`: `FLAG_SYS_NATIONAL_DEX`=0x840, `FLAG_SYS_CAN_LINK_WITH_RS`=0x844), and FRLG save layout (party at Section 1 offset 0x0038, 100 bytes/slot, 6 slots). Confirms National Dex and Celio quest are independent flags. |
| pret/pokeemerald | https://github.com/pret/pokeemerald | RSE decompilation. Cross-reference for `CanTradeSelectedMon()` (same trade rules as FRLG), RSE party offset (Section 1 offset 0x0238), and save section layout. |
| Bulbapedia Pokémon data structure Gen III | https://bulbapedia.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_data_structure_(Generation_III) | Authoritative reference for the 100-byte GBA party Pokémon structure: 32-byte outer header (PV, OT ID, nickname 10B, OT name 7B, marks, language, bad egg/obedience, checksum), 48-byte XOR-encrypted block (key = full OT ID u32). Four 12-byte substructures in permutation order `PV % 24`. Growth substructure: species, item, XP, friendship. Attacks: moves + PP. EVs/Condition: EVs, contest. Misc: Origins Info (game-of-origin bits 7–10; 15=Colosseum/XD), IVs/Egg/Ability (bit 30 = Egg flag, bit 31 = MODERN_FATEFUL_ENCOUNTER). Per-Pokémon checksum: sum of all 24 u16 words in decrypted 48-byte block, take low 16 bits. |
| Bulbapedia Save data structure Gen III | https://bulbapedia.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Save_data_structure_(Generation_III) | Gen 3 save file layout: 128KB Flash, two alternating 57,344-byte save slots, 14 sections × 4,096 bytes each. Section structure: 3,968-byte data area, section ID (u16), checksum (u16), validation (u32=0x08012025), save index (u32). Per-section checksum: sum all u32 words in data; add upper and lower u16 halves of 32-bit result; take low 16 bits. Party data in Section 1 (ID=1): FRLG offset 0x0038, RSE offset 0x0238. |
| Bulbapedia Pokémon Colosseum | https://bulbapedia.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Colosseum | Reference for Colosseum GBA trade rules: requires completion of Story Mode, trade via Pokémon Center in Phenac City basement, Shadow Pokémon must be fully purified before they can be traded to GBA, Eggs cannot be sent to or received from GCN games, English GCN ↔ English GBA language restriction. |
| Bulbapedia Pokémon XD | https://bulbapedia.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_XD:_Gale_of_Darkness | Reference for XD GBA trade rules (same as Colosseum). Purification and MODERN_FATEFUL_ENCOUNTER requirements for Shadow Pokémon. |
| pret/colosseum-mb | https://github.com/pret/colosseum-mb | Full C decompilation of the GBA-side multiboot ROM that Colosseum/XD sends to the GBA during a trade. This is the primary reference for the JoyBus trade protocol GBA side. Key file: `payload/src/unk_200C5DC.c` — implements `JoyBusVCOuntIntr`, all LINK_CMD_* handlers (`LINK_CMD_RESET`, `LINK_CMD_READ_INPUT`, `LINK_CMD_TRAN_PLAYER_DATA1/2`, `LINK_CMD_RECV_TEXT`, `LINK_CMD_RECV_MON_DATA`, `LINK_CMD_RECV_PARTY_MON`, `LINK_CMD_RECV_GIFT_DATA`, `LINK_CMD_TRAN_GIFT_DATA`, `LINK_CMD_SOFT_RESET`). GBA JoyBus slave setup: `REG_RCNT = 0xC000` (bits 14–15 = JoyBus mode), `REG_JOYCNT |= JOYCNT_IRQ_ENABLE`. Game type detection: reads ROM header at 0x80000AC to distinguish FRLG vs RSE, selects party save offset accordingly. colosseum-mb reads the physical cart save directly via `agb_flash.c`/`libpmagb`, not via the retail game. **The retail GBA game is NOT running during a Colosseum/XD trade.** |
| Wack0/gba-gen3multiboot | https://github.com/Wack0/gba-gen3multiboot | Wii/GC homebrew that sends the Gen3 multiboot ROM to a GBA via SI_Transfer. Implements the full multiboot key exchange algorithm. Usable as a template for sending colosseum-mb from the Wii to a physical GBA over DOL-011. Note: key derivation occasionally fails; may need retry loop. |
| hatkirby/gen3uploader | https://github.com/hatkirby/gen3uploader | Wii homebrew using Gen3 multiboot (same SI_Transfer approach as Wack0). More reliable implementation; "works within a few tries." Second reference for multiboot send patterns. |
| Dolphin SI_DeviceGBA | https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/Core/HW/SI/SI_DeviceGBA.cpp | Dolphin's emulated GCN-side JoyBus device implementation. Uses TCP sockets (clock port 49420, data port 54970) to forward JoyBus commands to mGBA. Running Dolphin + Colosseum ISO + mGBA and capturing port 54970 with Wireshark would reveal the exact GCN-side LINK_CMD byte values and command sequence — the primary remaining unknown for the middleman implementation. |
| Deokishisu GC link requirements | https://gist.github.com/Deokishisu/c9abef7d41dbd898194197c2509c62fd | Documents which in-game flags the GBA game must have set for GCN link features to work. XD specifically requires `FLAG_SYS_GAME_CLEAR` from the GBA side in addition to the Colosseum requirements. |
| AxioDL/jbus | https://github.com/AxioDL/jbus | JoyBus TCP server library (same protocol as Dolphin, port 54970). Alternative reference for the Dolphin ↔ GBA TCP data format. |

---

## Architecture

```
main.c
  └─ gb_operator.c          USB bulk driver (complete)
  └─ rom_cache.c            SD/USB ROM file cache (complete)
  └─ mgba_frontend.c        mGBA Wii frontend — video, audio, input, save hook (Phase 3)
  └─ cart_sync.c            Background LWP thread for save-to-cart upload (Phase 3)
  └─ dol_reload.c           In-place heap reset for successive sessions (Phase 3)
  └─ vendor/mgba/           mGBA source tree vendored in-tree (Phase 3)
```

### File Roles
- **`source/main.c`** — entry point; inits video, Wiimote, filesystem; orchestrates cart ops and mGBA launch; all logging to `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/log.txt`
- **`source/gb_operator.h/.c`** — all USB communication with the GB Operator; owns device lifecycle
- **`source/rom_cache.h/.c`** — builds filenames from cart title/code, reads/writes ROM chunks to `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/roms/`
- **`source/mgba_frontend.h/.c`** — wraps mGBA core; owns emulation loop, GX video, AESND audio, GC controller input, save dirty callback, Reset button hook
- **`source/cart_sync.h/.c`** — background LWP thread that receives queued save buffers and calls `gbop_write_save()`; owns retry logic and notification state
- **`source/dol_reload.h/.c`** — in-place app restart after mGBA session; zeros BSS (resetting dlmalloc and all statics), then branches directly to `main()`. Preserves GX CP state and initial SP via `.data` statics across the BSS zero.
- **`vendor/mgba/`** — mGBA source vendored in-tree; modifications tracked in this repo; base version recorded in `vendor/mgba/VENDOR.md`

---

## Build System

**Toolchain:** DevKitPro (devkitPPC). Requires `DEVKITPRO` environment variable set.

```bash
# Configure
cmake -S . -B build -G "Unix Makefiles"

# Build
cmake --build build -j4

# Output: build/wii-gb-operator.dol
# Deploy: copy DOL to SD card at /apps/wii-gb-operator/boot.dol
```

VSCode tasks are configured for all three steps (configure, build, copy to SD).

**Links against:** `ogc` (libogc — Wii runtime + USB APIs), `fat` (libfat — SD/USB filesystem), `mgba` (mGBA core — vendored static lib, Phase 3)

---

## Implementation Notes

- ROM cache filenames are sanitized from cart title + game code; `.gb` / `.gba` extension based on cart type
- Cache check compares file size to expected `rom_size_kb * 1024` — not a hash
- ROM is dumped in 4KB chunks (`DUMP_CHUNK_SIZE = 4096`) looped across full ROM size
- SD is always preferred over USB for both cache reads and writes; USB is fallback
- Cart header location: GB/GBC at `0x100–0x14F`, GBA at `0x00–0xBF`

---

## mGBA Integration (Phase 3)

### Intent
mGBA is compiled into the same binary — no separate DOL, no channel switch. The Wii reset button exits emulation and returns to the wii-gb-operator main menu. Every action (ROM load, save event, cart sync result) is logged to the same `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/log.txt`.

### User Flow
1. From the main menu (after reading cart info), the user may select **Launch mGBA**.
2. A ROM browser lists all files in `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/roms/` (`.gb`, `.gbc`, `.gba`).
3. After selecting a ROM, a save browser lists matching save files from `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/saves/`. The user may also choose "No save / new game."
4. mGBA boots with the selected ROM and save. Emulation begins immediately.
5. The player plays normally (see Save Sync Design below for how saves are handled per cart type).
6. Pressing SELECT opens the OSD overlay menu (see OSD Overlay below).
7. Pressing the Wii physical Reset button exits the emulation loop and returns to the main menu. If an unsaved-but-unsynced state exists for GB/C, a Y/N prompt warns the player before exit.

### Save Sync Design

Save handling differs by cart type because GBA SRAM is written atomically (one save event per save action) while GBC games continuously dirty SRAM via hardware RTC, making it impossible to tell a real save from a clock tick without comparing total SRAM drift since the last known-good sync.

**GBA (automatic):**
- `savedataUpdated` threshold = 1 (any change is treated as a save).
- Every save event queues a background cart write on the LWP thread. Emulation is never paused.
- Save indicator dot appears in-game (green dot, bottom-right) while syncing and after success.
- Player never needs to interact with the OSD to sync — it happens automatically.
- OSD "Sync to Cart" remains available as a manual override.
- 10s cooldown after each sync prevents hammering on games that update SRAM frequently.

**GB/GBC (manual, with automatic detection assist):**
- Two comparison baselines are maintained:
  - `s_sync_snapshot` — rolling, updated every 30 frames of `savedataUpdated`; used by `is_real_save` check (threshold = 64 bytes for CGB, higher for pure GB) to trigger **auto-sync** when a large single-event SRAM change is detected (e.g. a fresh save from a GB game that only writes on explicit save).
  - `s_stable_baseline` — initialized to on-disk save at session load; updated only on `on_cart_sync_success`. Accumulates total SRAM drift since last confirmed sync. If diff ≥ 512 bytes, sets `s_new_save_since_sync = 1`. This detects Pokémon Gold/Silver saves that arrive in small RTC-sized increments across many `savedataUpdated` firings.
- **No save indicator dot** is shown for GB/C (RTC background writes cause too many false positives).
- OSD "Sync to Cart" is the primary cart-write mechanism for GB/C. It is gated by `s_new_save_since_sync`: if 0, shows "Save in-game first, then sync" and does nothing. If 1, performs a cart write and clears the flag on success.
- After a successful sync, the post-sync guard re-arms: if the player continues playing and then tries to sync again without an intervening in-game save, the same "Save in-game first" message appears.

**SD is always authoritative for both cart types.** The sync thread writes the save to `sd:/apps/wii-gb-operator/saves/<name>.sav` after every successful cart write. The final save on teardown (`savedataClone` → fwrite) is a separate failsafe that runs if the sync thread's SD write was skipped (e.g. due to shutdown timing). The SD file is never contingent on cart sync success.

**Retry on failure.** If `gbop_write_save()` fails, the sync thread retries with a 3s delay between attempts. Retries continue until success or shutdown.

### OSD Overlay
Opened with SELECT. Items:
0. **Sync to Cart** — GB/C: gated by `s_new_save_since_sync`; blocked with hint if no save detected since last sync or if save+sync then continued play without saving. GBA: always allowed as manual override.
1. *(reserved for future ROM/save browser navigation)*
2. **Quit** — exits emulation. For GB/C, if `s_player_saved_ingame && s_new_save_since_sync`, shows Y/N warning: "You saved in-game but haven't synced to the cartridge yet. Your save is backed up on SD." A: exit anyway. B: return to OSD to sync first. If saved+synced (or GBA auto-synced), exits silently.

Session status line shown in OSD:
- "Not saved yet" — `!s_player_saved_ingame`
- "Saved (not synced)" — `s_new_save_since_sync == 1`
- "Saved + Synced" — `s_synced_this_session && !s_new_save_since_sync`

### Save Indicator (GBA only)
Small dot rendered on the game framebuffer, bottom-right corner. States driven by `cart_sync_state()`:
- *(idle)* — not shown
- **syncing** — yellow/orange dot
- **success** — green dot, shown for ~2 seconds (120 frames), then clears
- **failed** — red dot, shown persistently while retry is pending

### Threading Model
| Thread | Content | Priority |
|--------|---------|----------|
| Main thread | mGBA emulation loop, GX video render, overlay draw | Normal |
| AESND callback | Audio mixing, pulled by Wii DSP | High (system) |
| Cart sync LWP | `gbop_write_save()` + retry loop | Low (background) |

The cart sync thread owns the GB Operator handle exclusively during upload. The main thread must not call any `gbop_*` functions while the sync thread is active. A mutex guards the handle.

### mGBA Frontend (mgba_frontend.c)
- **Core init**: `mCoreFind()` → `mCoreCreate()` → load ROM → load save → `mCoreRun()`
- **Video**: GX, nearest-neighbor scale of GBA native 240×160 to TV output. Letterboxed on 4:3; pillarboxed on 16:9.
- **Audio**: AESND double-buffer at 48kHz. mGBA audio core configured to match.
- **Input**: GC controller (PAD). Mapping: A→A, B→B, Z→Select, Start→Start, D-pad→D-pad, L→L, R→R, X/Y unused initially.
- **Reset hook**: `SYS_SetResetCallback(on_reset)`. `on_reset` sets `g_exit_emulation = 1`. Emulation loop checks flag each frame.
- **Save hook**: `mCoreCallbacks.savedataUpdated` → copies save buffer to shared memory, signals cart sync thread via `LWP_SemPost`.

### mGBA Source (vendor/mgba/)
- Vendored in-tree (not a git submodule) so modifications are tracked in this repo.
- Base version and any local changes documented in `vendor/mgba/VENDOR.md`.
- CMake builds `vendor/mgba/` as a static library target (`mgba`) linked into the main executable.
- Do not add mGBA upstream changes without updating `VENDOR.md`.

### Phase 3 Implementation Order
1. ~~CMake: add `vendor/mgba/` as static lib target, verify build links~~ ✓
2. ~~`source/mgba_frontend.c`: core init, ROM load, minimal emulation loop~~ ✓
3. ~~GX video output: render GBA framebuffer to TV~~ ✓
4. ~~AESND audio: wire mGBA audio core to Wii DSP~~ ✓
5. ~~GC input: map controller to mGBA buttons~~ ✓
6. ~~Reset button: `SYS_SetResetCallback` → exit loop → return to main menu~~ ✓
7. ~~`source/cart_sync.c`: LWP thread, mutex, retry loop~~ ✓
8. ~~Save hook: `savedataUpdated` callback → signal sync thread~~ ✓
9. ~~OSD overlay (SELECT): sync control, session status, quit with unsaved-warn~~ ✓
10. ~~`source/main.c`: add "Launch mGBA" menu option, ROM browser, save browser~~ ✓
11. ~~Custom border system: load `border_<CODE>.bmp` from SD for CGB games~~ ✓
12. ~~GB/C manual-sync workflow: stable baseline, `s_new_save_since_sync` gate~~ ✓
13. ~~Successive-session heap corruption: skip `core->deinit()` in all paths; `while(1)` in `main()` handles successive sessions~~ ✓ (confirmed test_90/91)
14. ~~Main menu: Commit Log + Exit to Menu options; automatic log_commit_sd after mGBA session; log_force_flush fix at cart-info checkpoint~~ ✓
15. ~~GBA sync thread crash (intermittent) — not reproduced across extended stress testing (test_91/122); considered resolved for now~~ ✓
16. ~~GB SGB border slow load — inherent SGB protocol delay in mGBA core; low priority, deferred~~ ✓ (deferred, not a blocker)
17. ~~→ Phase 4 begins~~ ✓ (underway — see Phase 4 Plans above)
