### External tester ROM-dump failure — unrecognized game + oversized fallback size + silent SD-write failure (`Save Read Write Test/test_external_1`, first v1.1.0-era external report)

An external tester (GB Operator firmware "most recent," release build, `use_old_firmware=0`) reported ROM dumps failing. Traced directly from the log — genuinely different root cause from anything else in this project's history, not a repeat of the connection-reliability issues documented at length above.

- **Cart is Mario Golf: Advance Tour (GBA, code `BMGE`)** — not present in `rom_sizes.c`'s `kGBA[]` table at all (only Pokémon Gen 3, two Zelda titles, two Golden Sun titles, and Mother 3 are listed). `rom_sizes_lookup_rom_kb()` correctly returned 0 for it, so `enrich_info_from_buf()` fell to the generic "unrecognized GBA game" fallback: **32768 KB (32MB)**, with `rom_size_confirmed = 0`. This is 2x the largest GBA ROM this project has ever successfully dumped (16MB, Pokémon Gen 3) and is very unlikely to be Mario Golf's real capacity (not independently confirmed — flagged as a to-do, not guessed at, per this project's sourcing rules).
- **New, previously-untested evidence about how the device behaves when asked for the wrong size**: `rom_sizes.h`'s own doc comment explicitly flagged this as unknown ("it has not been tested what a real device does when asked for a size that doesn't match its actual capacity"). This log answers it, at least for this case: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()`'s cycle 1 free-running drain-to-footer (which ignores the requested total entirely, just reads until the device's own footer appears) found that footer at 33,553,984 bytes — within 448 bytes of the full 33,554,432-byte (32MB) *requested* size, not at some independent, much-smaller "real ROM size." Cycle 2's capped streaming loop (which does stop at the requested total) then completed cleanly with an immediate, unrejected footer confirmation. Both point the same way: **the device appears to honor/stream up to (at least) the requested size**, rather than always terminating at the cart's true physical capacity — meaning an inflated size guess for an unrecognized, actually-smaller game very plausibly produces a bloated file that is a real ROM for its first (unknown, correct) length followed by wrapped/mirrored/padding content for the rest, not a cleanly-truncated correct-size file. This is a real gap in the "generous unconfirmed fallback" strategy that `rom_sizes.h` should no longer treat as a safe, cost-free default for a completely unknown game — it's an open risk, not yet mitigated.
- **Separately, and directly explaining the actual failure the tester hit**: despite `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` logging `"ROM dump complete... SUCCESS after 2 cycle(s)"`, the dev-menu "Dump ROM" handler still reported `"[FAIL] ROM dump failed"` immediately after. Root cause: `dump_rom_new_protocol()`'s final step — `fopen()` + a single whole-buffer `fwrite(buf, 1, total, f)` writing the entire captured buffer to SD in one call — had **zero logging on failure**, on either branch. A 32MB single-shot `fwrite()` (again, 2x any previous GBA dump's write size, and a single call rather than the old chunked-write path `rom_cache_dump()` used) failing silently is the most likely explanation for the visible "success immediately followed by failure" contradiction; free SD space on the tester's card, a libfat/stdio limitation on a very large single `fwrite()`, or a media error are all live possibilities that weren't distinguishable from this log alone.
  - **Fix**: added `errno`/`strerror()`-based logging to both failure branches in `dump_rom_new_protocol()` (`source/main.c`) — `malloc()` failure, `fopen()` failure, and a short/failed `fwrite()` (logs bytes actually written vs. requested plus errno). Not yet hardware-verified; the next report from this tester (or a repro with an artificially huge `rom_size_kb` on the developer's own hardware) should show exactly which of the three failed and why.
- **Not yet actioned**: adding a real, sourced `rom_sizes.c` entry for `BMGE` (Mario Golf: Advance Tour) once its true ROM size is confirmed (No-Intro or a direct hardware read via a smaller, deliberately-conservative probe) — would eliminate the oversized-fallback risk for this specific game. Also not yet actioned: any broader mitigation for the general "unconfirmed fallback size may be too large and the device may honor it" risk this log surfaced — e.g. capping the fallback lower, or detecting a suspicious mirror/repeat pattern in the captured buffer before trusting it.

### Root cause found and fixed: missing `mkdir()` in the new dump path broke ROM dumps universally on any fresh install (2026-08-01)

A second log from the same external tester (`Save Read Write Test/test_external_1/log (1).txt`) arrived showing Pokémon Gold (GBC) hitting the **identical** failure shape as Mario Golf: `gbop_dump_rom_continuation()` logs `"ROM dump complete... SUCCESS after 2 cycle(s)"`, immediately followed by `"[FAIL] ROM dump failed"`. Gold already has a `CONFIRMED` table entry (2048 KB) — its requested buffer was an ordinary 2MB, nothing like Mario Golf's 32MB fallback guess. Two different cart types, wildly different buffer sizes, identical failure — this rules out buffer size (and by extension the size-guessing work below) as the cause of the actual crash and points at something universal in the write step.

- **Root cause**: `dump_rom_new_protocol()` (`main.c`, the default dump path whenever `use_old_firmware=0` — i.e. everyone, including this tester) calls `build_rom_path_sd()` then `fopen(rom_path, "wb")` with **no directory creation anywhere**. The old/legacy dump path, `rom_cache_dump()` (`rom_cache.c`), by contrast, unconditionally calls `mkdir(roms_dir, 0755)` before its own `fopen()` — but that function is only reached via `dump_rom_with_retry()`, which only runs when `use_old_firmware=1`. For the default new-firmware setting, the only `mkdir()` call in the entire dump path never runs at all.
- **Why this was invisible on the developer's own hardware**: their SD card has had a `roms/` directory since long before `dump_rom_new_protocol()` existed (created ages ago by old-firmware-era testing), so `fopen(path, "wb")` always found an existing directory. On a genuinely fresh install — exactly what an external tester downloading the release for the first time has — the directory has never been created, `fopen()` fails with `ENOENT`, and every single ROM dump fails identically regardless of game or size. A universal, first-run-breaking bug for the default code path, not a rare edge case.
- **Fix**: added the same `mkdir(roms_dir, 0755)` call (`main.c`, `dump_rom_new_protocol()`, right before `build_rom_path_sd()`) that `rom_cache_dump()` already has — cheap, harmless no-op on an already-existing directory, safe to call unconditionally on every dump. Checked and confirmed unaffected: `dump_save_to_sd()` and `play_game()`'s inline save write already have their own `mkdir(saves_dir, 0755)` calls, so save paths were never subject to this bug.
- **Not yet hardware-verified** — next report from this tester (or a local repro: delete/rename `roms/` and dump) should confirm ROM dumps via the default path now complete end-to-end instead of "SUCCESS" immediately followed by "[FAIL]".

### Ascertain real ROM/RAM size from the cart instead of a per-game table (2026-08-01)

Prompted directly: a hand-maintained size table can never cover every game — the program needs to determine real ROM/RAM size from the cart itself. Two independent, high-confidence mechanisms, one per cart family:

- **GB/GBC carts self-describe their size in the header.** Standard Game Boy header bytes at offset `0x148` (ROM size code) and `0x149` (RAM size code) directly encode real capacity (Pan Docs "The Cartridge Header"). Both bytes fall inside the header checksum range (`0x134-0x14C`) that `header_checksum_valid()` (`gb_operator.c`) already requires to pass before any header buffer is trusted — so these bytes are exact, not a guess, for *any* GB/GBC cart, not just ones in a curated table. **Fix**: added `gb_header_rom_kb()`/`gb_header_ram_kb()` (`rom_sizes.c`/`.h`), direct switch statements on the two header bytes per Pan Docs' published tables. `main.c`'s `enrich_info_from_buf()` now calls these for GB/GBC instead of the old `kGBC[]`/`kGBTitle[]` curated tables, which were removed entirely (they're strictly worse than reading the header directly, and `kGBTitle[]` in particular only ever covered the 4 Gen-1 Pokémon games). RAM size needed special care: unlike ROM size, a genuine "no save RAM" (code `0x00`) is a real, common, correct answer (e.g. **Super Mario Land has no save chip at all**) and must not be coerced to the old nonzero fallback (128KB) — `gb_header_ram_kb()` returns `UINT32_MAX` (not `0`) for a genuinely unrecognized byte, letting the caller distinguish "confirmed zero" from "unknown." This directly explains a plausible mechanism for the tester's separately-reported Super Mario Land crash: the old code, having no table entry for it, wrongly assumed 128KB of save RAM on a cart that physically has none — `main.c` already has `ram_size_kb == 0` guards that skip save read/write entirely, so a cart wrongly assumed to *have* save RAM when it has none is exactly the kind of mismatch that could plausibly hang/crash when a save read/write is attempted against nonexistent save hardware. Not confirmed by a log yet, but well-grounded.
- **GBA carts have no such header field** (GBATEK's GBA header layout has no ROM/RAM size byte), but the Mario Golf log provided new evidence generalizing the existing `gbop_verify_gba_rom_size()` mirror-check technique: requesting an inflated 32MB read against the real 8MB cart made the device stream (and footer-terminate) at essentially the full 32MB requested, not at 8MB — confirming the device does not enforce its own true capacity; content past the real end is a mirrored repeat of content from offset 0 (real GBA ROM chips wrap their address lines past physical capacity). **Fix**: added `gbop_detect_gba_mirror_size()` (`gb_operator.c`/`.h`) — a pure, no-device-I/O post-capture analysis checking standard candidate boundaries {1,2,4,8,16} MB in increasing order via full-range `memcmp()` (not a small sampled window — the whole buffer is already in RAM, so a full compare costs nothing meaningful and removes any false-positive risk). Wired into `dump_rom_new_protocol()`: after a successful capture, when `info->type == CART_TYPE_GBA && !info->rom_size_confirmed`, the buffer is checked for a mirror boundary; if found, the file is truncated to the real size and `info->rom_size_kb`/`rom_size_confirmed` are corrected before writing — no more oversized, mirror-padded dumps for unrecognized GBA games. On no match, behavior is unchanged (write the full requested buffer).
- **Known, explicit gaps**: GBA RAM/save size still has no header field or mirror-style detection and keeps using the `kGBA[]` table + 128KB fallback. The GBA mirror-check still costs a full worst-case 32MB transfer for an unrecognized game even if the real cart is much smaller (the candidate ceiling must stay at 32MB to correctly verify even a real 16MB cart) — an escalating/doubling request strategy would cut this but adds more USB handshakes on an already well-documented flaky connection; flagged as a future optimization, not attempted.
- **Not yet hardware-verified.**

---

## Do Not

**Note on the entries below referencing the GB Operator USB protocol (ACK formats, drain counts, ZLP handling, in-stream ACK cadence, etc.):** these were all established against GB Operator firmware v9.2.0 (2023) and earlier. Firmware v10.0.10 (2026-07) uses a materially different, simpler protocol — see "NEW FIRMWARE PROTOCOL REBUILT FROM WIRESHARK" and "Old-firmware support decision" under Hardware test findings. These rules remain correct and in force for the `use_old_firmware` code path; they do not describe the default (new-firmware) path. Do not assume they generalize to new discoveries without checking which protocol era they were written for.

- Refactor `rom_cache.c` — it is complete and correct. Its dump loop (`while (offset < total)`) is written for a protocol where the total size is known upfront and authoritative — true on old firmware (resp[26] shift-code) but not on new firmware, where no cart-info field has been found to reliably encode size (see "resp[26..29] is a fixed constant" finding) and the real stream-completion signal is a `C0 DE 01...` footer marker instead. The chosen accommodation is a best-effort size estimate (lookup table, see `source/rom_sizes.*` / progress-display notes) fed into this same total-based loop, with the underlying new-protocol read function tolerating the estimate being wrong rather than restructuring this loop to be footer-driven. Do not restructure this loop even under new-firmware understanding — layer the fix underneath it instead.
- Use interrupt transfer APIs (`USB_WriteIntrMsg`/`USB_ReadIntrMsg`) for GB Operator — it uses bulk transfers
- Call any `gbop_*` function from the main thread while the cart sync LWP thread is active — the GB Operator handle is not thread-safe; the sync thread owns it exclusively during upload
- Call `GX_Init` more than once per process lifetime — `g_gx_initialized` guards against this. The GX CP stays armed to `s_gxfifo_buf` between sessions; a second `GX_Init` on a live CP causes crashes. Do not remove this guard. Do not make `g_gx_initialized` static again — `dol_reload.c` must be able to preserve it across a BSS zero.
- Free or move `s_gxfifo_buf` — it is a `.data` static array at a fixed address. The GX CP stays armed to that address permanently; reallocating or freeing it causes machine check exceptions. It must remain a `.data` static for its lifetime.
- Store values that must survive a BSS zero in local variables or BSS statics — on this Wii, the initial SP falls inside the BSS range (both `.sbss` and `.bss` are zeroed by `dol_reload`), meaning the live call stack is inside the zeroed range. Use `.data` statics with non-zero initialisers instead (`__attribute__((section(".data")))`). All `.data` addresses must be below `__sbss_start` to be safe.
- Zero only `.bss` (from `__bss_start`) in `dol_reload` — `.sbss` (small BSS, below `__bss_start`) contains libogc's `s_firstThread` (LWP thread-list head) and `postRetraceCB` (VI retrace callback). Leaving `.sbss` unzeroed leaves these pointing into the now-zeroed `.bss` TCB pool → DSI in `LWP_CreateThread` or VI interrupt. Always zero from `__sbss_start` through `__bss_end`.
- Leave `MSR[EE]` (external interrupt enable) disabled after `dol_reload` branches to `main()` — `_CPU_ISR_Disable` clears it for the memset; it must be restored (`ori MSR, 0x8000; mtmsr; isync`) before `b main`. Without this, `VIDEO_WaitVSync()` in `init_video()` spins forever.
- Branch to the CRT entry point (`_start`) during `dol_reload` — the CRT calls `SYSTEM_Init()` which reinitialises IOS on already-live hardware → DSI crash. Branch directly to `main()` instead.
- Call `core->deinit(core)` anywhere in `mgba_frontend.c` — it corrupts dlmalloc's free list when freeing mGBA's internal allocations (WRAM 288KB + VRAM 96KB + audio ~130KB). The next `malloc()` call (including inside `fopen`) follows a poisoned free-list pointer to 0x10000000 (physical MEM2 base, not mapped) → DSI. Confirmed across tests 84-89. Null `s_core` and leave mGBA internals allocated; ~640KB leaks per session but the heap stays intact for the next while(1) iteration in `main()`.
- Call `log_commit_sd()` at "Session ended" in `mgba_frontend.c` — use `log_force_flush()` there instead (no malloc involved). `fopen()` inside `log_commit_sd()` calls `malloc()` which crashes on the corrupted heap if it runs before the skip-deinit fix is in place. Even with the fix, prefer `log_force_flush()` in teardown paths.
- Add a final `USB_ReadBlkMsg(4)` probe at the end of `gbop_write_save` for GB/C — this read blocks longer than the 4.5s `cart_sync_shutdown` timeout, causing thread abandonment and a use-after-free race in the next session. All protocol data is already transmitted after the last per-chunk ACK. The GBA path does not have this problem (GBA probe is quick).
- Read a save immediately after writing without a close/reopen cycle and appropriate delay — the device is not ready for a new command until the USB handle is cycled; attempting a read on the same open handle after a write causes an indefinite `USB_ReadBlkMsg` hang. GBC needs 200ms delay after reopen; GBA needs 30s for Flash erase+program.
- Call `log_commit_sd()` (fclose+fopen on g_log) from the emulation loop or anywhere while the cart_sync LWP thread is alive — fclose frees the FILE's internal stdio buffer while the sync thread may be inside vfprintf on the same FILE, causing a heap use-after-free that corrupts the malloc free list and causes future malloc calls to hang. Use `log_force_flush()` (fflush only) during emulation.
- **Use any external source (repository, documentation, specification, or web resource) to inform implementation without first adding it to the Sources table.** This applies to protocol details, API behavior, hardware specs, and reference implementations — not to general language/stdlib knowledge.
- Add a hardcoded per-game ROM/save size lookup table to work around the GBA ROM-size-doubling bug — `gbop_verify_gba_rom_size()` deliberately verifies size via the cartridge's own hardware mirroring behavior instead, so it works for any affected cart without a maintained data table. See "GBA ROM-size doubling" under Hardware test findings.
- Call `gbop_verify_gba_rom_size()` and then reuse the same handle for anything else — it always streams via the ROM-read command when its quirk-gate fires, leaving the device mid-stream. Always `gbop_close()` and get a fresh handle (`gbop_reopen_wait_fresh()`) afterward, exactly as `main.c`'s `verify_rom_size_before_dump()` does, regardless of whether it returns a correction.

---

## Pokémon Box Wii (PBW)

A separate homebrew project that grew out of wii-gb-operator. PBW provides Pokémon Box-style save management for Gen 1–3 games running on the Wii via the GB Operator. It is developed in its own GitHub repository (`pokemon-box-wii`) and must NOT be merged into or built from the wii-gb-operator repository. The wii-gb-operator repo is archived (hardware-verified, complete) and is never modified by PBW work.

**SD path:** `sd:/apps/pokemon-box-wii/`

### Main Menu

Three options on boot:
1. **Box** — open the box management UI (primary feature)
2. **Adventure** — boot a specific game from PBW storage (plays via mGBA integration; player has access to their full PBW box storage during the session)
3. **Options** — display scale, storage device preference, and other settings

### Box Storage Structure

- **14 PBW boxes**, each holding 30 Gen 3 Pokémon slots = 420 total
- Boxes are displayed as **7 pairs** (two visible at a time); L/R cycles through pair index 0–6
- Gen 1 and Gen 2 Pokémon are stored separately in Gen 2 format boxes (20-slot boxes); they live in their own storage pool pending PCCS migration to Gen 3
- The cart row at the bottom of the Box screen shows up to 30 Pokémon currently in the connected cartridge (read via GB Operator) using animated icon sprites

**Subsidiary saves (PBW box persistence):** PBW boxes are stored as blank Emerald saves (PKHeX-compatible), 14 boxes × 30 = 420 slots per file. File: `sd:/apps/pokemon-box-wii/saves/pbw_main.sav`. Any PKHeX-compatible Gen 3 tool can read or validate this file directly.

### Box Screen Layout (GX framebuffer — 640px wide)

- **32px safe-area buffer** on all sides (Wii overscan)
- **Controls bar at top:** L/R pair navigation with "Pair X/7" label; hint badges for A (context menu), X (grab mode), Y (quick move); **Save & Exit** button top-right (no Cancel button)
- **Two 6×5 PBW boxes** side by side below the controls bar (30 slots each, `aspect-ratio:1` slots)
- **Box header** for each: box name (click to rename inline) + "Box X/14" counter. No navigation arrows on the headers — L/R on the controls bar handles all pair cycling.
- **"Stored XXX/420"** total count shown below or adjacent to box pair
- **Cart icon row** at the bottom: animated icon sprites (2-frame canvas animation, 500ms toggle) from the connected cartridge or selected save file

### Slot Interaction Model

- **Single-click** to pick up a Pokémon (sets `selected`; slot highlights)
- **Single-click on destination** to place or swap (atomically swaps slot contents; marks `dirty = true`)
- **Right-click** opens a context menu with: **Move** (pick up), **Summary** (show summary panel). No Migrate option; no Sort Box option.
- **Summary panel:** shows full battle sprite + Level, Nature, OT, ID No.
- **Sprite transition:** Pokémon in the cart row use animated icon sprites. When picked up (selected), the cursor display transitions to the full-size battle sprite.

### Inline Box Rename

Clicking a box name converts it to an `<input>` field in place. Enter confirms, Escape cancels, blur confirms. Name is bound to `boxNames[pairIdx*2]` or `boxNames[pairIdx*2+1]`. Names persist to the subsidiary save file header or a separate metadata file.

### Dirty State and Save & Exit

- `dirty = true` on any slot swap or box rename
- **Save & Exit:** if `dirty`, shows an "unsaved changes" confirmation modal before exiting. No background auto-save; the player must confirm Save & Exit to commit changes.
- No Cancel button — the only exit path is Save & Exit (saves and exits) or the modal's "go back" option.

### Sprite Naming Conventions

All sprite files live in `sprites/` at the repo root. Names are **UPPERCASE** with `.png` extension.

| Folder | Content | Frame format |
|--------|---------|-------------|
| `sprites/gen3/` | Full battle sprites, Gen 3 | Single frame |
| `sprites/gen3-shiny/` | Shiny full battle sprites, Gen 3 | Single frame |
| `sprites/gen2/` | Full battle sprites, Gen 2 | Single frame |
| `sprites/gen2-shiny/` | Shiny full battle sprites, Gen 2 | Single frame |
| `sprites/gen3-Icons/` | Icon sprites, Gen 3 | 2 frames **horizontal** (frame 0 left, frame 1 right; split at `naturalWidth/2`) |
| `sprites/gen2-Icons/` | Icon sprites, Gen 2 | 2 frames **vertical** (frame 0 top, frame 1 bottom; split at `naturalHeight/2`) — **folder currently empty** |

**Gen 2 icon download:** `https://github.com/cRz-Shadows/Pokemon_Crystal_Legacy/tree/main/gfx/icons`

Icon animation renders via `<canvas>` (48×48 display). Every 500ms, `frame` toggles 0↔1 and `drawImage` redraws with the correct x-offset (gen3: `frame * naturalWidth/2`) or y-offset (gen2: `frame * naturalHeight/2`). `imageSmoothingEnabled = false` for pixel-accurate rendering.

### PCCS Legal Mode — Gen 2 → Gen 3 Conversion

This is a one-way migration only (no Gen 3 → Gen 2 conversion is planned). **Legal** is the preferred standard (not Faithful) — the goal is a Pokémon that is legal in Gen 3 without requiring exact reproduction of Gen 2 internal values.

PCCS Legal mode is not yet implemented in the upstream PCCS project; PBW will implement from the spec below.

**PID generation (LCRNG loop):**
1. Generate a candidate PID via LCRNG.
2. Accept if `PID % 25 == target_nature` (nature determination).
3. Accept if gender DV threshold satisfied: gender ratio byte from species data; female if `DV_ATK % 8 < ratio_threshold`, male otherwise — PID gender bit must match Gen 2 gender.
4. Accept if ability bit (PID bit 0) matches desired ability.
5. For Unown: accept if `((PID >> 28 & 3) << 6 | (PID >> 24 & 3) << 4 | (PID >> 20 & 3) << 2 | (PID >> 16 & 3)) >> 2` matches the letter encoded in Gen 2 DVs.
6. Loop until all constraints satisfied; no iteration cap (legal values always exist).

**Shininess (SID calculation):**
- Gen 2 shiny condition: `DV_ATK == 10 && DV_DEF == 10 && DV_SPD == 10 && DV_SPC == 10`. If shiny: choose TID (from Gen 2 save), generate PID, compute `SID = TID ^ (PID & 0xFFFF) ^ (PID >> 16)` such that `(TID ^ SID ^ (PID & 0xFFFF) ^ (PID >> 16)) < 8` (shiny XOR threshold). Assign that SID to the Gen 3 trainer profile.
- Non-shiny: assign `SID = 51691` (arbitrary value that will not accidentally create shininess with any common TID/PID combination) or `SID = 0`.

**EV conversion (proportional):**
- Gen 2 uses 0–65535 StatExp per stat. Compute `total = sum(all 6 StatExp values)`.
- If `total == 0`: all Gen 3 EVs = 0.
- If `total > 0`: `EV[i] = min(255, floor(StatExp[i] / total × 510))`. All-maxed Gen 2 (total = 393210) → 85 per stat (6 × 85 = 510).
- Gen 3 EV sum cap = 510 enforced automatically by the proportional formula.

**EXP:** Copied exactly from Gen 2 to Gen 3 (no conversion needed; both use the same EXP growth curves for the same species).

**Nature:** Determined by the LCRNG loop (PID % 25); not read from Gen 2 (Gen 2 has no nature concept).

**Held items:** Before migration, attempt to return the held item to the Gen 2 trainer bag. If bag is full, place in PC item storage. If both full, block the migration and inform the player — the item must be manually freed first. The item is never silently discarded.

**Korean nicknames:** If a Pokémon has a Korean-encoded nickname that cannot be represented in the target Gen 3 language encoding (e.g. English cartridge), prompt the player to enter a new nickname before proceeding. Default to the species name in the target language if the player skips.

### Box UI Mockup (Web Artifact)

A browser-based interactive mockup of the Box screen exists at:
**`https://claude.ai/code/artifact/3e619d84-1fbe-4811-ac71-34928dbfa1c0`**

Implements: 640px Wii-width canvas, 32px safe area, controls bar with L/R pair cycling, two 6×5 PBW box grids with full battle sprites, animated icon canvas row for cart Pokémon, right-click context menu (Move + Summary), summary panel, inline box rename, Save & Exit with unsaved-changes modal, dirty-state tracking. Source file: `pbw_box_ui.html` in the session scratchpad. Embedded sprite data (`sprites.js`) includes 10 full sprites + 10 icon sprites for: Bulbasaur, Pikachu, Charizard, Gengar, Eevee, Snorlax, Gyarados, Mewtwo, Arcanine, Alakazam.

### PBW — What Is Not Yet Decided

- Exact SD file layout for Gen 1/2 box storage (Gen 2 format; 20-slot boxes)
- Adventure mode integration with mGBA (how to wire PBW box access into the emulation session)
- Link cable trading from Adventure mode (deferred — see Phase 4 item 5 in wii-gb-operator)
- Box background art, cursor sprite, home screen background (all placeholder — user will supply art; generate named template BMPs when requested)
- Gen 2 icon sprite download and integration (Crystal Legacy repo, see above)
- Wii GX rendering pipeline for the Box screen (to be ported from wii-gb-operator's GX setup)
